# 37 Player: notes

The big picture as of 31 October 2026 (version 1.4.148). Commit messages hold the detail of each change. This file says how the whole thing works and why it is the way it is. Update it when a decision changes.

## What it is

A playlist player for live sessions at Kadampa centers: chanting, prayers, mandala offerings, teachings, with silences and stops between them. It's built for an operator who needs the right audio, in the right order, every time, often on an old computer or an iPad. It came out of IKRC's old and new Macs being unable to share a Music.app library, and Music.app becoming hard for non-technical people to use.

- One self-contained HTML file, `37-player.html`, running as a PWA. It's offline-capable, and the whole library (audio included) lives in the browser's IndexedDB.
- Users so far: kr37, two friends, and Samudrabadra (Fort Myers). Prospective: KMC Texas (three rooms wanting one shared library) and IKRC (Macs and an iPad). Phones are a "handy sometimes" platform, not the main target.

## Where it lives

- **Repo:** `github.com/kr37/37player` (public). Layout:
  - `37-player.html` (the app), `sw.js`, `help.json`, `help/` (topics and `generate_help.py`);
  - `sync.php` and `SYNC-SETUP.md` (shared libraries on the droplet); `token.php` and `GOOGLE-DRIVE-SETUP.md` (shared libraries on Google Drive);
  - `index.html` (the about page, not the app) and `homepage/` (its icon, logo and screenshots);
  - `scripts/` (`convert_incompatible_audio.py`, `scan_incompatible_audio.py`, `get_ffmpeg.sh`, `pull_droplet_backup.sh`);
  - `tests/` (browser tests; see tests/README.md);
  - `NOTES.md` (this file).
- **Site:** `37player.com/` (before 1.4.010: `player.37dakinis.net`, still served as a `ServerAlias` so older devices keep working; see "The move" below) is a git working copy of the repo on the droplet, plus `libraries/` (not in git: only the starter track, `STARTER_TRACK_FILE`, since server libraries were removed in 1.4.006). `sync.php`'s data is outside the web root at `/var/lib/37player/`, bind-mounted (fstab) from its own 62 GB DigitalOcean volume, `/mnt/37-player-libraries/var/lib/37player` (since 1 Nov 2026; the websites' `/var/www` is on the 37 GB `/mnt/volume_1`). kr37's own library (`rinzin`, 47 GB on 1 Nov 2026) is most of it; he plans to move his personal library to pCloud once 37 Player can keep a library there, so the droplet holds only Centers'. The fstab bind line should wait for its own volume (`x-systemd.requires-mounts-for=/mnt/37-player-libraries`; it said `/mnt/volume_1`, fix suggested 1 Nov).
- **Release routine:** the droplet's copy is also the live test bed (the laptop mounts it over SSH).
  - Changes made there: run `help/generate_help.py` (embeds the help topics into `37-player.html`, writes `help.json`) commit; push.
  - Changes made by a Claude session go to the standing **`dev`** branch. At the start of a session, bring `dev` up to date with `main` first (`git merge origin/main`). Push to `dev` as work goes; when a batch is ready, open (or update) one pull request from `dev` into `main` and say it's ready. kr37 merges it; `dev` carries on afterwards, it isn't deleted. **Before every push, check whether the open PR has been merged** (`gh api repos/kr37/37player/pulls/N -q .merged`). kr37 may merge without saying so, and a merged PR stays editable, so commits pushed afterwards silently miss `main` (1.4.023–024 were stranded after #8 this way). If it's merged: `git merge origin/main` into `dev`, push, and open a new PR for what's left. **Small changes go straight to `main`** when `dev` has nothing unmerged (kr37, Oct 2026); then bring `dev` level with `main`.
  - Testing `dev`: on the droplet, `git checkout dev && git pull --ff-only` (or a separate test site, below); back to `main` with `git checkout main && git pull --ff-only`.
  - Commit and push any edits made on the droplet before starting a session, so the session builds on them.
  - The test site, `test.37player.com` (alias `playertest.37dakinis.net`), is a second working copy on `dev`, with its own IndexedDB on every device (a different address). A test copy of the shared library for it isn't needed (kr37, 29 Oct 2026): he now keeps separate personal and professional libraries, so a test can use a library of its own.
  - **Commits** from Claude sessions are made as `kr37 <kr37@users.noreply.github.com>`, with the session's Co-Authored-By line (kr37, 29 Oct 2026; the name and GitHub's private address since 16 Nov 2026, earlier commits being under his own name and email).
  - `sync.php` changes take effect as soon as the droplet has them. `SYNC-SETUP.md` covers first-time setup and the Apache upload limit.
- **Backups of the droplet:** running (29 Oct 2026). `scripts/pull_droplet_backup.sh`, on a systemd timer on the T470, pulls dated, hard-linked daily copies of `/var/lib/37player/` (SYNC-SETUP.md section 6).

## Architecture in brief

- **IndexedDB `temple-sound-library`.** Never rename it, or every user loses their library. Stores: `state`, `audio`, `artwork`. Audio and artwork are keyed by track ID; IDs never change.
- **The storage layer** (1.4.151, the first step of v2). Nothing outside it touches IndexedDB: a library's storage is a *library store* with one small interface, `get`, `put`, `del` (one key or several, all or none), `keys` (optionally by prefix), `update` (read some records and write or delete in one step, as for the copies waiting in `incomingCopies`), `clear` / `pruneExcept` (keeping `STATE_KEPT_ON_CLEAR`: backup, converter, libraryInfo), `writeRootIfCurrent` (the library record, only if its revision is unchanged) and `close`. Today's one implementation is `IdbLibraryStore` (reconnecting when the browser closed the connection, `settleTx` resolving only once a write is really done). This window's library is `libStore`; another library's is `storageOpen(id)`; the device's are `storageLibraryIds()` and `storageRemove(id)`. The app's own rules stay above it, in `idbPut`, `idbDelete` and the rest (revisions, backups to redo, a library that didn't load). A second implementation (files on disk, then Tauri) only has to keep the same promises. **The files store** (1.4.152, step 2, `FolderLibraryStore`): the same promises kept with files. For now only in the browser's private file system (OPFS), chosen per device by localStorage `37player.storage` = `opfs` (nothing in the app offers it yet); a folder the person chooses, and then Tauri, come next. A library is a folder: `state/`, `audio/`, `artwork/`, one `<key>.json` per record, its files (blobs) beside it as `<key>.<tag>.<ext>` and in the .json as `{"$blob": name, type, size}`; keys keep a–z 0–9 _ -, anything else (capitals too: Windows and Mac folders ignore case) is %XXXX. **Every write gives a file a new name** and the replaced or deleted one waits a day in `orphans.json` (`FILES_GRACE_MS`) before it's removed: a browser can't read a file that changed after it was opened (IndexedDB kept the old copy alive as long as it was in use, so a track playing while its audio was replaced or deleted carried on), and this keeps that. Files first, the .json last, so an interruption leaves the record as it was; leftovers are found by the tidy run 20 s after opening (and every 6 hours while open). **Only a window's own library tidies** (`opts.tidies`, given to `libStore` alone; 2.5.056): another library's store is a quick look and leaves its folder alone, and that library tidies itself when it's next opened. One writer at a time per library across windows (Web Locks), reads unlocked (each .json is swapped in whole). Tests: tests/filestore.js; and any test runs on the files store with `STORE=opfs` when the test copy is made (make-debug-copy.py): bkkeep, undo, copylib, share, p37, stopimport, dlcrash, miss, part, fold, rev, dup all OK that way (tests that open IndexedDB themselves can't). **Libraries in a folder the person chose** (1.4.153, Chrome/Edge on a computer; kr37's option C, 4 Nov 2026: the closest a browser gets to Move and manage, and down from three copies to two). Settings → Library info → **Kept in** → **Keep it in a folder…** (`moveLibraryToFolder`): explains (where, about how much, automatic backups stop), the person picks a place (`showDirectoryPicker`, starting in Music), 37 Player makes a folder named after the library there (or, 1.4.180, kr37: with several libraries he made an empty folder for this one first and got `Name/Name`, so a folder that is this library's, or empty and named like it, is used as it is, and another empty folder asks "Use “X”" or "Make “Name” inside", the latter suggested when X looks like a holder such as "37 Player Libraries" or Music) with `37 Player library.json` (`FOLDER_MARKER`: format, library ID, name) and a README, copies audio and artwork, then the state records last, while holding the sync, transfer, backup, standardize and incoming locks; checks every key arrived and the library record's revision didn't move; then marks the library list entry `folder` (the folder's name), keeps the folder's handle in its own tiny database `37player-folders` (browsers remember folders only as handles in IndexedDB; lose it and the person points at the folder again), tells other windows (`moved`: they stop and reload) and reloads. A stopped or failed copy leaves the browser's library as it was; choosing the same place again carries on. **A folder that already holds a library is never written into** (2.2.019, the plan's 5.1): only this library's own unfinished copy is carried on into, known by the marker's `copyToken` (a random token kept in the library's own storage, state record `folderCopy`, so it goes when that storage goes) or by having no library record yet (the records are written last, `folderHasLibraryRecord`); another library's folder, whether chosen itself or found inside the chosen folder under the library's name, is refused ("already holds a 37 Player library"), and no library is made inside one. Before, the ID alone decided, and every browser's first library is `main`: after Chrome's storage was cleared, a new, empty first library pointed at the old library's folder wrote its empty records over that library's (the recordings stayed on disk, unlisted). kr37's T470 try (read-only files) failed quietly for that reason. A sub-folder of another library's folder can't be noticed: a browser can't look upward from a chosen folder. Since 2.2.020 the refusal offers **Open that library**. **Opening a library folder that's already there** (2.2.020, the plan's 5.2; `openLibraryFolder`): Settings → Libraries → **Find a library folder…** in Chrome/Edge too (the desktop app's button and wording, `canOpenLibraryFolder`), for Chrome's data cleared while a library was in a folder, a computer given a copy of the folder (USB stick), and the folder Keep it in a folder… refuses. Nothing in the folder is written by opening it. The library keeps the folder's ID (its marker's), since `libraryFolderDir` finds a folder by it, so when this browser already has that ID: a library in another folder → asks, then uses this folder instead (the other stays); a library in the browser's storage that is still only the starter (`libraryIsJustStarter`, now usable on another library's records via `libraryJustStarterIn`; the case of every new first library, `main`) → the folder's library takes its place and name, the browser's copy left alone; one with tracks or playlists of its own → refused ("same identity"), nothing changed. Otherwise it's added to the list (toast, as in the desktop app); this window's own library starts again from the folder; other windows on it get `moved`. A desktop app's folder opens too; tracks it uses in place show as not connected (the browser can't reach those folders). Not built, on purpose (kr37, 12 Nov 2026: keep refusing; he'll test it): opening a folder whose ID clashes with a browser library that has content. It would need the folder to take a new ID, which would break its being found by anything that knows the old one. Test: folderlib.js. After the reload, `offerRemoveBrowserCopy` offers to delete the IndexedDB copy (Remove the browser's copy / Not now). `storageOpen` gives a folder library a FolderLibraryStore on `libraryFolderDir(id)`, which checks permission and the marker. **Start:** `ensureLibraryFolder` before anything is read: no permission → "Open it" (asks); folder gone or note lost → **Find the folder…** (refuses a folder whose marker isn't this library's); or **Open another library**. An installed app keeps the permission; a tab may ask after a restart. **Backups:** a folder library is its own safe copy (kr37: no continuous backups in v2): `autoBackupActive` and `libraryNeedsBackup` are false, `libraryProtected` true, the backup button reads **In a folder** (tooltip: back it up with the computer, or export a .37p; clicking still offers a manual backup). **Deleting** such a library forgets it; the folder and its files stay, and the dialog says so. Not yet: readable artist/album names; the 24-hour Deleted folder; a Move that removes originals. Test: tests/folderlib.js (stand-in folders in OPFS). No change in behaviour: 17 tests run (libs, copylib, undo, full, quota, notloaded, libdel, bkkeep, restore, e2e, dlcrash, pieces, damaged, share, p37, stopimport).
- **Readable recording names** (1.4.174; v2, kr37: integral, the same week as leaving IndexedDB). In a library kept as files (Chrome/Edge folder libraries, the OPFS test store, later the desktop app), recordings are `music/<Artist>/<Album>/<NN> <Title>.<ext>` (under `music/` since 1.4.179, kr37: the folder's top reads like a table of contents, and it matches the portable export), named as the portable export names them (`recordingPathFor`, `recordingPathPart`: `Unknown Artist` / `Unknown Album`, what Windows and the Mac refuse becomes "-", device names get "_", parts kept to 80 characters); a clash gets " (2)". Everything else is in `37 Player's own files/` (a library made before keeps `state/`, `audio/`, `artwork/` at its top, and only its recordings move out). The audio record points at its recording by `{"$path": …}`. `renameRecordings` runs 2.5 s after each save (`scheduleRecordingRenames`) and once after opening: a recording whose details changed is moved (so Undo moves it back); one a player holds is copied and the old file waits its day (`canMove`, `trackHeldByAPlayer`); a change of capitals only is left alone, since Windows and Mac folders ignore case and the old name would be tidied away. Readable files go on the waiting list before they're written, so tidying removes one an interrupted write left, but never a file 37 Player didn't write; emptied album and artist folders go too. Re-tagging writes over the recording's own file (same name): a player holding it at that moment may stop. The folder's README says what's where (kept current, `keepFiles("readme")`). **`playlists/`** (1.4.179, kr37): an .m3u8 for each playlist, in its folders, pointing into `../music/`, written as the portable export writes them (`buildPlaylistEntries`), tracks only (silences and stops left out); a courtesy for other players, never read by 37 Player; kept in step after each change (`syncPlaylistFiles` → `FolderLibraryStore.keepFiles`, which rewrites only what changed and removes only files it wrote itself, remembered in `37 Player's own files/written.json`). Only the app's own library gets names (`storageOpen` for `LIBRARY_ID`, the move into a folder); IndexedDB is unchanged. **Read once (2.5.021; made as 2.5.018 on the `slowSave` branch in the conversation "Mac mini update bug", renumbered when merged into `dev` after the Bring back work had used 2.5.018–2.5.020):** `renameRecordings` and `syncPlaylistFiles`'s `recordingPaths` each read every audio record after every save. On kr37's 2014 Mac mini (707 tracks, the desktop app, three calls to the shell per record) that held the library's lock for most of a minute, so the next edit's Save waited that long (15 Nov 2026, pasting artwork into several tracks: the button bounced, nothing happened, then the dialog closed). Now the store keeps each recording's file details in memory (`recordingList`, read once; `noteRecording` from its own writes, `rekey` and moves), and a record is read only to move it; another window's "library" message resets the list (`forgetRecordings`), and a list read while something was written, or with a record that couldn't be read, isn't kept. Tests: readnames.js (the store, including that nothing is read when nothing moves), folderlib.js (moving in, renaming on an edit).
- **Several libraries on one device** (1.4.001; Settings → Switch libraries, and the name in the top bar once there's more than one). Each library is its own database: the first is `temple-sound-library` (id `main`), the others `temple-sound-library~<id>`. The list is in localStorage `37player.libraries` (names, sizes, the last one chosen, ids of deleted ones), and each database also holds its own name (state record `libraryInfo`) so a lost list is rebuilt from `indexedDB.databases()` (`rediscoverLibraries`).
  - **Switching reloads the page** into the other library (`switchLibrary`), so nothing in memory carries over. Which library a window has open is per window (sessionStorage `37player.library`); two windows can have two libraries open.
  - **Per library:** everything in its database (library, view settings, session, backup record and folder, `sharedBase`, `sharedPending`, `sharedSeen`), and the channels, locks and localStorage keys that concern its contents: `libScoped()` adds `~<id>` (`37player-sync`, the sync, transfer, backup, tag-snapshot, mp4-repair and standardize locks, `37player.analyzing`, `37player.sharedLog`). The first library keeps the old names, so an older window still agrees with a newer one.
  - **Per device:** `DEVICE_SETTING_KEYS` (language, display, screen-off playback, the default device name, server addresses) in localStorage `37player.device`, laid over each library's settings on load and saved with every save; a `storage` event brings another window's change in before this window can save the old value back. The converter stays in the first library's database whichever is open (`mainStateGet/Put/Delete`). Device-wide channels and locks: `37player-playing`, `37player-version`, `37player-convert`, `37player-converter-fetch`, and `37player-libraries` (the list changed; a library was deleted).
  - **Only the open library syncs.** A new library starts empty (the starter prayer is only for the first). New library → Join a shared library joins at once after the reload (sessionStorage `37player.pendingJoin`) and takes the shared library's name. Joining from a library that already has tracks offers a new library first, since combining would put them in front of everyone.
  - **Deleting** is for libraries that aren't open: their database is deleted (the first library's is emptied instead, as it holds the converter). Windows on it are told, close it at once, and reload into another.
- **State is split** into the library (`library`, `tree`, `rev`) and the view (settings, session). The library is written only when its hash changes, and only if no other window has written since (`idbWriteLibraryIfCurrent`). A BroadcastChannel keeps other windows live.
- **Old windows:** two windows on one computer share one library, so a window left open on an old version syncs by old rules (a 1.3.021 window and a 1.3.045 one re-saved the library every 3 minutes all night). Each window announces its version on BroadcastChannel `37player-version`; one that hears of a newer version stops syncing and asks to be reloaded (1.3.047). Devices also strip local-only fields (`SYNC_LOCAL_TRACK_FIELDS`) from the shared library as they receive it (1.3.046).
- **Rule for all code:** `root.library` and `root.tree` can be replaced at any moment by another window or a sync. Hold IDs, never long-lived object references.
- **`sw.js`** can't be inlined (a spec restriction). It caches only the app page and `help.json`, network-first, and skips Range requests.
- **Help** is Markdown in `help/NNN Title.md`. It's embedded into the HTML because a `file://` copy can't fetch `help.json`. It's English only, by design.
- **The UI** is translated into 9 languages (en, es, fr, pt, de, zh, vi, el, it). Every new string goes into all nine.

## Playback model

- **Highlighted vs. queued.** "Highlighted" (`selectedItemId`) is UI only. "Queued or playing" (`currentItemId`) changes only through `activateItem()`. A single click highlights; a double-click or Enter plays from the start.
- **Each step.** The next item is looked up fresh when an item ends, so edits made mid-session take effect. Deleting the playing entry lets it finish.
- **Silences and stops.** Silences tick on their own clock. Stops wait for a click.
- **Only the latest press counts.** If a track's audio must be fetched first (shared library), a press that's still waiting is dropped when another is made. Playback never starts by itself minutes later.
- **Loudness.** "Even out loudness" and per-track +/- adjustments use `el.volume` when turning down. Turning up needs Web Audio (`createMediaElementSource` into a GainNode). Once an element is routed that way, it stays routed until reload.
- **No peak limit** (1.4.098, `trackLevel`; before, 1.4.059, evening out stopped at the loudest peak). Evening out and a track's own boost (✎, `volumeDb`) both go past the peak if that's what it takes; the details say how far past full volume the peaks go (`over`) and that turning the track down by as much avoids the distortion. kr37: loud enough on small speakers, with a warning, beats too quiet. Album by album is the default kind (`levelKind`, Settings lists it first). Album mode is one gain per album, an album being its name and artist since 1.4.131 (its combined loudness), and each track's own Volume is added on top in either mode, and also with evening out off.

- **A silence's clock on a computer** (1.4.158; SKBC's Mac mini, the desktop app, 5 Nov 2026: a 60-second silence took 60–69 s with the window covered, 69 s hidden; kr37's stopwatch). A silence's time is real time (`advanceSilence`), but it was only looked at on animation frames and the silent loop's `timeupdate`, which a covered or hidden window on a Mac gets rarely; tracks moved on on time, because a recording's `ended` still comes promptly. Now, except on touch screens (phones and tablets keep their tested arrangement), a silence also plays a silent WAV exactly as long as the time left, in pieces of up to 30 s (`runSilenceClock`, `silentWavBlob`: 8 kHz, 8-bit), and each piece's `ended` looks at the clock. kr37's earlier tests on other computers may not have caught a few seconds a minute either. Test: tests/silclock.js (no animation frames or timeupdates from page load; fails on 1.4.157).

### Playing with the screen off (phones and iPads)

The hardest-won part. Found and fixed in October 2026 (1.3.022 to 1.3.028), using the playback log.

- **The problem:** on Android and iPadOS, a track that starts while the app is off screen (screen locked, or another app in front) plays for a couple of seconds, then goes silent. The player still reports "playing" and its time keeps running. The sound comes back when the app returns to the screen.
- **The fix:** while the app is off screen and the player is playing, a silent 10-second WAV loops on its own `<audio>` element (`silentKeepAlive`, `updateLoopHold`). The page never stops making sound in the phone's eyes. The same loop already carried silences along with the screen off.
- **Between items** (`playBridge`): the moment after one item ends and before the next is really playing counts as still playing. The loop stays on and the lock screen keeps saying "playing".
- **Stops** (`stopHold`): with "Keep the lock screen ready at stops" on (default), the silent loop runs through a stop, the lock screen shows the stop's name, and its play (or pause) button continues the session. Off saves battery; then a stop needs 37 Player itself.
- **Pauses off screen** (`pauseHold`, same setting, now "…at stops and pauses"): after a pause with the app off screen, the silent loop runs for up to 30 minutes so the lock screen's play still works. Without it an iPad put the page to sleep at once, and play gave no sound.
- **Lock-screen commands:** Media Session "play" only plays and "pause" only pauses. They used to toggle, so a stray "play" from the phone paused a playing track.
- **A computer's "play" on waking is ignored** (2.6.000; kr37's MacBook, report 2026-11-20-153553: twice, 37 Player started playing when he opened the lid, though it had been paused). The log showed the same pattern both times (19 Nov 1:26 PM, 20 Nov 10:26 AM): as the lid closed, macOS sent "pause" with 37 Player already paused ("already paused: ignored"), and on waking, "play", which was obeyed. So on a computer (`useSilenceClock()`), a Media Session "play" that comes more than a minute (`WAKE_PLAY_AFTER_MS`) after a "pause" that found 37 Player already paused, with nothing played since (the audio element's `play` clears it), is ignored, with a playback-log line. Only that one: a second press of the play key plays. Phones and tablets keep every "play", since their lock screen is how people use it. A short sleep (under a minute) can still resume; a wake with no "pause" before it isn't recognised (not seen).
- **Phone pauses at a track's start:** if the phone pauses a track within 5 seconds of its start, with the app off screen and no press, it's restarted once. Later pauses (a phone call, unplugged headphones) are respected.
- **Not on computers** (2.5.046, branch `noSilentLoopOnDesktop`; kr37's MacBook, desktop app, 18 Nov 2026). `silentLoopUsed()` (the opposite of `useSilenceClock()`): the loop plays only on phones and tablets. On a computer there is no `pauseHold` or `stopHold`, `updateLoopHold` never wants the loop, and a silence is timed by the silence clock alone (1.4.158, below in "A silence's clock on a computer"). **Why:** 37 Player froze after the lid had been closed for two hours. The media process hung at 2:02:23 PM, the loop's last stop ("silent loop off"); the page kept going until its sync check at 2:11:30 and froze before the next (report 2026-11-19-020252-ea88a6), the first time it then asked the media process something that waits for an answer. So the page was free for those nine minutes: the media process got stuck by itself, inside Apple's framework, and the page only walked into it. (The first reading, a circle through the page's main thread supplying the WAV's bytes, doesn't fit that; feeding the silence clock's pieces from the shell instead was weighed and dropped for it.)  The menu bar answered, the window didn't. The playback log (localStorage, copied off the disk) ended with "app back on screen" and "silent loop off". `sample` of the three processes, twice 40 minutes apart, showed the same waits each time. The WebKit media process's main thread was inside `RemoteMediaPlayerProxy::pause()` → `-[AVPlayerItem currentTime]`, waiting on a MediaToolbox lock that its other threads were also waiting on. Its audio reader was waiting inside `WAVEAudioFile::ReadBytes`, which was the loop's WAV. The page's main thread was waiting on a synchronous message to that process (`AudioSession::tryToSetActive`), so it could do nothing. This looks like Apple's bug, not ours. But the loop had started and stopped every time the window was hidden with a track paused, 60 times since the morning before, all of it for a lock screen a computer doesn't have. That made the bug far more likely to hit. The setting "Keep the lock screen ready at stops and pauses (mobile devices)" does nothing on a computer now, so since 2.5.047 it isn't shown there, on the Playback page or in All settings (hidden as "Keep syncing with the screen off" already was; suggested 18 Nov 2026 and easily changed back). "Keep syncing with the screen off" already needed a touch screen (`COARSE_POINTER`). **Not done** (kr37: no more background work): a watch on the page from the shell. The shell's stall watch (2.5.035) sees only its own thread, so this freeze left no trace there. Test: tests/noloop.js. tests/ph.js, stoplock.js and bridge3.js now run as an iPad.
- **Diagnosis:** the **playback log** (Settings → Playback, `playLog()`) records track starts, the app leaving and returning to the screen, pauses the phone made, and a "still playing" line every minute. Keep it. Ask for it first whenever playback misbehaves on a device.

## Shared library sync (1.3.000 onward)

The full plan and history are in the project doc `shared-library-sync.md`. In short:

- **Server:** `sync.php` is plain PHP, with no framework or database. Libraries are joined by a secret code; the server stores only its hash.
- **Versions:** each library version is one JSON file, so history comes free. Saves are "only if still at version N" (`flock`); otherwise the server returns 409 and the app merges and retries. Audio and artwork are named `id.rev` and never overwritten.
- **Merging** is three-way: this device, the shared library, and the last-agreed base (IndexedDB `sharedBase`).
  - A change made in one place wins.
  - A track changed in both places merges field by field.
  - A playlist changed in both places: both are kept, with the device name added to the copy and a ⚠ mark.
  - Edited on one device, deleted on another: the edit wins.
  - A sibling merely moving is not an edit, so it never revives a deletion.
- **Engine:** the merge engine is the `SYNC-MERGE:BEGIN/END` block, pure functions with their own tests.
- **Transfers:**
  - Audio moves in the background, one window at a time: `navigator.locks`, with separate locks for down and up.
  - It waits while anything plays, because transfers made phones crackle. A track you press play on is still fetched at once, with one download per file at a time.
- **Keep syncing with the screen off** (phones and tablets): a setting that stays as set (`cfg.keepAwake`). While on, whenever there's something to send or receive and nothing is playing, a silent loop keeps the page awake, with progress on the lock screen. Playback stops it; it restarts the moment playback stops. The lock screen's pause holds it off until the app is next on screen or plays. When a track is paused part-way, the lock screen keeps the track ("Paused · Receiving 2 of 5"), and its play (or pause) button resumes the track.
- **Review changes:** a FreeFileSync-style overlay with arrows per row (send / take / skip). It holds back automatic syncing while open.
- **What's on this device** (1.4.007, `openSharedChoose`): the whole shared library as a tree with checkboxes and sizes; Albums open (▸) into their tracks, each ticked on its own with its size (1.4.068; ticked, a track comes with no playlist, which the selection already allowed: it's the set of folders, playlists and loose tracks); a track that comes with a ticked playlist shows ticked and locked, "comes with a playlist". Apply (`sharedApplySelection`) builds this device's document without what was unticked, marks what was ticked "take", and runs the review steps (`syncReviewRows`, `syncClassifyPartial`, `syncApplyDependencies`, `syncApplyReview`), so the engine is unchanged. On a full-sync device, unticking switches it to partial first, after asking. Settings → Shared library: **On this device: Everything / Only what I choose** replaces the Partial sync switch; with Only what I choose, **New playlists from other devices come here by themselves** (`cfg.autoNew`: before an ordinary sync, `sharedBringNewOnes` ticks what's new since last looked, by `sharedSeen`; turning it on marks everything current as seen). Opening What's on this device marks what's new as seen too (1.4.062: the count goes, the New marks stay for that visit), unless `cfg.autoNew` is on; before, only Apply did, and Apply needs a change, so "2 new" couldn't be dismissed. The New marks are bright green and beside the name (1.4.063): the small amber ones went unnoticed. Review changes is now only for Review mode; the "new" badge opens What's on this device.
- **Partial sync** (a "left sync", "Only what I choose" in the app):
  - **What it means, since this keeps being forgotten:** a LEFT JOIN with this device on the left. Everything on the device is also in the shared library; the shared library may hold more that this device doesn't. So once a partial-sync device is up to date, the shared library already has every one of its files, exactly as with a full-sync device. "Partial" only ever means the device holds less, never that the shared library does. Nothing on a partial device is device-only (that would be the old "On this device only" folder idea, never built).
  - Everything on the device syncs both ways.
  - New playlists from others arrive only when chosen in Review.
  - New tracks in playlists the device already holds arrive automatically.
  - Deleting offers three choices: Cancel / Remove from this device / Delete everywhere.
- **Missing tracks** are marked big and clear, saying where each one is coming from ("on its way from {device}" or "waiting for {device} to send it").
- **A dialog closes first, then does its work** (2.5.031, 16 Nov 2026; kr37: "when someone presses Save, can the dialog disappear before any of the background work starts? Then it would feel snappy": 1) the dialog goes, 2) the status bar says "Saving…", 3) the work). Found: Edit details waited for its picture's write and the library's save, which waits its turn behind sync and other saves (the SKBC Mac mini's minute, report 2026-11-15-213154); Delete closed at once but left the tracks on screen until every one's audio had been put aside for Undo and the save was done (likely Gen Palden's "laggy when deleting", report 2026-11-16-073224); editing several tracks redrew only after every picture was written; Replace audio file closed and then showed nothing until it had finished. Now each closes, shows the change at once (the library in memory has changed already) and hands the rest to **`afterDialog(textKey, failKey, work)`**: the jobs run one at a time in the order given (so a picture put aside for Undo is never overtaken by the next edit of the same track), and the status bar shows `bgSaving` / `bgDeleting` / `bgReplacing` only when a job takes over half a second (`AFTER_DIALOG_SHOW_MS`, so quick saves don't flash). **Is there any benefit to leaving the dialog up?** (kr37 asked.) No: a save that fails already turns the top bar red and is tried again every 30 s, the change kept in the window (`noteSaveFailed`), so `afterDialogFailed` stays quiet then; anything else that fails (a picture's write, audio put aside) says so in a dialog ("Not finished", `bgSaveFailed`…) once the screen is free, and in the playback log. **Safeguards:** while a job waits, closing the window asks (`beforeunload`; `closingWouldStop` → "saving your last change" in the desktop app), and Undo, Redo, Update now and switching libraries wait for it (`afterDialogIdle`), since its writes go to the library that's open. **Details:** Edit details' fields go in at once (`commitEditFields` now returns the rest as a function), for Save, Convert, Replace audio file and Split alike; Replace's file picker opens at once, no longer after the save (a long wait could outlast the click the browser needs to open it). A new picture shows at once through `artworkUrlCache`; `hasArtwork` changes only with the write, which sets `artRev`, which sync goes by. `deleteLibraryTracks` does everything on screen before its first await, so callers that redraw their own list (Library cleanup's, Track details') do so at once; it still returns the job's promise for those that wait. Playlist and folder deletes already redrew at once. **Writing tags into the files stays** (kr37: whatever the person sees, their library and its files, should be completely usable; compromise only if it causes great difficulty): it was already in the background, and now nothing waits for it. Test: tests/dlgfast.js (with saving held up 3 s: `__ad.slowSaves`), in the browser and with `STORE=desktop`.
- **Removing a library can move its folder to the Trash** (2.5.032 with shell 2.5.2, 16 Nov 2026; kr37: the remove question could also offer "Remove local library files from disk (20 playlists, 300 tracks, 4.5 GB)", with a second, stern "Heads up!" question). Settings → Libraries → Remove from list… has a third button, **Remove and move its folder to the Trash…** (Recycle Bin on Windows: `trashWord`), only in the desktop app with a shell that says `trash` (`canTrashLibraries`): a web page can't use the Trash, only delete outright, and **nothing is deleted outright**, so a mistaken yes can be undone (the reason "remove from the list" was made to touch nothing in 2.4.003 still stands for that button). The second question (`trashLibraryAsked`) says what goes (playlists, tracks, the folder's size from the shell's dry run), that files used in place stay (they're outside the folder), and how much would be lost, from the library's own records with no connection (`libraryTrashFacts`): all in its shared library (its `sharedCovered` fingerprint matches `libraryFingerprint` of its records) or a backup with all of it (the backup record's `lastFingerprint`) is a calm line; joined but not all sent when last open, or an older backup, is a red strip (`.trash-risk`); not shared and no backup, the sternest ("This is the only copy of this library"). Cancel is the first, highlighted button; **Move to the Trash** is red. `deleteLibrary(id, {trash: true})` takes it off the list, lets other windows let go (the 400 ms it already waits), then `fs_root_trash`; if that fails, the library is still off the list and the person is told its folder stays and Find a library folder… brings it back. **Shell 2.5.2** (`files.rs`): `fs_root_trash(id, dry)` only for a library's own folder (`lib-…`) holding its `37 Player library.json`, never the home folder, one holding it, or a drive's top (`may_trash`); the `trash` crate, on the Mac by NSFileManager, which asks for no permission (the Finder way asks to control Finder; Finder may not offer Put Back for it, but the folder can be dragged out). Tests: tests/libtrash.js (fake-shell's `fs_root_trash`, `__fakeTrash`), the shell's `only_a_library_folder_may_go_to_the_trash`. **Removing no longer makes an empty folder in its place** (2.5.056; kr37's MacBook, 19 Nov 2026, report 2026-11-19-184915-2a6a08: a new "My library (n)" moments after each removal). Remove's questions read the removed library's records (`readLibraryBackupRecord`, `libraryTrashFacts`) through a store of its own, and every store opened tidied its folder 20 s later; by then the folder was in the Trash, or forgotten by Remove from the list, so the store's open made it again (`desktopLibraryDir` → `fs_root_default`, named "My library"), empty, with the removed library's ID in its marker. Fixed in two places, so neither alone has to hold: only the window's own library tidies (above, the storage layer), and `desktopLibraryDir` never makes a folder for a library that's off the list and isn't this window's (NotFoundError). Folders already made that way hold only a README and empty records, and nothing lists them (the ID is in the list's `deleted`): kr37 puts them in the Trash himself; no repair pass, by his rule. Nothing is lost by a removed library missing that tidy: it only deletes files unused for a day, which the library already did while open, and it runs again if the library is brought back. Test: libtrash.js (25 s after Move to the Trash, and after Remove from the list, no new folder).
- **The Playing screen with no playlists at all** (2.5.032, kr37, 16 Nov 2026) says "First, make a playlist on “Edit Playlists”." instead of "Choose a playlist from the list to begin." (`noPlaylistsYetToBegin`, the tab's own name). Test: tests/emptyplay.js.
- **A new dialog starts at its top** (2.5.034, kr37's Mac, 16 Nov 2026: after Send feedback the "Thank you" dialog was an empty box until a click outside closed it). The feedback form is taller than a laptop's window and scrolls; it was scrolled down to its Send button, and the next dialog reuses the same element (`modalBody`), so WebKit most likely kept that scroll on the much shorter "Thank you" and showed only its empty lower part (Chrome resets it itself, so the Chrome tests can't see it; the WebKit tests on GitHub can). `closeModal` sets `scrollTop` to 0, and so does `openModal` for a dialog that wasn't already open (one drawn again while open keeps its place). Test: tests/feedback.js.
- **"Not here yet" names the sending device only when the shared library doesn't have the file** (2.5.033, kr37, 16 Nov 2026: his new Mac, still downloading, went on to the next track and was told "Rz Pixel 6a still has to send it", though the file was already in the shared library and arrived moments later; the list said simply that it was on its way). `showAudioMissing` went by `audioFrom` (who sent it) whatever the server had; now it goes by what the lists go by (`sharedTrackMissing`: not there at the last check, `sharedNotOnServer`) or a 404 just now (`"absent"`). In the shared library: "is on its way to this device now"; not there: the device, or "it was to send this copy, but no longer has it" when that's this device (as `sharedMissingNote`). `sharedEnsureAudioWithin` says "absent" for a fetch that ends with nothing only when the server didn't have it, else "fetching". Test: tests/miss.js.
- **What the desktop app saw, in the playback log** (2.5.035 with shell 2.5.3, 16 Nov 2026; Gen Palden's MacBook, report 2026-11-16-073224: sometimes 37 Player crashed outright, sometimes a spinning wheel for about 30 s, and a restart at 8:23:57 while two ~60 MB recordings went to the shared library again after a rename). The page can't see its own window not answering, or how the last session ended, so the shell keeps both (`watch.rs`): a thread asks the main thread to run a no-op every 250 ms and counts any wait over 2 s as a stall (with when, how long, and whether the window was in front; a tick that comes much later than due is the computer asleep, not a stall); `running.json` in the app's data folder, its time renewed every 30 s and removed on a clean exit (Quit, the window closed, an update about to install), so one found at start means the last session ended unexpectedly, with the Rust panic's message if there was one (release builds abort on a panic). `p37_watch` gives both once; the page asks every 20 s (`desktopWatchPoll`, when `desktopShell.watch`) and writes "37 Player didn't close normally last time (app 2.5.3, last seen running at …); it stopped on an error: …" and "the app's window didn't respond for 31 s, from 13:22:40 (in the background)". Also logged: a file of 16 MB or more read whole into the page or written through the app, with its size and time (`bigMoveLog`: every piece passes the window's main thread on a Mac, the likely cause of the wheel), and a start that was a reload of the window (`performance` navigation type) rather than a fresh one. Tests: tests/watchlog.js (fake-shell's `p37_watch`, `__fakeWatch`), the shell's `a_held_up_main_thread_is_a_stall`, `the_computer_asleep_is_not_a_stall`, `a_session_file_reads_back`.
- **New details are written into a recording on the disk** (2.5.035 with shell 2.5.3, same report). Renaming a ~60 MB MP3 in the desktop app read it whole into the page (`realBlob` in `blobWithCurrentTags`), wrote it back through the page, and read it whole again to check it (`putRewrittenAudio`'s WebKit check, which is about WebKit's browser storage, not the app's folders): three passes through the main thread and the file in memory, per track. Now, for an MP3 or M4A read where it lies (`blobWithCurrentTags(…, {onDisk: true})`, from `rewriteFileTagsIfPossible` only; exports still read whole), the writers already read only the details' bytes and build the new file from slices, so the result is a `JoinedBlob` (`joinedFile`: neighbouring stretches of the same file joined) of new first bytes and the old file's rest; the app's writer copies each stretch on disk with **`fs_append_range`** (shell 2.5.3: bytes start..end of one file onto the end of another, refusing a file onto itself, a stretch past the end, or a path outside the root) into the `.p37part` beside the file, then renames it on. With an older shell (no `append_range`), the stretch goes through the page piece by piece, still never all in memory. In the app's folders `putRewrittenAudio` checks the stored size from the disk instead of reading the file back. `JoinedBlob` gained `slice` for the readers. Other formats (WAV, FLAC, Ogg, WebM) still read whole, and the 256 MB `BIG_REWRITE_BYTES` limit stays. The re-send to a shared library after a rename still reads the file (that's the upload itself). Test: tests/tagdisk.js (MP3, a faststart M4A whose offsets move, and an older shell; ffprobe checks the name, the length and that it decodes), the shell's `a_stretch_of_a_file_goes_on_the_end_of_another`.
- **A sync with nothing new doesn't download the shared list** (2.5.043; kr37, 18 Nov 2026). Every sync (every 3 minutes, `SHARED_SYNC_EVERY_MS`, and after each change) downloaded and read the whole shared list, even when its version hadn't moved: 361 KB compressed for kr37's 2,800 tracks (measured from state/000927.json.gz, which sync.php sends as-is), about 170 MB a day for a device left open, and the window reading it through every time. Now `sharedGetState` keeps the list it last received in memory with its version (`sharedStateCache`, keyed by library) and sends that version (`?have=N`); sync.php (SYNC_VERSION 2.5.043; kr37 deploys) answers 304 with no body when it's still the latest, and the kept copy stands in for the list. Tried first with the saved base (`sharedLoadBase`) as the copy: rev, choose and part failed, since the base isn't always the server's copy (a device choosing what it holds, a review); only what the server sent is kept now. After this device posts a change, the next sync downloads once (the version moved), as before. The first sync after a start downloads too. Google Drive needs no change at Google: `driveLatest` already lists the newest version files' names first (a small request; there's no "latest" file on Drive on purpose, since Drive can't make two writes one and two devices can save at once, so the listing is the truth), and the backend now returns 304 when that's the version this device has. Older servers ignore `have` and send the list; older pages don't send it. Every caller of `sharedGetState` benefits (a version never changes on the server). Each change still posts the whole list back; sending only changes was judged not worth its cost (server merging, every device agreeing). Test: tests/notmod.js; drive.js covers Google Drive.
- **Deleting a track whose audio hasn't reached the shared library, on a device with everything** (2.5.041; kr37, 17 Nov 2026: in his experience these are mostly not syncing delays but the sending device's library disconnected or deleted, so the audio may never come). `confirmDelete` (full sync, not receive-only) asks `confirmDeleteNotArrived` when any of the tracks is waiting (`sharedTrackMissing(id).waiting`): it names the device that has to send it, and offers **Only on this device** (this device switches to choosing what it holds, `cfg.partial`, logged in the sync log; the note says that if the device sends the audio later the track can be chosen again, through Settings → Shared library → What's on this device, built from the app's own labels; kr37: not "it can be brought back", since the shared library doesn't have it and may never), **Delete everywhere** (the usual delete; the note says it will be deleted from the sending device too, kr37's wording, 2.5.042), or Cancel. Only on this device with playlists here using the tracks goes on to the partial-sync question about those playlists. Test: tests/missdel.js (`__dbg3.delTracks`, `__dbg3.cfg`).
- **History says what changed, not "different audio", for a rename** (2.5.040; kr37: "different audio" says the sound changed, when it was a rename). `histAudioWord` (replacing 2.5.039's `histJustRetagged`): a new audio revision is "different audio" only when the length differs by half a second or more, or nothing else is known; when the name, artist, album or artwork changed too, only that shows; otherwise "file size changed" (`histOtherSize`) if it did, else nothing.
- **Deleting in a playlist selects the next item** (2.5.040; kr37, deleting stops one after another with Backspace): the item that takes the deleted one's place, or the one above when it was last. Test: tests/editdel.js.
- **Scrollbars always shown** (2.5.040; kr37: disappearing scrollbars, the Mac's default, are a hassle for less practised people). Thin, in the theme's colours (`--scroll-thumb`), drawn with `::-webkit-scrollbar` (WebKit, Chrome), with the standard properties for Firefox only (in Chrome they'd replace these); only with a mouse or trackpad (`hover:hover` and `pointer:fine`), so phones and tablets keep their own. Headless Chrome hides scrollbars, so the tests can't show them.
- **Checks over every track no longer read every recording** (2.5.039, kr37's log 2026-11-17-130821 and his History: a version of "1036 tracks changed" with nothing to see). In the desktop app, loading a stored recording of 32 MB or less reads it whole (`DesktopFileHandle.getFile`), so `reconcileFileSizes` (8 s after every start) and the missing-audio check in Library cleanup (`tracksWithMissingAudio`, "thorough": opens each file) put the whole library through the page. On kr37's new Mac it most likely ran from the 7:21 start until 7:48 (versions saved at 7:37–7:42 don't have the sizes yet) and saved the sizes of 1036 tracks (sizes aren't shown in History, so the version looked empty). Now `storedAudioSize(id, probe)` asks the store: `FolderLibraryStore.blobSize` reads the record's .json and stats its file (`fs_stat`; for probe, one byte with `fs_read`); a browser's file handle doesn't read the file for its size. The playback log's big-transfer lines now list whole files only, not the 16 MB pieces a recording is sent in, and recordings only, not the library's own .json files (its list of tracks is 22 MB on kr37's Mac, written at every save: worth a look some day). History doesn't say "different audio" when the name, artist, album or artwork changed too and the length is the same (`histJustRetagged`): renaming or new artwork writes the details into the file, so its revision is new, but the recording is the same. Test: tests/sizecheck.js.
- **FLAC, WAV and AIFF details written on disk too** (2.5.038, from the audit). Their writers (`rewriteFlacTags`, `rewriteChunkTags`) already built the new file from slices; they now give a `joinedFile`, and `blobWithCurrentTags(…, {onDisk})` keeps these kinds where they lie, so the app copies the sound on disk as for MP3 and M4A. Ogg and WebM still read whole. (A track with no `fileTags` snapshot keeps a FLAC's or WAV's own title until its name is edited: by design, `effectiveTagMeta`.) Test: tests/tagdisk.js.
- **A test for names declared twice** (2.5.038): tests/names.js fails if 37-player.html declares a top-level function, const, let, var or class twice. 2.5.037's new `sameBytes` replaced the tag writers' `sameBytes`, so changed artwork stopped being written into M4A and FLAC files. Found on `dev` before release, and renamed `sameFileBytes`. The one other duplicate, `libraryEntry` (two identical copies since 1.4.193), lost its second copy.
- **Import's duplicate check compares a piece at a time** (2.5.037, from the audit). A file the same size as a track already here was compared by hashing both whole (SHA-256), once per candidate: whole recordings through the page in the desktop app while importing. `sameFileBytes` reads 4 MB (not `sameBytes`, which compares two arrays of bytes: the first try reused that name and replaced it, breaking the artwork check in the tag writers; caught before release, 2.5.038) of each at a time and stops at the first difference; `hashFileSHA256` (its only use) went. Test: tests/samefile.js.
- **The next track ready before it's needed** (2.5.036; kr37, 16 Nov 2026: from the plan for steady playing, "a very easy-to-understand concept"). Before, the move on at the end of a track (`audioEl` "ended" → `skip(1)` → `activateItem`) read the next recording from storage at that moment (`getPlayableAudioUrl`; in the desktop app a file of 32 MB or less read whole through the page), with whatever else the window was doing. Now, `NEXT_READY_AFTER_MS` (5 s) after any item starts, `prepareNextTrack` makes the next track ready: the first track after it, past silences but not past a stop (after a stop item itself, the one after it). Only a track already on this device (no download during playback, nothing from a drive not plugged in). Its address stays in `objectUrlCache` (`releaseUnusedAudioUrls` keeps `nextReadyId`) until it plays or another is made ready, so at most one more recording is held; the Linux app's local address isn't kept, so there's nothing to make ready there. The start line in the playback log says ", ready ahead" when it was used, for measuring. Test: tests/nextready.js (`window.__nextReadyMs`, `window.__nextReady`). In WebKit on GitHub (17 Nov): nextready, play, silclock and stop pass; bridge3 failed once (4 checks of the silent-loop bridge across track changes) and passed on two reruns, and on `main` before this change: the flakiness noted under "In WebKit and Firefox too", it seems, but worth watching.
- **Less of a recording in memory in the desktop app** (2.5.035, kr37, 16 Nov 2026: crashes come mostly while editing, and that's what makes 37 Player feel unreliable). From an audit of every place the app still moved a whole recording through the page: (1) waveforms and loudness (`analyzeAudio`, `measureTrackLoudness`) are made a piece at a time whatever the length when the format allows (`desktopPiecesFirst`; falls back to a whole decode if the pieces fail and the file may be decoded whole). A whole decode held all of a recording's sound at once, up to the 1 GB allowance (WebKit doesn't report memory). (2) Sending from the app's folders skips the read-whole check before each recording (`desktopFolderStore()`; it looks for a Safari browser-storage fault). Also found: `analyzeM4aInPieces` could end on a piece of a few frames that doesn't decode ("Unable to decode audio data", about 1 time in 4 on a 6-minute M4A); a short remainder now goes with the piece before. Not done, by decision: playing tracks of 32 MB or less from the disk rather than read into the page. kr37: 32 MB is small; the log shows track changes start within the second, and moving most tracks to another way of playing risks the main function for no seen gain. Still to do from the audit: exports, the Splitter, "Check stored files". Test: tests/desktopmem.js.
- **The desktop app receives a recording onto one file** (2.5.035, report 2026-11-17-021332: kr37's new Mac froze for good receiving its first recording over 50 MB, 51 MB, during a first download of 724 files). A file over one piece (16 MB) was received as pieces each stored as its own file in the state store, read back into the page at the end, put together there (the whole recording in memory) and written out again: three passes through the page, every byte of each through the window's main thread on a Mac. Now, in the desktop app's folder libraries (`sharedReceivePlace`: `STORAGE_KIND === "desktop"`, a `FolderLibraryStore` whose own folder is a `DesktopDirHandle`), audio goes to `sharedReceiveOnDisk`: each piece is appended (`fs_write` with `p37-append`, which every shell has) to "receiving/<id>.<rev>.part" in the library's own files' folder, then the finished file, checked for its full size, is stored as a whole `DiskBlob`, which the app copies into place on disk (`fs_copy`); the .part and its note go. Each byte passes once and only one piece is in memory. A cut-off download carries on from the .part's size; its total and type are in the state record "dlpart:<name>:disk", written before the first byte, and a .part without it starts afresh. `sharedDropStalePieces` clears both for a file no longer waiting. Pieces kept the old way are dropped and the file starts again. The Safari read-back check isn't needed there (it's about WebKit's browser storage). Browsers, and files of one piece or less, as before. kr37 and Claude (16 Nov) also talked over the main thread and playing: the sound plays on the browser's own threads, but moving to the next track, Pause and the waveform need the main thread, and in the Mac app (as far as we know) so do the audio's bytes from the library folder, so a long stall could drain the buffer. Plan, in order: no whole files through the page in the app; close the gaps in "wait while playing" (a transfer under way finishes its piece; things started by hand run at once); the next track ready before it's needed; measure (the stall lines of 2.5.035 beside the player's waiting/stalled); only if sound still stops, native playback in the app. Test: tests/piecesdisk.js.
- **Dialogs that open by themselves wait their turn** (2.2.028; kr37: "Import finished" made the dialogs he was working in disappear). One dialog area: `openModal` replaces whatever is there, which is right for the steps of one task. Dialogs nobody asked for just then go through `showWhenFree(label, fn, ownSelector)` (or `await myModalTurn(label, ownSelector)` in a function that opens its own): shown once no dialog is open and none has opened for 600 ms (so one doesn't slip in between two steps of a task and get replaced by the next), one at a time, in order; while waiting, the status bar says "… : waiting until the open dialog is closed" (an `activeOps` entry with `waiting`, which `activeOperation` skips). A task's own dialog (found by `ownSelector`, e.g. `#bulkImportStatus`) isn't in the way: its summary or next question takes its place at once. And work finishing closes only its own dialog (`closeOwnModal`): the folder import and the loose-files import used to call `closeModal()` at the end, closing whatever was open. Converted: the folder import's summary and error, Review before importing (after the analysis), conversion failures, unplayable files, the orphan-audio offer, Copy to library's done question; `whenNoModal` is now `showWhenFree` without a label (so it settles too). Not a stack: bringing a half-finished dialog back, with its choices and work in flight, would be fragile. Test: dialogq.js.
- **About lists three versions** (2.2.031, 12 Nov 2026, kr37): the page's, the desktop app's (when in it), and the converter's: "Converter: ffmpeg.wasm 0.12.10-nosimd" (`aboutConverterLine`). It moved there from Library cleanup, whose converter note now says only whether it's on this device and its size. With none on this device yet, the version is the one beside the app (`ffmpeg/VERSION`, on the site and bundled by the desktop build's `copy-app.mjs`), followed by "(not on this device yet…)"; a copy from a file asks 37player.com, and shows "?" if it can't. Tested in `desktop.js`.
- **The desktop app updates itself** (page 2.2.040 with shell 2.2.3, 13 Nov 2026; planned in desktop/PAGE-UPDATES.md item 6 for once the builds were signed; kr37: "is that possible to make it earlier for folks?" Earlier matters: only an app that already has the updater can update itself, so everyone who installs before it ships needs one more download by hand). The shell (`updates.rs`, on tauri-spike) uses Tauri's updater plugin: `p37_shell_update_check` reads its channel's `latest.json` (a "d" build desktop-latest-dev's, an "m" build desktop-latest's), whose packages are signed with the page key and checked with page-key.pub, and offers only a newer version; `p37_shell_update_install` downloads, installs and restarts. The page (`shellUpdateLater`, `offerShellUpdate`): when the shell says `updates`, it checks once a start, a minute and a half in, and asks ("A new version of the desktop app … Update and restart / Not now") only when nothing would be stopped (`closingWouldStop`: playing, sending, copying) and no dialog is open; Not now until the next start; About's Check for updates looks for a newer app first. It saves before installing. The builds make the update packages (`createUpdaterArtifacts`, signed with TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY: the Mac .app.tar.gz, the Windows installer's .sig) and desktop-latest.yml (main) copies them onto the home pages' releases with a `latest.json`. Linux .deb installs can't be updated this way (Tauri replaces AppImages only), so latest.json has no Linux entry. Tested in `shellupd.js`; the real thing needs two builds in a row (install the first, then the second appears on the release).
- **The converter reads a desktop file from the disk as it goes** (2.2.039, 12 Nov 2026; the fix named after the Mac mini's restart of 11 Nov: converting big WAVs one after another, WebKit's Networking process held 4–6 GB, because each input was read whole into Blobs, `realBlob`, for the worker's WORKERFS, and WebKit keeps a Blob's bytes there until the page's collector gets to it). `converterInput` (used by `converterConvert`, `splitViaConverter`, `analyzeWithConverter`): a DiskBlob (a file of the desktop app's, read where it lies, whole or a stretch of one) goes to the worker as `{p37lazy, name, size, start, url}`, its `p37lib://` address (`__p37FileUrl` in tests). In the worker, `lazyInput` serves ffmpeg's reads from one 4 MB block (the shell's most per request), fetched with a synchronous Range request (a worker may wait, and ffmpeg can't be made to), replaced by the next as it reads on; no Blob is made for the blocks. It's mounted with WORKERFS as before, whose read is patched once per worker (`readsFromAddress`) to ask such a file directly. Before the first use, a `probe` reads the file's first bytes that way; if it fails, `converterLazyOff` (until reloaded) and the old way (the file read whole first), and the playback log says which: "the converter reads files from the disk as it goes" or "… can't … here (reason)". Shell 2.2.2 (tauri-spike) answers a CORS preflight on p37lib (`preflight`: OPTIONS → 204 with Allow-Headers Range), in case WebKit asks before a cross-origin request with a Range header; shell 2.2.1 doesn't, and then the probe fails and the old way is used. Not yet tried in WebKit (kr37's Mac mini, tonight): whether a worker may make synchronous requests to p37lib:// at all, and the memory while a 2.7 GB WAV converts. The output is still held whole (in the worker, then a Blob): an MP3 of a 4-hour WAV is about 350 MB, which was comfortable; FLAC (not recommended) would be far more. The big-file warning (one at a time, quit between) stays until the Mac mini shows it's no longer needed. Tested in `lazyconv.js` (Chrome: a 9 MB WAV on the stand-in shell's disk converts with no read of it by the page; a broken address falls back and says so).
- **Small things from kr37's to-do list** (2.2.038, 12 Nov 2026). **Playlist details:** the notes box is six lines (it was twenty, 420 px: most of the window), can still be dragged taller, and has the dialog fields' background (`--bg-deep`, white in the light themes) like the name; Enter in the name saves. **Converting's line in the status bar** has Settings (Settings → File format) and Help (the iTunes / Music.app topic, where converting and the File format setting are explained) after Stop: an operation can now carry more buttons (`setActiveOperationProgress(…, {links})`, `convertBarLinks`). **Volume:** kept per device with the theme (`DEVICE_SETTING_KEYS` "volume", saved 400 ms after it stops moving): it was kept nowhere, so every reload, a page update's included, started at full; and the slider is squared, not cubed (half way -6 dB instead of -18, a quarter -12 instead of -36), since the bottom half went silent too soon. Tested in `pldetails.js`, `conv4.js`, `volume.js`.
- **A folder import's looking-through can be clicked away** (2.2.037, 12 Nov 2026; kr37: importing a big folder of near-duplicates, the Analyzing folder dialog was easy to dismiss, the import seemed abandoned, and the review turned up many minutes later). `analyzeFolderImport` now registers as an operation (`rememberOperationDialog`, `setActiveOperationProgress` with "Checking what to import: n of N…", `analyzingBar`): clicked away, it goes on in the status bar, and clicking the bar brings the dialog back, live. Stop, in the dialog (new) or the bar, ends it with nothing imported and no review. When it's done it closes only its own dialog (`closeOwnModal`): it used to call `closeModal()`, which closed whatever dialog the person had opened meanwhile; the review then waits its turn as before (`myModalTurn`, 2.2.028). Not done (kr37 wasn't sure it helps): Escape offering to go on with the files looked through so far. `window.__p37SlowAnalyze` (ms per file) slows it for tests. Tested in `analyzebar.js`.
- **Waveforms in their own record** (2.5.044, kr37, 18 Nov 2026: his option A, chosen for the list's size). The list of tracks (state/root.json, 22 MB with 2,803 tracks) was mostly each track's 720-number waveform, so every start parsed all of it and every save wrote all of it. Now the library has a record `waveforms` (track ID → peaks, plus a `tag` naming that version) beside `root`. The split and the merge are in the two library stores (`splitLibraryWaveforms`, `mergeLibraryWaveforms`), so the rest of the app still sees tracks with `peaks`: `get("state", "root")` puts them back, and `keys("state")` hides the record. Only the state store's root goes through them (`writeRootIfCurrent`, and the copy's `put`). **In `root`, a track whose waveform is in the record has the placeholder `[0]` (`WAVE_STUB`)**, not no peaks. That's for older versions, which can still open the library: kr37 trying `dev` with his library and going back to `main`, or `main` put back after a release. An older version analyses every track with no peaks again (`measureMissingLoudness`, a whole decode each, saving the whole list after each one: 2,800 times 22 MB). With `[0]` it shows a flat line and leaves them alone; checked with 2.5.043 itself, which also kept the placeholders, so the waveforms were all there again in 2.5.044. A `peaksTried` mark wouldn't do: an older version clears any that isn't its own version at start. An older version might send `[0]` to the shared library as a track's waveform, so `isStandInPeaks` now counts fewer than two numbers as a stand-in: made again here, never sent on. A stand-in stays in `root` as it is. Real peaks in `root` were put there by an older version (or before 2.5.044), so they're the newest and win; the next save moves them out. A save writes the waveforms only when one is a different array from the one stored: arrays are replaced, never changed in place, so identity is the test. A store remembers what it stored under its tag, and a read of the same version hands back those same arrays, so the app's copies keep matching (without that, a read replaced the arrays and the next save wrote every waveform again). A track with no waveform in the list being saved keeps the one stored, so a list without waveforms can't wipe them. A rename writes `view`, `root` and `rootRev`, nothing else. A library an older version saved moves its waveforms out on its first real change (a save with nothing changed writes nothing). A damaged `waveforms` record leaves its tracks without waveforms (a console warning), and they're made again; the list opens. In a folder library, `root.json` is smaller and `waveforms.json` sits beside it; tidy leaves it alone (only non-JSON files are strays). Tests: tests/waveforms.js (both stores, unchanged saves, placeholders, an older version's waveforms, migration, damage).
- **A full device: the messages** (2.5.044, kr37, 18 Nov 2026: his 64 GB iPad on full sync, its library bigger than its free space). The "Changes here aren't being saved" dialog now stays until it's closed. A save that works says so in it ("Saving works again") instead of closing it, and a later failure says "Still not saving": a small save can work on a full device while the files still to come can't be stored. When files from the shared library are still to come here (`sharedStillToComeHere`: what's pending, or what the last pass couldn't store), a full device is probably 37 Player's own doing, so the dialog says that, how many files, and offers **What's on this device…** (unticking there switches a full-sync device to "Only what I choose", after asking) beside freeing space. Receiving that stops because the device is full (`err.storing`) never put the sync in an error state, so Settings said only "waiting for 3 files from other devices". It now says the device is full and why (`storeFull`); the What's on this device… button is just below. A sync that fails on a full device with files still to come says the same. Test: tests/fullowned.js; full.js checks the dialog staying.
- **A recording's extension comes from its content** (page 2.5.048 and shell 2.5.5; kr37, 19 Nov 2026: a festival recording, `2026_KMCSp_FaF_T3_IT_unedited.mp3`, played on Linux but not in the Mac's desktop app; ffprobe: Opus in WebM, "encoder: Chrome", 192 kb/s). Its lineage: Standardize, before the converter, turned a WAV into AAC through the browser's recorder (`encodeAudioBestEffort`), and native Chrome on Linux can't record AAC, so it fell back to Opus/WebM. The recorder's file has no name and is typed `audio/webm;codecs=opus`; `extensionFor` went by name, then by a list of types that lacked WebM (and compared the type with its parameters), so it gave the default `.mp3`. Every device that wrote it into a library folder (the T470, then the MacBook downloading it) named it so. The shell types what it serves by the extension (`mime_for`), and WebKit on the Mac believes it: it handed a WebM to the MP3 player. Chrome and WebKitGTK on Linux look at the content, so they played it. The file itself is sound: today's Chrome writes the duration and the index (Cues), with a cluster about every 30 s (checked by recording one here). Very likely also the "elapsed time doesn't move" of report 2026-11-18-134224. Now: (1) `extensionFor` knows WebM, Matroska, AIFF and Opus and drops a type's parameters; a recording written under a readable name goes by its first bytes (`recordingExtFor`: the name's or type's extension when it agrees with `sniffAudioFormat`, else the content's; content it can't tell keeps the name's). (2) The shell (`mime_for_file`, both p37lib and the Linux http server) reads the first 12 bytes of an audio file and serves the type they show, else the extension's; an ID3 tag in front of a ".flac" keeps FLAC. **No repair of recordings already misnamed** (kr37: he'd rather not carry one-time fixes in the code; a pass at start and then a step in Check library were written and dropped): they keep ".mp3" on disk. **(2) alone didn't make it play** (kr37's MacBook with 65d, 19 Nov 2026): WebKit chooses its player from the address's extension before any answer arrives (`MediaPlayer::load`, when the element gives no type), so it still went to AVFoundation, which can't play WebM; WebKit's own WebM player is chosen only for a WebM type. (3) So (page 2.5.050, shell 2.5.6) a recording whose first bytes and extension disagree is played from an address ending in `/.p37type.<ext>` (`playbackTypeTail`, in `audioObjectUrl`, one small read as it's loaded); the shell takes the tail off before finding the file (`strip_type_suffix`, both servers) and says it can (`typed_path` in p37_shell_info), so an older shell is never sent one. Considered and not chosen: `<source type>` in every player (the code compares `el.src` in many places), and renaming the file as it's played (a repair by another name). Their names matter now only to other players reading music/; delete and import again to rename one. Also: the next track loaded ahead (2.5.036) counts as held by a player (`trackHeldByAPlayer`), so a rename copies its file instead of moving it from under the loaded player. (4) **Confirmed on kr37's MacBook with 66d** (19 Nov 2026): the file plays and seeks. His Replace audio file test then found two more places that went by the name (2.5.052): Replace measured a picked WebM named .mp3 as 15:17 instead of 93:50, because the picker types a file by its name (audio/mpeg) and `getDurationViaAudioElement`'s blob address took that type (`typedAudioBlob` now corrects a type that disagrees with the content, not only a missing one: every blob address, on the website too); and Table's Type said MP3, from `track.filename` (`fileNameForContent`: a file coming in, by import or Replace, gives the track its name with the content's extension). Also from his test: Replace reading a long file now says so in the status bar (`replaceReadingFile`; "I thought it wasn't importing"). Tests: tests/extfix.js, tests/typedpath.js; the shell's `types_by_content` and `type_suffix_comes_off`.
- **About says which channel** (2.5.049, kr37, 19 Nov 2026: "Could our two About pages indicate what channel we're on?"). Settings → About and Help's About (one function, `aboutSectionInto`) link to the site of the page's own channel (`aboutShowSite`): in the desktop app, by the channel its shell reports (`p37_page_info`'s `channel`, kept in `desktopPageChannel`): "test" (a d build) → test.37player.com, with a line saying it's a test build (`aboutTestBuild`); anything else → 37player.com. The desktop app is looked at first: on Windows its page's own address is http://tauri.localhost. On the website, its own address (test.37player.com or 37player.com; "Running from the website …" already named it). A copy opened from a file: 37player.com. Tests: desktop, helpabout.
- **An iPhone's choices carried into its Home Screen app** (2.5.051 and `manifest.php`; kr37, 19 Nov 2026: he watched someone set up on an iPhone, choose the language, add 37 Player to the Home Screen, and be asked the language again there, and think something had gone wrong). The Home Screen app has storage of its own, apart from Safari's, so it starts as a new install. iOS makes it from the manifest, and opens it at the manifest's start address. So: (1) In a Safari tab on an iPhone or iPad, opening the Install guide (`openSetupGuide`) first makes sure the tab's address carries its choices (`setupCarryQuery`), reloading once to `37-player.html?lang=…&sk=…#setup` when it doesn't (the `#setup` reopens the guide). The choices: the language, and a shared library this tab is in: `sk` s with its join code `sc` and server `su` (only when not 37player.com's), or `sk` g with its Google Drive folder `sf`; then the device's name `sd` and the library's name `sn`. **Never a sign-in or the Google account**: the address stays in the Home Screen icon and lands in the web server's log. (2) A page whose address carries `lang` or `sk` points its manifest at `manifest.php` with the same query (the small script at the top); manifest.php is manifest.webmanifest with only `start_url` changed, each value checked (`su` must be https) and anything else dropped; `id` stays `./37-player.html`. Reloading first, rather than changing the manifest after the page has loaded, because it isn't known when iOS reads it; a real address rather than a `data:` manifest (as copies opened from a file use), because it isn't known whether iOS accepts one (kr37 chose manifest.php). (3) In the app, the choices are read once (`SETUP_CARRY`) and taken out of its address; they're used only while setup isn't done: the language without asking (`openSetupWizard`), then **Join your shared library here?** (`setupCarryJoin`, in an empty library): Join joins with the code and device name (a failure opens the join dialog with the code in it, the guide behind it), or for Google Drive signs in and the chooser picks that folder (`intent.folder` in `openDriveChooser`); Not now goes on to the guide. Later starts from the icon ignore them (setup done). A library that isn't shared still travels as a backup file (the guide's own step). To check on a real iPhone: that iOS keeps the query in the start address. Test: tests/carry.js (iPhone emulated in Chrome; manifest.php from the PHP server).
- **Library cleanup's missing recordings in groups that fold; Replace file in the dialog** (2.5.053; kr37, 19 Nov 2026, on his MacBook: the "can't be played" dialog's Library cleanup link led to a list of 200+, with no way to find the track; most were simply not here yet). `renderMissingGroups`, using `sharedTrackMissing`: **Missing, with nothing on its way** (not in a shared library, or waiting for this very device, which no longer has it); **Waiting to download** (the shared library has the file); **Waiting for “X” to send them**, one group per device (kr37's grouping and choice: the ones he expects to delete). Remove all in the first and the last, not in Waiting to download (those files are fine and on their way); the last says that removing them removes them from the shared library on every device. A group of 20 or fewer starts open; open or closed, each stays so while the page is redrawn (`missingGroupsOpen`). The plain "This track can't be played" dialog, and the shared one when this device was to send the file (`notHereLostHere`), have **Replace file** for that track (`wireMissingReplace`: at once, as the file picker needs the click; behind the PIN). Tests: tests/missreplace.js, tests/miss.js.
- **"Replace audio file" renamed by where it's offered** (2.5.054; kr37, 19 Nov 2026: the name made him think he had to find some raw audio without details; he had never used it before the WebM; weighing the options, he found the noun was what confused, and chose by context). Where the track's recording is missing or unusable (the "can't be played" dialogs, Library cleanup's missing, zero-length and damaged rows, and the messages that name the button): **Find or replace…** (`replaceMissingBtn`; he suggested "Find/Replace" for Library cleanup's rows, and the same words were used there so it reads alike everywhere a file is missing). Where it plays (Edit track details, its dialog's title, the list of tracks that couldn't be converted): **Replace file** (`replaceFileBtn`). The confirmation says what stays ("The track keeps its place in your playlists, and its details": the new file's own details are offered field by field where they differ) and no longer says it can't be undone: Undo has brought the old recording back since 2.5.012. Help topics 040, 125 and 128 follow.
- **What a track's file is, kept apart from its name** (2.5.059; kr37, 20 Nov 2026: "there are other oddball audio codecs"; agreed: a help topic, converting by choice, and Type showing what a file really is). `track.fileType` is the kind found inside (`sniffAudioFormat`'s extension: "webm", "opus", "m4a"…). Set at import and Replace (`fileNameAndKind`, `setTrackFile`), by a conversion or Standardize (the output's), and by Check library's new step 4 for a track without it (`recordTrackKind`; the summary counts those another kind than their names). **A field of its own, not a corrected `filename`:** the name stays the file's own (Table's File column; exports and a folder library's files are named from the details anyway), and nothing is renamed, so it's a record, not a repair (kr37's aversion to one-time fixes). `trackExt(tr)` is `fileType`, else the name's extension, and is what Table's Type, Library cleanup's convert list, the file-type counts, the format rules (`trackMatchesPolicy`), the split's label and converter input, "all alike" exports and Standardize's categories go by; the converter is handed the file under that extension (`trackSourceName`).
