In sync it's one of the audio group's fields (`SYNC_AUDIO_FIELDS`), so it travels with the file it describes; it's left out of what counts as a person's change (`SYNC_MEANING_TRACK_SKIP`, and `meaning_track` in **sync.php, which changed too**: the servers need it pulled), and Undo puts it back with the file (`UNDO_FILE_AUDIO`).
- **Convert, by choice; Help: Audio playback problems** (2.5.059, the same talk). **Convert** in Table's bar (above the table) and Browse's tracks bar (`libConvertDef`, behind the PIN) converts the selected tracks to the preferred format whatever the File conversion settings say (`convertTracks(ids, {asked: true})` → `askedConvertIds`), except those already in it, judged by what's inside (found then for a track without a record), with a line saying how many were left. The choice lasts through the run and a restart (`convertAsked`, kept as `asked` with the waiting list; `trackConvertWanted` is what the queue checks). With Don't touch my files the originals are kept without asking, as for files that can't be played. The help topic (help/140) says which kinds play everywhere (MP3, M4A, WAV) and which may not (WebM, Opus, Ogg, FLAC, AIFF, ALAC outside Safari), what that looks like (won't start; time running in silence; wrong length), Convert step by step from Table sorted by Type, and what Type shows; the plain "This track can't be played" dialog links to it (`openHelpTopic`), and topic 110's file-format part mentions Convert. Test: tests/convask.js (fixture `conv/festival.mp3`, WebM named .mp3, in make-fixtures.sh).
- **Every file playable everywhere** (2.5.060; kr37, 20 Nov 2026, from Rak-ma's case: WebMs she imports on the temple Mac sync to the breezeway Mac and the temple's iPad, which may not play them; and his ranking of 37 Player's purposes: first a player that helps Centers run pujas and retreats, then a player for individuals with shared libraries for their own devices). Only MP3 and AAC in M4A play on every device (`UNIVERSAL_KINDS`; kinds by extension in `FILE_KINDS`, via `trackExt`, `fileKindOfExt`; a track of no known kind is left alone). (1) **The recommended setting** (`reduce`, now "Make every file playable everywhere") converts every other kind as it comes in: WAV, AIFF, FLAC, WebM, Opus, Ogg, WMA and the rest, an allow-list rather than a list of bad kinds, so a kind never met before is converted too (his idea). A library that never changed the setting follows it; nothing already in it converts by itself (only imports, splits and copies do), but Library cleanup's convert list now shows them. (2) **Choose the rules** has the kinds kept as they are instead of "Uncompressed" and "Less widely playable types" (`rules.keep`); the preferred format's kind is always kept (ticked, fixed) and saved with the list, so changing the format later doesn't start converting it. A library on the rules before keeps what its checkboxes meant (formatPolicy's migration: other kinds kept, as they were). (3) **A shared library holds only those kinds, whatever its settings** (`trackMatchesPolicy`: `sharedConfig() || joiningShared`): an import or a copy into it (`applyIncomingCopies` → `convertAfterImport`) converts the rest. Receiving devices aren't asked anything: they can't tell whether they can play a file (the Mac mini says yes to WebM Opus and plays silence), several devices converting the same album would each send a new version, and they're often playback machines nobody watches; the importing device decides, where the person knows the files. (4) **Don't touch my files is a way to start, not a rule for ever** (kr37): after an import with other kinds, `askAboutKeptKinds` offers Convert (this once) or Leave as they are, with "Convert such files from now on" (switches to the recommended setting) and "Don't ask again in this library" (`formatPolicy.noAsk`). The originals are kept under that setting anyway. (5) **The sharing check** (`shareCheckConvert`, in the join, when this device's tracks will go up): its tracks of other kinds, offered for converting first ("Convert, then share" / "Share as they are"); the join waits for the conversions, so each file goes up once, already converted. Treating every library as if it might be shared would ask people who never share at every import; this asks once, when the person is there deciding. (6) **FLAC retired as a preferred format** (not offered; a library that chose it keeps it, but a shared one converts to Standard: `preferredFormat`). FLAC doesn't play everywhere, so converting to it for playability made no sense, and a FLAC library would "convert FLAC to FLAC" by a keep list (it can't: the preferred format's kind is always kept). (7) **Lossless files at the highest quality**: an option, off (kr37: "I don't want a bunch of 320k MP3s when I bring in WAV files"; it's to appease the few who'd mind, such as a festival's remastered FLACs): `formatPolicy.losslessHigh`, `losslessTarget` in `converterConvert` (the library's own conversions only, by the probe's codec: PCM, FLAC, ALAC…), MP3 320k or AAC 256k. Considered and not done: playing the original on the importing device while the shared library has the converted file (two files per track to keep in step through splits, Replace, Undo, loudness and waveforms; the one device that differs would be the one a person tests on; nothing anyone would hear). (8) **Kept originals readable** in a folder library: `originals/Artist/Album/01 Title.ext` (`recordingNameOpts`: an `orig:` key gets the track's path under originals/ instead of music/), apart from music/ so it holds only what's played. renameRecordings moves those kept before into it, as it moved an older library's recordings out of audio/, and moves them with the track's details. Still on that device only. The folder's README says so. Help 110 and 140; strings in nine languages. Test: tests/convkeep.js.
- **Row buttons beside Remove have a light outline** (2.5.055; kr37, 19 Nov 2026, from a screenshot of Library cleanup: "Find or replace…", a ghost button, read as plain text next to the rust-outlined Remove from library; he wanted the two of equal weight). `.btn.outline`: transparent, the dim text colour's outline (as the top bar's Install), the faint text colour's in the light theme, where the dim one looked heavier than the rust. Used for Library cleanup's row buttons (Find or replace…, and Convert in the empty-file rows) and the couldn't-convert list's Replace file. A quiet button beside a Remove uses it from now on.
- **Saving a big library is quick** (2.2.035, 12 Nov 2026; kr37: Save in the Edit dialog took seconds to respond, and typing in Table's search lagged, on the T470 while imports and syncing ran). Saves run one after another (`saveChain`), imports and syncing save often, and each save held up the page: measured with 2,500 tracks on a fast computer, a save that changed one track took 0.59 s and one that changed nothing 0.5–0.8 s (several times that on the T470). Two thirds of it was checks, not the write: `libraryHashOf` turned the whole library into text (33 MB, almost all waveforms: 720 full-precision numbers a track) to see whether anything changed, and `writeRootIfCurrent` read the whole stored library back (IndexedDB) or parsed all of root.json (a folder) only to compare its revision. Now: a waveform counts in the hash by a fingerprint made once per array (`peaksPrint`, a WeakMap: waveforms are only ever replaced by new arrays, never changed in place); IndexedDB keeps the revision in a small record of its own, `rootRev`, written in the same transaction as `root` by every path that writes it (`keepRootRev`: putNow, update, del, clearState, writeRootIfCurrent) and trusted only when it equals the revision expected, else `root` is read as before; a folder's root.json is written `{"key":"root","rev":N,"value":…}` so `rootRevQuick` reads the revision from its first 64 bytes, and a root.json an older version wrote (revision last) is read whole, as before. After: one track changed 0.2 s (the write itself), nothing changed 4–7 ms. Known gap, only while two windows run different versions: a window of an older version saves `root` without `rootRev`, and a window of this version that hasn't yet reloaded from that save's broadcast would not see it. And (2.2.036) Library's search (Browse and Table) redraws once typing pauses (150 ms; emptying the box redraws at once): a redraw with 2,500 tracks is 30–45 ms on a fast computer, and one per letter held up the letters themselves on the T470. Tested in `savefast.js`. Not done: rounding stored waveforms (would shrink the write and the shared library's document, but changes every track once, so every shared library would sync everything again).
- **An import reads every kind of MP3 tag** (2.2.034, 12 Nov 2026; Rak-ma imported an album, probably Medicine Buddha prayers in a folder, and only the first track got its artwork, artist and album; no file to look at). The import had its own ID3 reader (`parseId3Tags`), which knew only plain v2.3/v2.4 frames: a v2.2 tag (older iTunes rips) or one with an extended header came back empty, a v2.4 frame with a data length indicator came back with junk in front, an artist only in TPE2 (album artist) was missed, and so was anything in ID3v1 once ID3v2 had a title. An album with track 1 retagged in another program and the rest left as ripped would come in just as she saw. Now it reads through the tag writer's `parseId3Block` (with `id3TextValues`, `parseId3Picture`): v2.2 to v2.4, unsynchronisation, extended headers, v2.4 frame flags and plain-sized v2.4 frames; TPE2 when TPE1 is empty; the front cover when there are several pictures; then ID3v1 (with v1.1's track number) for any field still empty. The old helpers (`readId3Int`, `decodeId3Text`, `parseApicFrame`) are gone. `tests/id3read.js` builds one file per case; the old reader got 6 of its 8 wrong. Not known yet whether this was her case: her files would tell.
- **A renamed album no longer leaves a copy of itself behind** (2.2.033, 12 Nov 2026; kr37 on the T470 in Chrome: each rename of an album, while one of its tracks played, left the whole album in the old folder too, and a third rename a third folder). Not a failed move: an edit writes the new details into each file's tags too (`rewriteFileTagsIfPossible`, unless Don't touch my files), and in a folder library that writes each recording out whole under its new name (`writeRecord`); the old file went on the waiting list for the day's grace, deleted only at the next opening of the library, and the T470 stays open for days. Now `noteSuperseded`: a readable recording replaced by a new version of the same track is deleted about a minute later (`FILES_SUPERSEDED_MS`, `dropSuperseded`), once no player here holds it (`canMove`; a playing one waits and is tried again), and not if the record points at it again (an Undo); empty album and artist folders go with it. A deleted track's recording still waits its day. And the tidy runs every six hours while the library stays open (`FILES_TIDY_EVERY_MS`), not only at opening. Known gap: another window playing the old version of that same track loses it (two windows on one library, rare). Old copies already left on a disk go at the first tidy after their day. Tested in `folderlib.js`.
- **From kr37's install in Chrome on the T470** (2.2.030, 12 Nov 2026). Chrome and Edge install by moving the open tab into the app's window, without reloading it, so the guide still offered to install and Install stayed in the top bar. Now the display mode is followed (`matchMedia` change listeners for standalone, fullscreen, minimal-ui and window-controls-overlay, and `appinstalled`): `setupDisplayModeChanged` updates the top bar and redraws an open guide (`setupGuideRedraw`; its environment is read at every draw, and the carry-on flag is cleared once it's the app). A done step's ✓ is dark, bold and larger, and only the step's text is faded; the step text is 15px. **Questions' buttons** (`askChoice`, everywhere): one column at the right, as wide as the widest button (a grid of `max-content`), not the whole dialog; ghost choices get a light outline there so each reads as a button. Test: setup.js (a tab becoming the app while the guide is open; screenshots shots/setup-done.png, setup-welcome.png). The full suite (110 tests) passed on 2.2.029 once conv.js and conv2.js waited for the can't-be-played warning, which since 2.2.028 opens a moment later, after "Import finished".
- **Small fixes from kr37's to-do list** (2.2.029, 12 Nov 2026). **A recording used in place that's gone** no longer marks its whole folder as not connected: `placeMarkMissing` asks whether the folder itself can be reached, and only then is it "On a drive that isn't connected"; otherwise **Its recording isn't in its folder** says it was moved, renamed or deleted and how to put it right (put it back, or Replace audio file). Converting such a track gives `convertPlaceGone`, not "its audio isn't on this device yet". **A folder import's summary** says "created from the playlist files" for playlists from .m3u8 (and other playlist files), and "mirroring the folder structure" only for ones made from folders (`playlistsFromFiles`). **The Google Drive step of joining** keeps its message ("checking…", errors) on the buttons' line, so the dialog doesn't jump. **The can't-be-played dialogs** (`showAudioMissing`'s last case, `showCantPlay`) have a **Library cleanup** button (`wireOpenCleanup`, behind the PIN). **The ⚙ button closes Settings** when it's open, as ? does Help. **Even out loudness starts as By album** for a new install or library (`firstRunDone`, only when unset and not in a shared library, whose own setting comes with it). **The glow** (▶, Import / Export, display) only for a device's first library. The M4A repair notice waits its turn too (2.2.028's queue). Found already done: "tick" is gone from the English strings and Help; the home page's Mac line says right-click → Open, twice.
- **Joining with plain files keeps this device's copies** (2.2.025, the plan's item 5; `sharedAdoptSameFiles`). A device given the recordings some other way (the music folder by USB stick, a Portable export, its files imported afresh) has new IDs for everything; joining matched them by name, album, artist and length and then removed this device's copy and downloaded the shared one (about 50 GB for a 50 GB library). Now, before the fold, for every choice (Use the shared library here, Combine, Only what I choose), each match is compared with the shared library's file: same size, same first and last 64 KB (`sharedServerHead`, six at a time, a progress bar). Where they agree, the audio record moves to the shared ID (`libStore.rekey`: the folder store rewrites only the small .json, so the recording stays where it is; the IDB store moves the record in one transaction), the track becomes the shared library's (its details and revision; this device's waveform kept), playlists here point at it, and its artwork is queued to download (this device's may differ). Two tracks here matching one shared track: one takes the ID, the other folds into it. Matches that differ, or can't be compared (not on the server yet, a read failing, the list unavailable), go on as before. The join question's download sizes for Use the shared library here no longer count matches (as Combine's already didn't). Tests: dup.js (F's copies kept under the shared IDs, no whole file downloaded; it downloaded two before), folderlib.js (rekey in a folder library).
- **Setting up 37 Player: Try it out / Install** (2.2.023; kr37's design, 12 Nov 2026, in place of the welcome wizard Claude had drafted, which nudged people step by step from wherever they started). The first run asks the language, then **Try it out** or **Install** (Install highlighted); the home page offers the same (`37-player.html#try`, `#setup`, read once by `SETUP_HASH` and taken out of the address). "Join a shared library?" is no longer the welcome's question (it's in Install's steps and Settings); the desktop app keeps it, being installed already. An installed app (Home Screen, Chrome app) goes straight to the guide. **Try it out** (`startTrial`, `backupInfo.trial`): quiet for two days or until the fifth import (`trialQuiet`, folded into `quickJobActive`: the backup button amber, no daily prompt, no Heads-up); on a computer in Safari/Firefox/Brave-without-folders one suggestion first (Chrome or Edge, copy the address `#try`; Safari tabs: "for testing only"). Each import counts (`trialCountImport`, from `headsUpBeforeImport`); the fifth, or a start after two days (`setupOnStart`), ends it once with the guide as **Done trying it out?** (`endTrialAndAsk`; closing it lets the import go ahead; the top bar's Install stays). **Install** (`openSetupGuide`, a wide dialog in the Heads-up's step style): **Mac/Windows: the desktop app only** (kr37: with whatever guidance gets people past the warnings): a direct download for this OS (`desktopDownloadUrl`, the test site's builds from desktop-latest-dev), the Gatekeeper and SmartScreen steps (unsigned builds), and **I can't install apps on this computer** → the Chrome/Edge way. **Other computers** (`deviceOs`: Linux, ChromeOS) or that way: from Safari/Firefox only "open it in Chrome or Edge" (copy the address `#setup`, Get Chrome / Get Edge, or **Just try it out here**, a trial); in Chrome/Edge: install (the browser's prompt, else the menu's words), then **Keep the library in a folder** (only in the installed app), then a shared library ("works well for multiple devices", kr37's wording). **iPad/iPhone tab:** the Home Screen steps, then Install again there; **Home Screen app / Android:** install (Android), then **Do you use Google Drive?** (Yes: the join dialog) or **No**: backups by hand, kept few (the newest full one and its changes). What's here is carried along (`carry`): a joined library is joined again there (its code, or Google Drive); otherwise a .37p to import (**Save a backup file**). After the browser's own install, or a move to a folder (which reloads), the guide carries on (`SETUP_CONTINUE_KEY`, read in the installed app only). An **Install** button sits in the top bar wherever 37 Player isn't installed (`updateInstallButton`; an outlined pill, so it reads as a button). **From kr37's first try in Edge on the HP (12 Nov, 2.2.026):** steps are labelled "Step 1" in an unfilled, rounded box (`stepBox`; the dark number on an amber circle was nearly unreadable and didn't say it was a step), in the Heads-up too; the desktop app "doesn't have to repeatedly prompt for permissions"; the Windows warning step follows what Edge really shows: in its downloads, … → Keep, then in "Make sure you trust…" the arrow ▾ inside the black Delete button → Keep anyway, then at opening More info → Run anyway. The old Heads-up stays for libraries set up before and is otherwise no longer reached. Tests: setup.js; the hooks answer the welcome as "No, just start" did (`__autoTry`) unless a test sets `__welcomeOn`; welcome.js tests the Heads-up that's left.
- **Section 2 of the library/backups plan, first part** (2.2.022). **"Your library is ordinary files (this is good!)"** (kr37, 12 Nov: say that it's a good thing; `maybeFilesNotice`, from `checkBackupState`, so after every save): once per library (`backupInfo.filesNoticeAt`), a library kept as files (desktop app, or a folder in Chrome/Edge) that has tracks and isn't in a shared library is told where it is (the desktop app's full path; in a browser only the folder's name is known) and that 37 Player makes no second copy, so: include the folder in the computer's backup, or join a shared library (button). Waits while a dialog or an operation is showing. Off in tests (`__noHeadsUp`), like the Heads-up. **A new library in Settings** (Chrome/Edge, `canOpenLibraryFolder`): "Where should this library be kept?", **In a folder** checked already (kr37, 11 Nov: suggested, not forced), or **In this browser's storage**. The folder is picked at the dialog's button (the browser needs the click); `newLibraryFolderIn` applies Keep it in a folder…'s rules (an empty folder named for the library is used; another empty one asks; otherwise a folder of the library's name inside; never one holding a library, which it offers to open, nor one with other things in it); `createLibrary(name, join, {parent})` writes the marker and README and the entry's `folder` before the library first opens, so it starts there (nothing to copy). A join made this way downloads into the folder. Tests: folderlib.js; libdel.js and drive.js choose browser storage.
- **A copied library on another device** (2.2.021, the plan's item 3; `installId`, `sharedDeviceUnconfirmed`, `askSharedDeviceCopy`): the join (with the device name) is in the library's settings, so a copied folder arrives joined, under the original's name, and the name is how devices tell each other apart ("waiting for X", kept-both copies, logs, recovering a file the shared library waits for from "this" device). Each install has an ID, localStorage `37player.install` (in the desktop app's device.json too, `DEVICE_MIRROR_KEYS`; never in a library), and the join lists the installs confirmed as this device, `cfg.installs` (set when joining; a join from before 2.2.021 takes the first install that opens it). Opened by an install not on the list: no sync, transfers or sending (`runSharedSync`, `sharedDownloadPending`, `sharedUploadInBackground` return), and **Is this another device?** after the start's own questions: **Another device: use this name** (not empty, not the old name in any capitals; the list becomes just this install, `sharedLastDevice` takes the name) or **This is still “X”** (this install joins the list, last 10 kept: another browser or site on the same computer, a reinstall, the app's data cleared). Closed unanswered: Settings → Shared library says it isn't syncing until then, and Sync now asks again. Expected consequence: the desktop app's two-stage wipe test (webview storage, then its data folder) now asks once after the second stage, since the install ID went with the data folder; "This is still" answers it. There is still no way to rename a device later in Settings. Test: copied.js.
- **Upload size limit:** Apache 2.4.54+ refuses request bodies over 1 GiB by default (`LimitRequestBody`), answering 413. A 1.2 GB WAV hit it. Both sites' vhosts on the droplet have `LimitRequestBody 0` (confirmed 29 Oct 2026; see SYNC-SETUP.md). The app now stops resending a refused file until "Sync now" and says so in the status (1.3.045). A later improvement: send big files in pieces (resumable).
- **Mobile data:** treated like any other connection, because library integrity matters more. Only the join dialog warns about the size.

## Chat branch toolbar and transport controls (2.2.003–2.2.008, 9–10 Nov 2026; ChatGPT with kr37, merged into dev as 2.2.009)

- Playing's context toolbar has a centered chevron to collapse/show only the option row. Transport, waveform, volume and playlist remain visible; the setting survives redraws and is kept per browser in localStorage (`37player.playingToolbarCollapsed`). Colours follow existing CSS variables, including high contrast.
- Previous/next controls in Playing, Library and the splitter share our own inline SVG (`ICON_SKIP_BACK` / `ICON_SKIP_FORWARD`), the same on every device instead of the platform's ⏮ ⏭ glyphs (kr37's to-do: "something of our own"). 2.2.009 (Claude, reviewing the branch): a bar and one triangle, the usual sign for previous/next track, where the branch had two triangles (the sign for rewind/fast-forward). The toolbar chevron's label is translated (`playingToolbarShow` / `playingToolbarHide`).

## Non-obvious decisions

- **Translated Playing controls** (2.2.008): the album recommendation and all new browsing/switching labels and explanations are supplied in Spanish, French, Portuguese, German, Chinese, Vietnamese, Greek and Italian, rather than falling back to English.

- **More room for browsed tracks** (2.2.007, kr37): overlay title/count and Use this playlist share a header, Browsing joins the count, and the replacement hint sits under Play from here. On viewports up to 850px high, browsing also reduces live-player padding and waveform height while keeping its transport, names, volume and seekbar. Closing restores normal sizing. The overlay follows changes in the live header height. Touch targets stay full-sized; the track list gets the recovered space.

- **Explicit actions while browsing another playlist** (2.2.006, kr37): the Playing overlay is labelled Browsing and names the playlist that remains active. Use this playlist makes the browsed playlist active at its first item without starting it (the existing confirmation still protects ongoing playback). Play from here starts the selected item at the waveform cursor, with a replacement warning during playback. Close is labelled. Title click/double-click, row double-click, Enter, Escape and outside-click shortcuts remain unchanged. The aim is to expose the two decisions without introducing a separate queue or requiring users to discover gestures.

- **Playing loudness picker** (2.2.005, kr37): a compact dropdown replaces the three-position chooser. Labelled Loudness, it offers By album (recommended), Off, By track, with just the short name shown when closed. Album leveling preserves an album's intended quiet/loud differences while evening out different albums, useful for practices combining prayers from several albums. Existing selections and gain behavior stay the same. The current choice's tooltip explains it; the native picker supports keyboard and touch, with existing theme variables.

- **A backup folder never loses a file for a track that's still in the library** (1.4.150, `runFolderBackupHere`, `backupFileFor`). Before, a track went on the backup's keep list only if its audio was in IndexedDB at that moment, and every other file in `audio/` and `artwork/` was deleted, while meta.json still listed the track. So a full disk, a restore whose writes failed, or storage the browser threw away while the track list came back from the shared library made each backup empty the folder of exactly what it was there to keep. That fits kr37's T470 (1 Nov 2026): the backup's library had 2,587 tracks and audio for 1. Now a track with no audio (or artwork) here keeps the folder's file and its `audioRevs` entry; only a track leaving the library frees its file. The playback log says how many were kept. Test: tests/bkkeep.js (fails on 1.4.149).
- **Colours and controls** (1.4.149; designed with ChatGPT on 2–3 Nov 2026 in the `chat` branch, 1.4.148.1–12, merged and tidied by Claude). One vocabulary everywhere: **green** is what's playing now (the row, its playlist's name in both trees, the bars icon `ICON_PLAYING`); **amber** is what's selected and the Jump to target (its outline runs round the start waveform too, `has-start-wave`); **brown** (`--active-bg`, the light theme's primary-button brown) is the chosen option in a toolbar or segmented group, the page tabs (Library's Browse | Table is a `.mode-tabs` too) and Play (a rounded square, white triangle: Playing's transport, Edit Playlists' preview, and since 1.4.164 the overlay of another playlist on Playing and the splitter's Play buttons, which had kept the old amber circle; kr37 asked for both); **muted grey with a ✓** is already played. A silence shows a clock, a stop a square in the row's own text colour (red looked pressable), both in the artwork column. Play is a triangle, disclosure a chevron, never the other way round: folders in the trees have no triangle at all (kr37: too many icons in a row; the line down from an open folder shows its contents), Settings' sections a big chevron and its logs a small one (`.settings-log`). Since 1.4.167 (kr37) every other `<details>` in dialogs and pages has the logs' chevron and the line down the left of what opens (one generic rule, `details:not(.sh-more):not(.settings-log)`), and the trees of Choose what's on this device (`.ch-twist`) and the shared-library review (`.rv-chev`) have the chevron instead of ▸/▶, with a faint line down from each open folder or album like the playlist trees' `.tree-line` (a `::before` per row, one line per level, from the row's `--d`). Number fields in dialogs have full-height up/down buttons (`timeSpinnerHtml`): silences' minutes and seconds, and since 1.4.167 the track details' track number and volume (half-decibel steps, −30 to +30; `compact`, as tall as the text fields beside them). Library: Browse warm, Table's data cool (`--lib-table-*`), the toolbar and preview player warm in both. The CSS is one section at the end of the style sheet, after the screens' own rules on purpose; its colours are variables, set on `body.theme-light` (and More contrast) with the dark value as each `var()`'s fallback, since a `:root` variable naming `var(--line)` would miss More contrast's `--line`. A new screen copies Playing's buttons rather than inventing its own. Icons are inline SVG in the Lucide/Feather style (THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md).
- **Stop and silence names** (1.4.149; `nameComboHtml`, `attachNameSuggestions`): one 44px field, a free-text name on the left and a tan chevron (56px) on the right that lists the library's earlier names, one outer border and no line between. Typing a new name is always allowed. On touch screens the list sits under the field. Open/close is on click (not mousedown), with `aria-expanded` and listbox roles.
- **Minutes and Seconds** in Insert/Edit Silence (1.4.149; `timeSpinnerHtml`, `attachTimeSpinners`): 44px like the name, with our own full-height up/down buttons, since phones' native arrows are tiny or missing. A tap is ±1; held 420 ms it repeats every 95 ms until released, moved off, cancelled, the window loses focus or the dialog closes, and the click after a hold is swallowed so it adds nothing. Each step sends `input`, so the automatic name still follows. Seconds 0–59. Scaling dialog text with the Display size was tried (1.4.148.8) and set aside.
- **Send feedback** (1.4.136, row 14). Help (under the topics) and Settings → About: **Send feedback…** (`openFeedbackDialog`, only on the website). A message, an optional reply address (remembered per device, `feedbackReply`), and a box per log (this device's playback and sync logs, as `logExportText` makes them), posted to `feedback.php`: mailed only to rinzin@kr37.net through Postfix (`mail()`; subject "37-Player: <first line>", logs attached; the reply address only as Reply-To), kept in DATA_DIR/feedback/<date>-<id>/, and filed in `kr37/37player-reports` (reports/<date>-<id>/) once DATA_DIR/feedback-github-token exists. Same origin only; 6 an hour and 20 a day per address (hashed); 3 MB. What a device sent is listed under the form (localStorage `37player.feedbackSent`). Setup: SYNC-SETUP.md, Feedback. Test: tests/feedback.js. **Other devices' logs** (1.4.137, row 15): after each sync round, at most every half hour and not in the first two minutes after opening (`sharedSendLogs`, localStorage `37player.logsSentAt`, per library), a device sends its playback and sync logs to the shared library, replacing its last copy: sync.php `op=log` (PUT/GET) and `op=logs` (list), kept in `<library>/logs/`, pruned after 90 days unsent by cleanup; on Drive a "logs" folder made the first time (`lg` in the library folder's appProperties). Named `<device>-<hash>-<kind>.txt` (`logFileStem`). Only to a sync.php ≥ 1.4.137 (SYNC_VERSION is now 1.4.137) or Drive. Send feedback lists the other devices' logs, ticked when from the last three days (`sharedOtherLogs`). Tests: tests/sharedlogs.js, drive.js.
- **A splitter piece played again** (1.4.135, row 18, seen on kr37's iPad): playing a piece a second time (the last one above all) did nothing, or played from the start of the track with the cursor stopping at the mark. Safari seeks in its own time, and `play()` on an element still counting as "ended" starts from 0. Now `splitPlayRange` waits for `seeked` (1.5 s at most) before `play()`, sets what to stop at again after it, a later press wins (`splitPlaySeq`); a range stops 80 ms short of the track's end so the element never reaches "ended"; and a start somewhere else is put right (`spl.playFrom`). The stop is checked on a 20 ms timer as well as each drawn frame (`splitCheckStop`, `splitStopWatch`): in the headless test, frames stopped coming and the end was missed. Checked in Chromium (tests/splreplay.js); the iPad itself still to try.
- **Heads-up for an installed app** (1.4.134, row 13). When the app is already installed (a Home Screen app, say), has no folder backups, and keeping the library safe is the only step left, the Heads-up no longer says "Do these first" (backing up by hand can't come before the first import): "Good job installing 37 Player", why a shared library is the set-and-forget way on iPads and iPhones (or "on this device"), then two plain boxes: a shared library, with Join, and backing up by hand once there's something to keep (`installedOnly` in `openHeadsUp`). Playing's "Even out loudness:" label is hidden at 1100px and under, where the bar would wrap (an iPad).
- **A new Drive library that's backed out of** (1.4.134, row 26): if joining a library just made on Google Drive is cancelled or fails before anything is saved in it, its folders go to the Drive's trash (`driveRemoveIfUnused`: only when all four of its folders are empty). The last question (how much will be sent) says so (2.0.002, kr37, 7 Nov 2026, from the HP: someone on mobile data should know it's been set up): "“X” is set up on your Google Drive … You can cancel now: it's taken off your Drive again, and you can set it up later" (`driveMadeSizeHint`, `opts.justMade`). 2.0.001 kept a cancelled one on the Drive instead; kr37 chose this, the cleanest, in case another shared library is chosen later. Test: tests/drive.js.
- **Folder lines and albums into a playlist** (1.4.130, rows 12 and 31 of the 30 Oct list). An open folder's contents (its add rows too) hang from a thin line in the folder colour, one per open folder, on Playing and in Edit Playlists' columns A and C (`addTreeLine`: placed after layout: on Playing under the folder icon; in Edit Playlists, whose rows indent less than its icon sits (a line there ran through the names), in the gap between the ⠿ handle and the triangle (kr37, 1.4.131); positions are divided by the page's zoom, since the size setting zooms). Column C's Albums can be dragged into column B: the whole album, in column D's order (`albumTracksInOrder`), always a copy. Mouse only so far: touch drags from C aren't wired. On Playing, folders, open or closed, have the same spacing above and below as playlists (1.4.133; a wider gap above folders made two playlists above one look like one wrapped title): the lines show the structure. **Albums are name + artist** (1.4.131, kr37): `albumLevelKey` is album and artist, so By album evens two "Volume 1"s by different artists separately; column C lists such a name once per artist, with the artist in small text under it (`getAlbumGroups`: key name + "\u0001" + artist, `byArtist`; a name with one artist keeps the plain name as its key). Library's Albums still group by name only, so their By album figure can read "+3 / −1 dB" (`albumLevelText`). Test: tests/edtree.js.
- **Loudness on Playing** (1.4.128, rows 8, 10, 11 of the 30 Oct list). Playing's bar (1.4.129, kr37's layout): **Display:** Wrap, Album, Loudness as icons only (`iconOnly`; device setting `showPlayLoudness`) | **Even out loudness:** By album / Off / By track (group labels: `{group: …}` in `fillCtxBar`, hidden at 600px and under) (`ctxSeg`, `setLevelMode`; the kind is still remembered in `levelKind` while Off) | Playback. The old Even out on/off button is gone. Rows (`fillPlayLoudCell`, whole dB, `fmtDbInt`): with Loudness on, what evening out does in the mode chosen (0 when Off, … when not measured); a track's own volume (✎, `volumeDb`) always, in bold, Loudness on or off. Library → Browse's Albums show what By album does to each album (`albumLevelDb`) while Library's Loudness is on. Test: tests/playloud.js.
- **Copy to another library** (1.4.126, kr37's design; `openCopyToLibraryDialog`, `copyToLibrary`, `applyIncomingCopies`). Import/Export → Copy to another library, shown only with more than one library: Export's "what" list (`exportChoices`) and a library list that starts at "Choose a library…" every time (kr37: helps avoid sending to the wrong one). Wanted from wherever you are, through Import/Export, not a choose-and-copy screen (Borrow from a library, by a shared link, is planned: see Open / later, 31 Oct 2026). Tracks keep their IDs: a track the other library already has is left exactly as it is there, audio untouched; a playlist with the same ID is brought up to date wherever it now sits; playlists go into the same folder path by folder names, made where missing; a different playlist of the same name there gets "(library)" added. One-time: nothing stays linked. **How:** the copying window writes only the new tracks' audio and artwork into the other library's database (`openDbNamed`, `dbOnce`), then appends an entry to its state record `incomingCopies`; it never writes another library's library record. The other library applies it with its own code: at once in a window that has it open (`37player-libraries` message `incoming`), else at its next start (from `init`), under a lock, taking the record out in the same transaction that reads it. So Undo (the copy is one step), shared-library sync (`audioFrom` set to this device when shared) and backups treat it like anything added there. A stopped or failed copy deletes what it wrote. Not copied: kept originals, analysis attempts, `addedAt` (stamped on arrival). **Interruptions** (1.4.129): while copying (`copyRunning`), a refresh or closing the window asks first (beforeunload); switching libraries lists the copy among what would stop, and Switch anyway stops it and waits while it takes back what it wrote; the done dialog (with Open “…”) comes only after the copy has been handed over. If the page dies anyway, the other library tidies up: the copy notes the tracks whose files it's about to write (`copyPartial` in that library, removed in the same transaction that hands the copy over) while holding the lock `37player-copyinto~<id>`; a note found with nobody holding the lock is a dead copy, and `cleanUpDeadCopy` deletes those files unless they've become tracks there. Test: tests/copylib.js.
- **The splitter's average level** (1.4.127, row 17 of the 30 Oct list): with the detailed waveform, both views draw the peaks fainter and each moment's average level (`splitLevelMean`, the 5 ms means `splitFineAnalysis` already kept) as a solid core inside them. A heavily limited master has peaks at full scale nearly everywhere (that's what full height means with "Quiet sounds larger" off); the average still shows its shape. No extra analysis.
- **The icon** (1.4.122): the five-bar icon used since September, its bars exactly where they were (47 of 512 wide), on black, with a centre line edge to edge: the two left bars and their half of the line gold (#FFC233), the right ones blue (#3FA9FF), and the middle bar a red bar (#FF4040) of the same width from top to bottom. Red stays faint in a 16-px Firefox tab on kr37's KDE desktop whatever we tried (#E5332A, #FF1F1F; white in 1.4.121 showed better); he chose to keep red for now (1.4.122). At 16, 32 and 48 px everything is drawn on whole pixels (the red bar at least 2 px, gold and blue 1 px at 16 so the gaps stay, the centre line a pixel thicker than it would scale to): in a Firefox tab a scaled-down icon blended the thin red into the black until it all but vanished (red is dark to the eye; gold and blue survive). Two tab icons, 16 and 32, with `sizes`, so the browser needn't scale. Drawn in `art/37player-icon.svg` (the large design; small sizes are snapped by the script that renders them) and rendered: 512 and 192 in both manifests (the file and the copy inside the page), 180 for `apple-touch-icon`, 192 and a 16/32/48 .ico for the homepage. Tried and dropped the same day: a traced waveform picture (1.4.117, too faint in a tab), thin bars with a thin red line (1.4.118), and this design with a darker red and no centre line (1.4.119).

- **Re-encode target is AAC 192 kbps, 44.1 kHz, stereo, `.m4a`.** It re-encodes only uncompressed audio, codecs a browser can't play, and odd channel counts or sample rates. An ordinary 192k MP3 is never touched.
- **Files a browser can't play are kept, never thrown away** (1.3.048). Import stores the original and marks its track `needsConvert` with the format's name ("Windows Media Audio (WMA)", "Apple Lossless (ALAC)", "AIFF-C (ima4)"; `describeFormatProblem`), so playlists keep their place for it and nothing is lost. It's part of the audio group in sync, so every device shows it, and whichever device later converts the file clears it. Marked like a missing track (bold, with the reason under it; `trackCantPlay`, `markRowNotHere`), and Playing says how many in a playlist can't play. Library cleanup → Tracks that can't be played → **Check again** (`recheckStoredAudio`) re-reads stored files with today's decoders: an AIFF an older version left at 0:00 comes back as WAV. Only a playlist file posing as audio is still left out.
- **The converter is ffmpeg.wasm** (1.3.049): `scripts/get_ffmpeg.sh` puts ffmpeg-core.js and .wasm (0.12.10 without SIMD since 1.4.166, about 26 MB) in the site's `ffmpeg/` (not in git; CORS via its .htaccess). A device downloads them once, when something first needs converting (or from Library cleanup → Converter), into IndexedDB's state store (record `converter`, kept when the library is replaced), so it works offline and in downloaded copies (which fetch from 37player.com). It runs in a worker built from inline source (`converterWorkerMain`), reading the input in place (WORKERFS). **Convert** (after an import, on Playing's banner, in Library cleanup) turns a track marked `needsConvert`, or a 0:00 track, into MP3 192k: channels kept, sample rate kept unless MP3 can't hold it, tags and picture carried, the library's details written in after (`applyConvertedAudio`). One queue, one window at a time (lock `37player-convert`); it waits while anything plays in any 37 Player window on the computer (each window says when it plays, BroadcastChannel `37player-playing`; `anyWindowPlaying`, also used by Standardize and background analysis), and a conversion under way when playback starts is dropped and done again after. Standardize's "Only when nothing is playing" switch turns this off for converting too.
  - **Without SIMD, everywhere (1.4.166; kr37, 5 Nov 2026):** ffmpeg.wasm 0.12.10 as published is built with WebAssembly SIMD (`-msimd128`), and a webview without SIMD refuses the whole file ("WebAssembly.Module doesn't parse … can't get Function local's type"). The desktop app on macOS 12 uses Monterey's own WebKit, which predates SIMD even with Safari 17.6 installed (on older macOS, Safari keeps its newer WebKit to itself, in StagedFrameworks); Safari before 16.4 is the same. `.github/workflows/ffmpeg-nosimd.yml` (branch `ffmpeg-nosimd`) builds the same source (ffmpeg.wasm commit 71aa99d, the core 0.12.10 release) with `-msimd128` and libwebp's SIMD off, and publishes it as the pre-release `ffmpeg-core-0.12.10-nosimd` (wasm 25.9 MB against 32.2 MB). Checked: valid without SIMD; the same ffmpeg 5.1.4 with the same decoders, encoders, formats and filters; every test conversion byte-for-byte the same as the standard build's. Speed, 10-minute stereo file, Chrome on Linux, median of three, time against the standard build: WMA→MP3 1.10×, ALAC→MP3 1.03×, FLAC→MP3 1.04×, WAV→MP3 1.05×, WAV→AAC 1.25×, WAV→FLAC 1.00×. kr37 chose this build for every device over two builds chosen by a SIMD check (one file, smaller, nothing to detect). `scripts/get_ffmpeg.sh` fetches it from the release and checks its SHA-256 sums; `ffmpeg/VERSION` reads `0.12.10-nosimd`. Devices that already keep the standard build go on using it (same output; nothing re-downloads everywhere). A stored converter that won't compile (`CompileError`) is fetched again from the server, once a session (`converterRefetched` in `converterStart`): the Mac mini's desktop app had kept the SIMD build. Test: convbroken.js.
- **Unplayable files are converted at import, without asking** (1.3.053). The converter is fetched quietly ahead of need (during setup while the language is chosen, and on any start where it's missing, once nothing plays; `fetchConverterWhenIdle`, `ensureConverter`). A conversion keeps the original in the audio store under `orig:<id>` (local to the device: not synced, not in backups; track field `original`, local), and at the end of the run "Keep the originals?" is asked once, with their size. A portable export takes a kept original; Library cleanup can discard them. Deleting a track's audio deletes its original (`idbDelete`).
- **The library's format settings** (1.3.054; Settings → File format, which replaces the old Standardize page and its background MediaRecorder queue, now stopped): one preferred format (presets standard / apple / small / lossless, or custom: MP3, AAC or FLAC, bitrate, rate, channels; FLAC asks "Not recommended" first) and when to convert: `untouched` (Don't touch my files: originals of unplayable files kept without asking), `reduce` (the default; WAV/AIFF as they come in until 2.5.060, since then every kind but MP3 and M4A: see "Every file playable everywhere"), `rules`. Stored in `root.formatPolicy`, saved with the library, carried in the shared document as `doc.policy` (`syncMergePolicy`: one field, the side that changed it wins; a document from an older version leaves it as it was). Conversions as files come in are done by the importing device; the page shows what the settings would convert in the library now; converting them is in Library cleanup (1.4.017). A file waiting to be converted isn't uploaded (`convertSoon`, `convertQueue`): only the result is. Portable export can "Convert to the preferred format" (the Music.app case), leaving the library alone. Re-encoding leans the library to 44.1 kHz stereo (the lossy presets set both, 1.3.055: stereo costs nothing at a constant bitrate); MP3 is constant bitrate (`-abr 0`) so seeking is exact. Kept originals stay on the converting device (the question says so in a shared library). Video files: asked about, their sound taken out (AAC/MP3 copied as is, anything else to the preferred format), the video not kept.
- **A shared library is a joined device's backup** (1.4.004). The welcome asks first whether the device will share a library, with the join code right there; a device that joins gets no backup advice (a computer that can keep a folder is offered one, as optional). "Back up library" and the reminder on leaving Edit stay away while everything on the device is in the shared library (the button reads "In sync"; "Syncing…" meanwhile; tapped, its backup dialog opens with "Up to date"): `sharedCoversLibrary`, the library's fingerprint at the moment the shared library held all of it, audio included (`sharedSyncedFp` after a sync round, then `sharedNoteCovered` once the uploads finish; state record `sharedCovered`). Meanwhile, joined, two-way and online, nothing asks (`sharedAboutToCover`). A receive-only device is covered when it has changed nothing.
- **Who keeps backups** (`doc.keepers`, `root.backupKeepers`: device name → time): a device with an automatic backup folder notes its backups in the shared library, at most every 12 hours, since each note is a new version. Merged per device, the later time winning (`syncMergeKeepers`, in the engine). Shown on the button's tooltip, in Settings → Backups and Shared library: "Backed up by kr37's laptop, today". No roles: any device can be a keeper.
- **Restoring and shared libraries** (1.4.004). Restoring a backup on a joined device used to keep it joined while dropping `sharedBase`, so the next sync put back everything deleted since the backup, for everyone. Now a whole-library restore on a joined device offers **Get the current shared library** first (`rejoinSharedLibraryFresh`: empty this device, join again, Use the shared library), or leaves the shared library before restoring. A backup records which shared library it's from (`meta.sharedFrom`: name and server, never the code); restoring one where it isn't joined suggests joining instead.
- **The welcome fits the device and browser** (1.4.002, `setupEnvironment`). Chrome/Edge on a computer: the backup folder, nothing about other browsers or a local copy (the folder gets 37-player.html anyway). Firefox/Safari on a computer: "works best in Chrome or Edge", with Copy the address, before anything is added. Brave: the brave://flags step. iPad/iPhone in Safari: **the Home Screen first**, because Safari erases a site after 7 days unused and the Home Screen app has a library of its own (so it has to come before importing). "Use it in Safari anyway" is a small link behind a warning; OK leaves setup undone, so it asks again each start. Home Screen app: manual backups into one folder in Files. Android: manual backups and an Install button (`beforeinstallprompt`; Chrome keeps an installed app's data). A local copy of the app and ffmpeg.wasm in the backup folder were dropped from the welcome: a lost library is restored by opening the website and choosing the folder, and a `file://` page can't load the converter beside it anyway. The language is asked once per device.
- **What can be imported:** `AUDIO_EXTENSIONS` (everything browsers play, plus what the converter decodes: WMA, ALAC, AC-3/E-AC-3, DTS, WavPack, TTA, AU, VOC, W64, Speex, RealAudio, CAF, MP2 …; each checked 21 Oct 2026 to import and play) and `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` (sound taken out). Both feed the file pickers (`IMPORT_ACCEPT`), so "All supported types" offers them.
- **Tag edits must never need a re-encode, and must never silently destroy tag information.** Getting the right audio in the right order matters more than perfect metadata.
- **History** (1.4.084; Settings → Shared library and Backups → **History…**, `openSharedHistory`). Lists the versions the shared library keeps (sync.php's `op=history`; on Drive the state folder's files), newest first, grouped by day, with who saved each and what it changed. Each save now sends that (`sharedPostState(cfg, base, doc, prev, extra)`: headers `X-Saved-By` and `X-Change`, a compact `syncChangeSummary`: counts `ta/td/tc` tracks, `pa/pd/pc` playlists and `fa/fd/fc` folders added/deleted/changed with the first name of each (`pan`…), `oc` orders, `rs` the time a restore came from), only to a sync.php ≥ 1.4.084 (`X-Sync-Version`: across sites an older one would refuse the headers and every save) or Drive. sync.php keeps them beside each version as `state/000123.json` (`info_file`, removed with its version by cleanup); Drive as the state file's `appProperties` `by` and `w` (124 bytes per key and value: names shortened, then dropped, `driveHistoryProps`). Older versions show "Saved".
  - **Comparing:** restoring is a review in which "now" (the shared library's current version, after sending this device's changes) is both sides and the earlier version plays the shared library: `syncReviewRows(now, now, then)` gives one row per difference; `syncRestore(now, then, keys)` takes the chosen rows (`syncApplyDependencies` adds the tracks a restored playlist needs) and gives the new document, posted as a new version based on now's (a 409 or a newer version: rows recomputed, choices kept where the row is unchanged, `histRecompare`), then a sync brings it here. **What changed at this point**: `syncReviewRows(previous, previous, then)`, view only. Receive-only devices can look, not restore.
  - **Since 2.5.007** the page has tabs and opens on Roll back the library; the rows with Keep now / Restore and the view "What changed at this point" that this entry describes were replaced by Bring back tracks or playlists (2.5.018) and removed from the code in 2.5.027 (see History redesign, under Open / later). `syncRestore`, `syncApplyDependencies` and `histRecompare` stay, used by Roll back and Bring back.
  - **Screen (until 2.5.018):** points down the side with ‹ › to step (radio buttons for the two views, per kr37: two-coloured toggle buttons are hard to read), rows with Keep now / Restore, playlist rows open to both versions side by side, items marked added/gone/changed. Tests: tests/engine/hist-test.js, tests/hist.js.
- **Settings → Shared library tidied** (1.4.084): Review changes before syncing, Receive updates only, Keep syncing with the screen off, the Sync log and the server's version folded into a closed **More** (`sharedMoreOpen` keeps it open across redraws). On this device stays as radio buttons.
- **Undo and redo** (1.4.084; ↶ ↷ beside Settings on Edit Playlists and Library, Ctrl/⌘-Z, Ctrl/⌘-Shift-Z, Ctrl-Y). Every library write (`doSaveState` → `undoNoteSaved`) compares the library with a starting point (`undoBase`, `undoDoc`: tracks without the audio, artwork and local fields, so background work never makes a step) and keeps the rows that differ, before and after, as a step (`undoStack`, up to 30, in state record `undo`, so they survive a reload); saves within 1.2 s join. What arrives from the shared library (`sharedApplyDoc`) or another window (`reloadLibraryFromStorage`) only moves the starting point (`undoRebase`). Undoing (`undoApply`) puts back only records still as the step left them (a later change from elsewhere stays, and the notice says so), keeps tracks a playlist still uses, and rebuilds the tree (`syncBuildDoc`). A deleted track's full record is kept in the step; its audio and artwork go to `undotrash:<time>:<id>` state records for a day (`deleteLibraryTracks` → `undoTrashPut`; purged hourly), so undoing a deletion brings the sound back; otherwise, in a shared library, it's fetched again. Artwork changes and conversions aren't undoable (artwork counts as audio detail). Find duplicates keeps its own Undo. Undo and Redo are greyed out while a page (Settings, Help, History, Review, Find duplicates) is open over the screen (1.4.085). Help: 'History and Undo' (help/128). A small notice at the bottom (`showToast`) says what was undone. Test: tests/undo.js.
- **Files from a shared library get their real type** (1.4.082). sync.php sends every file as `application/octet-stream`, and that's how received files were stored. Chrome plays them anyway; Safari plays an untyped file only when it recognises it, by an ID3 tag at the start, so split parts (which start straight with frames) made on the laptop gave error 4, "not supported", on the iPad (24 Oct 2026), while the iPad's own splits (stored as audio/mpeg) and every tagged track played. Now `typedAudioBlob` gives a file its type from its content (`sniffAudioFormat`) wherever audio becomes a URL (`getAudioUrl`, `getPlayableAudioUrl`, Library's player, the edit preview, `getDurationViaAudioElement`), which mends files already stored, and received audio is stored with it. Wrapping a blob copies nothing.
- **A received file that already looks doubled is kept** (1.4.082). `looksDoubled` (Safari's pattern, 1.4.016) refused four "LifeOfBuddha…" MP3s on the iPad every time (ID3 then ID3, which still plays), with the message "came out as 135882621 bytes instead of 135882621". Refusing changed nothing: the shared library's copy is the same. Now the check after putting pieces together only refuses when the first piece didn't already show the pattern (`doubledAlready`), and the message says which check failed. Test: tests/dbl.js (also the typing above).
- **Splitting without decoding** (1.4.079). Splitting the 1.2 GB WAV on the iPad (24 Oct 2026) went black and reopened on Playing: `performTrackSplit` decoded the whole file. Now a WAV is cut at a sample frame (`wavLayout`, a new header with the source's own fmt chunk, `wavHeaderFor`) and an MP3 at a frame (`mp3FrameWalk` counts frames from `mp3AudioStart` a few MB at a time; parts carry no ID3 or Xing header), by copying bytes (`splitWithoutDecoding`). Each part's waveform is sliced from the source's (`slicePeaks`); loudness is left to background analysis; `fileTags` is empty, so the library's details are the ones. On WebKit the bytes go through pieces stored on their own and are checked after (`storeAudioFromRange`, the same reason as 1.4.067), which needs the part's size free twice for a moment. **Fades at the cut** (1.4.080; kr37: "a re-encode is better than a click"): a WAV gets a 5 ms raised-cosine fade written into the samples either side (`wavScaleFrames`, `WAV_FADE_MS`); an MP3, without re-encoding, has the one frame either side of the cut silenced by setting its global gain to 0 (`mp3FadeFrames`, `mp3TurnDownFrame`, the way mp3DirectCut fades; `MP3_FADE_STEPS`): the first of part 2 may lean on bit-reservoir data part 2 lacks, the last of part 1 misses the next frame's overlap. 1.4.080 had a ramp after it (−24 … −3 dB, about 130 ms) and a 15 ms WAV fade: cut through music, the dip was noticeable, so 1.4.081 keeps just the silent frame (kr37: no ramp, no setting). Splits on 1.4.079, with no fade at all, sounded fine in kr37's tests, so browsers hide those frames well; the silent frame guards against one that doesn't. The gap that remains between parts played back to back is 37 Player's, which isn't gapless (one `<audio>` element); not worth building for Center use (splits make room for a talk or meditation), and a setting for any of this was judged needless; a frame's CRC, if it has one, is worked out again (not tested: ffmpeg's encoder writes no CRC). Tested with a 440 Hz tone cut mid-wave: no step bigger than the tone's own, silence at the cut. Other formats still decode whole, and past `tooLongForWholeDecode` they're refused with "convert it to MP3 first" (`alertSplitTooLong`). A failed split says why (1.4.083): a full device gets its own message (`alertSplitFull`: room for both parts, and on WebKit the larger once more), anything else names the error, and the playback log gets a line. Test: tests/split.js (also the WebKit path, in 256 KB pieces, and a full device). Since 1.4.086 (below) the byte cutting takes any number of cuts (`splitByBytes`, `mp3WalkCuts`, which picks the nearest frame boundary), and parts get their own waveform and the original's loudness.
- **The first run and protecting libraries** (1.4.092; the plan under Open / later, built 28 Oct 2026). **First run:** language, then "Do you want to join (or create) a shared library at this time?" (1.4.094; it had said "join code", which only fits the droplet) **No, just start** / **Yes, join or create one** (`setupShareStep`); nothing about libraries, browsers or backups. `firstRunDone()` sets `backupInfo.headsUpPending` (unless joined) and `backupInfo.glow` (play, import, display: `body[data-glow]`, CSS `firstGlow`; each stops when clicked. 1.4.094: "play" is also the big ▶ Play of a playlist not started, and "import" is the Library tab while on Playing (Import / Export is only on Edit Playlists and Library), then Import / Export). Joining on Safari (iPad/iPhone tab, Mac) shows the Heads-up first (`headsUpMattersForJoining`). The old multi-step welcome (backup folder, other browsers, Brave, manual, Home Screen, add audio, "keep backups here too?") and 38 of its strings are gone. **Heads-up** (`openHeadsUp`, gate `headsUpBeforeImport` at the top of `importFiles` and `bulkImportFolder`, not restores): only for libraries started with this first run (`headsUpPending`), skipped when joined, auto-backed-up or a quick job; at most three steps by platform (`headsUpSteps`): iPad/iPhone Safari tab: the Home Screen steps; Mac Safari: Chrome/Edge or the Dock, with Copy the address; Firefox: Chrome/Edge; Brave: the flags; Install when the browser offers it (Chrome/Edge/Android), Android's menu otherwise; then automatic backups + Join (folder-capable) or Join + backing up by hand. Buttons: Cancel / Import now; on Safari I'll do that first / Import here anyway, which asks **Are you sure?** (Go back / Import anyway / quick job). A step's button ticks it (✓) when done. **Quick job** (`backupInfo.quickJob`, `QUICK_JOB_TRACKS` = 20 more tracks, ended by opening Settings): no Heads-up, no daily prompt, the button amber instead of red. **Backup button:** red (`urgent`) as soon as `libraryNeedsBackup()` (no more amber for an hour, `BACKUP_URGENT_AFTER_MS` gone); no prompt on leaving Edit Playlists/Library (`openSaveBeforeLeavingDialog`, `backupPromptNeeded` gone). **Protected** = automatic backups, or joined and not stuck (`libraryProtected`). **Sync not finishing:** `noteSyncPending` (in `updateBackupIndicator`) records in localStorage `37player.syncPendingSince.<library>` when the library first isn't covered by the shared library and clears it when it is; after 3 days (`SYNC_STUCK_AFTER_MS`) the button reads **Sync isn't finishing** (red), opening `openSyncStuckDialog`. **Once a day** (`maybeDailyPrompt`, from `applyAppMode` on Edit Playlists/Library; localStorage `37player.dailyAsk.<library>` = the date): the stuck-sync dialog, or **Time for a backup** when not protected, changes not backed up and the last backup more than a day old (`DAILY_BACKUP_AFTER_MS`), with Back up now / automatic backups / Join / Not today. The theme tour wasn't built: the display button glows instead. **Backups as .37p:** manual backups and native exports end in .37p (still a zip; `exportFileName`, downloaded as application/octet-stream so Safari doesn't open it as a zip); portable exports stay .zip; everything that reads backups takes both (`isBackupZipName`); the import and Check my backup files pickers take .37p, but on iOS have no `accept` (iOS may grey out an extension it doesn't know; try once on an iPad); `manifest.webmanifest` claims .37p for the installed app, and `openWithFiles` restores it. Tests: welcome.js (rewritten), p37.js, share.js; other tests click **No, just start**, and the hooks turn the Heads-up and the daily prompt off unless `__headsUpOn`.
- **Signing in to Google while playing** (1.4.094): the sign-in leaves the page, so `driveBeginSignIn` asks first (**Stop and sign in** / Cancel) when something is playing. A new tab was the other idea, but the sign-in comes back into whichever page started it, and a second 37 Player window on the same library is what the rest of the app avoids.
- **Automatic restore** (1.4.093, `restore.php`; the last step of the first-run plan). Opt-in switch in Settings → Backups (`renderAutoRestoreSetting`, shown only where `restore.php?op=status` says available, so not in a copy opened from a file). When on, each library sends restore.php what would bring it back (`autoRestoreEntry`: its name, the browser, its shared library: join code, server, device name, or the Drive folder; and its backups: a folder's *name* only, since browsers never give a page a full path, or the last backup file's name, with the date), whenever that changes (`autoRestoreSoon` from `updateBackupIndicator`; compared with localStorage `37player.autoRestoreSent.<library>`). restore.php seals it (sodium secretbox, or AES-GCM) with a key only the server has (`restore.key` in SYNC37_DATA_DIR, made on first use, mode 0600) and keeps it in a cookie `p37restore`: HttpOnly (the page can't read or write it, so Safari's 7-day rule, which removes what scripts wrote, doesn't touch it), SameSite=Strict, Path = restore.php, 400 days; same origin only (Origin and Sec-Fetch-Site); entries at most 1500 bytes, the cookie under 3800, the oldest dropped first. Nothing is stored on the server. When 37 Player opens with an empty library and no setup done, `offerAutoRestore` asks for the entries, leaves out libraries whose database is still on this device, and offers **Bring it back / Bring them back** or **Start fresh** (not asked again in that library: `37player.autoRestoreDeclined.<library>`). Bringing back (`restoreFromEntry`): a droplet library joins again with the code (one tap); a Drive library asks to sign in to Google again; backups only: says where they were, then the folder picker or the file picker. Other lost libraries go into the list of libraries with `pendingRestore`, brought back when opened (`restorePendingForThisLibrary`). Deleting a library removes its entry; turning the switch off clears the cookie. Test: tests/restore.js (its own `php -S` on port 8863).
- **The saved app, and two copies of it** (sw.js, 1.4.090–091; kr37: a library must be something people can count on, whatever happens on the droplet). With the droplet's site partition unmounted (25 Oct 2026), Apache answered "Page not found", and a refresh showed that at IKRC and here: the worker used its saved copy only when a request failed outright. Now it keeps the last version that started properly on this device (`?37copy=good`) and the newest fetched (`?37copy=new`), with what it knows in `__37player-sw-state` ({good, fresh: {v, h}, bad: [h], reverted}), and opens the good copy when the server answers with an error, doesn't answer in 8 s (`NAV_TIMEOUT_MS`; the request is aborted, or the browser holds later requests for the same page behind it), sends a page whose last line isn't `<!-- 37 Player: end of file -->` (`END_MARK`: cut off; deliberately without a version number, since one that disagreed with the meta tag would make every device refuse that update), or sends a version that doesn't start. **"Up":** `signalAppUp()` once init has drawn the screen (also from the storage-not-responding message: the app is running), answered with whether a fallback happened (toast `swReverted`). **Watchdog:** a small script at the top of the page (runs even if the main script can't) tells the worker "failed" after 60 s without "up"; if that version is the fresh one and a different good copy exists, its hash goes on the refused list (last 10) and the page reloads into the good copy. A different file on the server is tried afresh. On upgrade, the copy kept by the pre-1.4.091 worker becomes the good one. The app file fetched by the page itself (not a navigation) falls back to the good copy on errors. Test: tests/swfallback.js (404, 500, no answer, cut off, a version that throws in init with the watchdog at 4 s, then a working one, then the server stopped). A deliberate rollback on the server to a version from before 1.4.091 (no end line) would be refused: roll back to 1.4.091 or later.
- **Switching library while audio is being sent** (1.4.087). Switching is a reload, and while audio is being sent to the shared library the page holds back leaving (`beforeunload`), so the browser asks its own "Leave this page?", which can't say why. On kr37's laptop Firefox (24 Oct 2026) that box was cancelled, not understood, and afterwards Firefox ignored every `location.reload()` in that tab (F5 still worked; another tab was fine). Now `switchLibrary` asks first, in words (Still sending audio: Cancel / Switch anyway), and Switch anyway skips the browser's question (`leavingOnPurpose`). Troubleshooting extras added on the way (a save watchdog, a reload fallback) were taken out again in 1.4.089. Test: tests/switchsend.js.
- **The splitter** (1.4.086, kr37's design): its own overlay page (`openSplitter`, state `spl`), from ✎ → **Split…** or Library's **Split track…**. The old two-part dialog is gone.
  - **Two views:** the whole track (`spOver`, with the stretch shown below as a rectangle) and a close-up (`spZoom`) drawn from a **detailed waveform**: every 5 ms, the first channel's average and the loudest of two channels (`splitFineAnalysis`; WAV read a few MB at a time, MP3 decoded in pieces placed by frame count, one frame early for the bit reservoir; others decoded whole if they fit, else the track's own 720 points). 2-hour MP3: about 20 s on the cloud test machine, 1.4 M buckets; 30-min WAV 2.6 s. It's kept for reopening the same track (`splitFineCache`). The close-up is in dB by default ("Quiet sounds larger", so breaths show).
  - **Cursor and marks:** the white line is where playing starts and where a mark goes. ← → move it (or the selected mark) 10 ms, Shift 100 ms, Ctrl a second; buttons −100/−10/+10/+100 ms for touch. M/Enter marks, Delete removes, [ ] go between marks, drag a mark to move it; a piece is at least 0.2 s (`SPLIT_MIN_PIECE`). **Up to here** / **From here** (, and .) play the two seconds before/after the cursor and leave it where it was; speed 0.5×, 1×, 2×, 3× with pitch kept (1.4.087: 0.75× and 0.25× dropped, kr37). **Snap:** only marks dropped while playing go to the quietest 30 ms within ±0.5 s (`splitFindQuiet`); a cursor placed by hand is exact (kr37: the moment before the inhalation). **Find the quiet** (S) snaps the selected mark on request.
  - **Colours** (1.4.087): each piece is drawn in its own colour on both waveforms (the first amber, then `ALBUM_TEXT_COLORS` in turn, as albums in a playlist; left out: grey), with the same colour on its row's number and edge (`splitPieceColor`). Marks are bright red (`splitMarkColor`). Two-finger scrolling works on the whole-track view as on the close-up (sideways moves the stretch shown; Firefox's line-based deltas allowed for).
  - **Pieces table:** keep or leave out, name (auto "… - part n", numbered among kept pieces until edited), track #, artist, album; ▶ plays one piece.
  - **Column B:** ✎ from the playlist being edited passes `{playlistId, itemId}`; Split then asks (radio, Do it now) whether that entry becomes the kept pieces. Only that entry: other playlists keep the original (kr37: splits are for a particular event). The replacement happens inside the same save as the new tracks (`opts.beforeSave`, `splitReplaceEntry`), so one Undo takes it all back.
  - **Format** (`splitTrackInto`): if the File format settings would convert the original (`trackMatchesPolicy`) and the converter is here, each piece is encoded from the original straight to the preferred format, cut to the sample, with 5 ms fades (`splitViaConverter` "encode"; no "keep the originals?" question). Otherwise WAV/MP3 by bytes; other formats through the converter as they are (`-c:a copy` for lossy; lossless PCM/FLAC/ALAC written again in the same codec, which is exact); with no converter, decoded whole to WAV and then `convertTracks` like an import. The converter is retried once fresh after a crash, and failing that the split falls back rather than failing.
  - **MP3 timing:** a player trims an MP3's encoder delay plus 529 samples when it has a LAME/Info header, so what's heard at t is t + that in the file's frames (`mp3PlaybackOffset`, about 25 ms). Cuts and the detailed waveform allow for it; tests/splitter.js checks a click at a known time lands within half a frame of where it's heard.
  - **Loudness:** pieces copy the original's `loudness` and `volumeDb` (kr37: the pieces of a recording should sound as it did); a source without a measurement leaves them to be measured. Waveforms: from the detailed one (`peaksFromFine`), else measured from the piece.
  - Tests: tests/splitter.js (all four formats, three cuts, the WAV policy case, keyboard, column B and Undo, phone width), tests/split.js.
- **Backups without a folder** (1.4.078). Found on kr37's iPad (24 Oct 2026): a 13.84 GB full backup closed its dialog within a minute, then Safari worked for about ten minutes before showing a preview page ("Open in…"), and only saving from there kept it (in a Home Screen app a download goes to that preview, not Downloads). The space was taken while Safari built the file; saving it was a move.
  - **A phone or tablet in a shared library** (`sharedCoversLibrary` or `sharedAboutToCover`): Back up library says it doesn't need a backup file, with the file's size and the space it needs; **Make a backup file anyway** carries on.
  - **iPad and iPhone** (`env.ios`): the tip says it takes many minutes; after building, `handOverFileOnIos` offers **Save to Files…** (`navigator.share` with the file, from a fresh tap; a share that went through counts as saved, a cancelled one asks again) or **Download it instead**; where files can't be shared, it downloads and says what to do on Safari's page. Whether Safari shares a file of many GB is still to be seen on the iPad.
  - **Do you still have your full backup?** When a changes backup would be suggested, it's asked first, naming the file (`manualBase.fileName`, kept since 1.4.078), at most once a week (`manualBase.confirmedAt`, `MANUAL_BASE_CONFIRM_MS`). No, or not sure makes a full one.
  - **Check my backup files** (Settings → Backups): picked zips (or a folder, on computers) are read only as far as their table of contents and meta.json (`readBackupMetas`), and judged (`judgeBackupFiles`): the pair a restore would use is kept, older fulls and changes can be deleted (total shown), a changes file without its full backup is flagged, other libraries' and nameless older ones are left alone. Which library: `meta.backupOf.name` (new in 1.4.078), else the name in the file name (`backupNamePart`, since 1.4.051), else a shared base. Finding the next changes backup's full backup confirms it; missing, **Make the next backup a full one** forgets it. A page can't delete files, so it only says which.
  - Tests: tests/bkcheck.js, tests/manualbk.js.
- **Getting a log off a device** (1.4.077): the sync and playback logs each have **Copy all** and **Share…** (a dated .txt through the share sheet; **Save as a file** where `navigator.canShare({files})` says no). Both start with a header in English (`logExportText`, `LOG_KINDS`): version, device name, browser and whether installed, address, library and its size, the shared library and its settings, storage, a short guide to the lines, and links to 37player.com and NOTES.md, so a helper (or an AI someone asks) can read it without the 34,000-line app. Lines oldest first. Copying the log by hand on the iPad had sent an old log through WhatsApp. Test: tests/logs.js.
- **A changes backup leans on a file the page can't see** (1.4.076). A manual "changes" backup only restores together with its full backup, and `backupInfo.manualBase` only records that the full one was *made*: it may since have been deleted from Files or Downloads (kr37 purged his on the iPad, and the dialog still offered changes since 3 October as the suggested choice). The dialog now says the app can't see whether that file is still there, and suggests **New full backup** when the changes pass half a full backup, the full backup is over 14 days old (`MANUAL_BASE_STALE_MS`), or the shared library holds everything here. Test: tests/manualbk.js.
- **Backup folders use ID filenames** under "Library - do not touch". Readable names would invite reorganizing. A Portable export is the player-ready copy.
- **An empty library never backs up,** so a wiped browser can't overwrite a good backup. A backup folder belongs to one copy of the app; another copy only takes it over by an explicit choice.
- **Silences and stops live only inside playlists,** and each one is independent: they're never shared between playlists. Portable exports write them as `STOP - name.wav` / `SILENCE - name.wav` placeholders, which turn back into real items on import.
- **The PIN only guards against accidental edits.** The repo is public.
- **sync.php carries its own version** (`SYNC_VERSION`): set it to the app's version whenever sync.php itself changes, not otherwise, both in `SYNC_VERSION` and in the comment at the top of the file. The app shows it in Settings → Shared library; `php sync.php version` prints it.
- **The minor version follows the shell** (kr37, 13 Nov 2026, at 2.3.000 and shell 2.3.0): when a page calls, or could call, on something earlier shells don't have, the minor version goes up, the page's and the shell's together (x.y.000 and x.y.0). 2.3 because the page can now tell the person about a newer shell and tell the shell to update itself (2.2.040, shell 2.2.3), and asks it to use the chosen copy of a library (2.2.041, shell 2.2.6). Not the same as `<meta name="desktop-bridge">`, which is what the page can't work without: a shell below it refuses the page; the 2.3 calls are only used when the shell has them. **2.6** (20 Nov 2026): the page asks the shell for files opened with 37 Player (`p37_opened_take`, shell 2.6.0). It was first numbered 2.5.062 with shell 2.5.9; kr37 pointed to this rule, and the page became 2.6.000 and the shell 2.6.0. (The shells' trash 2.5.2, watch 2.5.3 and typed_path 2.5.6 were also new calls without a new minor version.)
- **Every shipped change bumps** `<meta name="app-version">`. Replace only that first tag: a blanket search-and-replace of `app-version" content="…"` also rewrote the pattern in `versionFromHtml`, which broke Check for updates from 1.3.022 to 1.3.037 (fixed in 1.3.038; the pattern is now built so it can't be matched). The version stays visible in the header, and it's a button that checks for updates.
- **Delete everywhere, with a partial device, of tracks a playlist elsewhere still uses** (1.4.185; kr37, 6 Nov 2026, the Mac with the droplet library): the shared library keeps such tracks for its playlists (`keepUsed`), and the device used to take them back too (its part kept every track its base had) and download them again. Now `syncMergePartial` leaves a deleted-everywhere track off the device unless one of the device's own playlists uses it; after the sync it says how many the shared library kept and for which playlists (`partialKeptElsewhere`), and forgets those IDs. **1.4.186 (kr37's answer; help: Shared library → What's on this device):** after Delete everywhere a partial device looks at the shared library (`sharedPlaylistsElsewhereUsing`) and, if playlists it doesn't hold use those tracks, asks: **Delete everywhere, from those playlists too** (`cfg.purgeFromPlaylists`: the merge takes them out of the shared library's playlists this device doesn't hold, then deletes them), **Keep them for those playlists**, or Cancel; unreachable, they're kept for them. And playlists or folders the tidy-up removes along with tracks deleted everywhere go everywhere too: left in the shared library (a "removed here" playlist), the playlist kept the tracks and the device took them back, probably kr37's case. The kept-elsewhere note only counts tracks this device has actually let go.
- **Tried on the Mac mini (Monterey's WebKit, desktop Test 19, 6 Nov 2026):** page updates (1.4.183–186 arrived and applied, Update now without a restart, kept after quitting); the page's height fix; a pCloud album .zip imported and converted by itself (the 1.4.173 copy and the no-SIMD converter); a 1.2 GB WAV received through a shared library in pieces, put together, playing anywhere in it, and Damaged files → Check clean (one slow track: the check reads each file through).
- **Libraries in folders in the desktop app** (1.4.193, step 3 of 2.0, bridge 2; desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md). With a shell whose bridge is 2 (`window.__p37Bridge`, set before the page runs), `STORAGE_KIND` is `"desktop"`: a library whose list entry says `desktop: true` is a `FolderLibraryStore` over the shell's folders (`desktopLibraryDir`: root `lib-<id>`, made by `fs_root_default` in `Music/37 Player Libraries/<name>` with its `37 Player library.json` the first time it's needed). Every library is in a folder (from 1.4.196: see the next entries; 1.4.193 had a `desktop` flag per library, for libraries from before, dropped with the move). Deleting a library forgets its folder (`fs_root_forget`); the files stay, as for a browser's folder library. **Tests:** `STORE=desktop` runs the page as the desktop app against a stand-in shell (`tests/fake-shell.js`, folders in OPFS, device.json in localStorage `__fakeDevice`), as `STORE=opfs` does for the files store; tests that open IndexedDB themselves can't run that way (play, e2e, conv…).
