First run: edtree, exportfile, p37, copylib, undo, hist, bkkeep, deltidy, damaged, splitter, wav, reconcile all OK. Big recordings are read where they lie from 1.4.194 (next entry).
- **Big files read where they lie in the desktop app** (1.4.194, step 3b of 2.0). In a desktop library, `DesktopFileHandle.getFile()` gives a file over `DISK_BLOB_MIN` (32 MB) as a `DiskBlob`: size, type, name and `slice()` like a Blob, its bytes fetched from the shell in pieces (`fs_read` with a range) only when read. A whole one (`.whole`) plays from its address (`audioObjectUrl`: `p37lib://` through `convertFileSrc`; the shell serves Range), so a 1.3 GB WAV streams and seeks from disk instead of being read into the page; a part of a file is read and played as its own bytes. It is deliberately **not** a Blob: `new Blob([d])`, an object URL or a request body would quietly hold "[object Object]", so its `toString` throws and a missed place fails loudly. Where a real Blob is needed, `realBlob()` reads it whole (memory): requests (`sharedFetch`, `driveCall`), writing tags (`blobWithCurrentTags`; a rewrite of one over `BIG_REWRITE_BYTES` is skipped, as on WebKit), zips and exports, the converter's input (`converterFile`; the converter needs a File in its worker), recovering orphans. **Writing:** a whole DiskBlob into a desktop folder is copied by the shell (`fs_copy`), never through the page, and a write onto itself is skipped; anything else over 8 MB goes in 8 MB pieces into `<name>.p37part` (`fs_write` with `p37-append: 1`, new in the shell's files.rs) renamed onto the file at the end, so a crash leaves the old file whole. Into another kind of folder (a backup, an export) `writeBlobInto` writes a DiskBlob piece by piece. The splitter's piece of a big recording (header + stretch + tail) is a `JoinedBlob`, written the same way and read back from the library (`storeAudioFromRange`). A kept original that is a DiskBlob is copied on disk. Found by `STORE=desktop` with every file a DiskBlob (`fake-shell.js` sets `__diskBlobMin` to 0, a stress setting); `tests/diskblob.js` checks the pieces (none over 8 MB), the copy, the joined piece, playing a part and the whole, and a backup of a 20 MB file.
- **Big files are never held whole in the page by themselves** (2.2.010, 11 Nov 2026; kr37's 8 GB Mac mini with a 5400 rpm disk). What happened: a 2.7 GB WAV in a library joined to Google Drive; "Shared library: sending 1 of 1", then the whole Mac froze for 30 s at a time, Activity Monitor showing the page's process (`tauri://localhost`) at 5.48 GB and swap in use. Don't touch my files was on, so nothing was converting. **The cause:** `sharedFetch` turned a disk file into a real Blob (`realBlob`) before handing it to `driveFetch`, so the whole file was read into memory before Drive's own piece-by-piece upload began; and `realBlob` read it into one buffer and then copied that into a Blob, so twice the file's size. (The droplet's sends were already in pieces, `sharedSendInPieces`; Claude first blamed the converter, wrongly.) **Now:** (1) for a Google Drive library the body stays a `DiskBlob` through `driveFetch` to `driveUpload`, whose resumable upload slices it, so each 16 MB piece is read from the disk as it's sent (`driveCall` makes the piece real); (2) `realBlob` reads `DISK_READ_PIECE` (8 MB) at a time, each piece a Blob of its own at once, so anything that still needs the whole file takes its size once; (3) `BIG_FILE_BYTES` (500 MB; kr37's figure): above it nothing that needs the whole file in memory happens by itself. **Converting:** the converter still holds its input whole (`converterFile`), so `convertTracks(ids, {auto: true})` (after an import, after a split) leaves big files out; asked for by a person (Library cleanup, a banner, a track's dialog), big files get a warning first (`convertBigTitle…`: memory about the file's size, time, close other apps, playback sets it aside) with Convert anyway / Convert only the smaller ones / Cancel; the ones agreed to are remembered for the session only (`convertBigOk`), and `runConvertQueue` passes over any other big file. Library cleanup's convert note says how many are over the line and only convert when asked. **A queue carried over a restart** (`scheduleConvertResume`) never takes up a big file again (on 8 Nov the 2.7 GB conversion restarted at every opening, before Settings could be reached), and starts a minute after opening, not six seconds (`CONVERT_RESUME_AFTER_MS`; "nothing heavy in the first minute"). **Details:** `blobWithCurrentTags` looks at the format first (40 bytes), so a file with no writer isn't read at all, and a disk file over the line is returned as it is (`tooBig`): a portable export carries such a file with the details it already has. Still whole in memory, to do when a native converter comes (bridge 3): converting a file under 500 MB; a portable or .37p export, which gathers real Blobs of every file. Not changed: an import's waveform step for a big file can't be stopped mid-file ("Stopping once this file is done…"). Test: bigfile.js (the stand-in shell and the fake Google: the biggest read at once is 8 MB for a real Blob, 1 MB for a Drive send with 1 MB pieces).
- **2.2.000: the middle number is the bridge** (7 Nov 2026, kr37): bridge 2 is x.2.yyy, so 2.0.000–2.0.004 (only on his machines) become 2.2.000, and bridge 2 means the shell as of 2.2.000 (Google sign-in, `fs_root_info`, `fs_root_rename`, `fs_root_show`, `fs_places_restore`, `fs_remove_if_empty`); an older 2.0 shell (Test 24) lacks them, which is why Show did nothing there. A command the page relies on after this makes bridge 3 and 2.3.000. Desktop builds are named by version first: "37 Player 2.2.000 for computers (build 27)", files `37-Player-2.2.000-mac.dmg`, `…-windows-setup.exe`, `…-windows.msi` (the release tags stay `desktop-test-<n>`; desktop-latest.yml still takes the test number).
- **The wipe test on the HP** (2.0.004, 7 Nov 2026, kr37): deleting `%LOCALAPPDATA%\com.player37.app\EBWebView` lost nothing (a flash of the dark theme, then the library and the queued track as they were); deleting `Roaming\com.player37.app` too found the library in the default place at once, but every track used in place said "On a drive that isn't connected": the shell's list of folders (roots.json) had gone with it. Now, before the library opens, the shell puts back the folders the library's own record names (`fs_places_restore`, `places_to_restore`: it reads `places.json` in the library's folder itself, takes only that library's `in-<library>-…` ids, and only folders that are there), so the page can't name a folder of its own choosing. **Move into the library** that leaves the folder with nothing but empty folders and the system's leftovers (`.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini`) offers to remove it (`fs_remove_if_empty`). **Edit Playlists' imports stay questionless** (kr37: on Edit Playlists every obstacle to making a playlist is removed): dropped or chosen files and folders are copied into the library, duplicates and all; Use in place is only Library's Import → Choose a folder…. Seen once, not reproduced: after the data folder was emptied (on 2.0.000), choosing the other library in the top bar's picker didn't switch, while Settings → Library's Open did. Tests: inplace.js.
- **Use in place, from the HP** (2.0.003, 7 Nov 2026): **Look for new files** brought converted tracks in again (a .wma used in place, converted, its original let go: nothing pointed into the folder for it). Each folder now remembers every file it gave the library and the track it became (`f.seen` in `places`, `placeRemember`, `placeKnownPaths`); a file is known while its track is in the library, and `tidyPlaces` keeps such a folder. (Skip in that import's review was the playlists' question, not the recordings'.) **A drive unplugged while a track plays** (kr37: odd-ball, not worth much more for now): the three players are watched (`watchPlaceDrive`): an error, or waiting over 3 s, on a track used in place looks at its file, and if it's gone pauses and says the drive isn't connected; Play (`placeAwarePlay`) says so while it's gone, and once it's back loads the file again where it was (before, Play did nothing even after the stick was back). The dialog adds that Copy into the library prevents it (`placeOfflineTip`). Test: inplace.js.
- **kr37's first afternoon with 2.0 on the HP** (2.0.002, 7 Nov 2026):
  - **A library's folder follows its name:** at the app's start, before the library opens, the shell renames its folder within the folder it's in (`fs_root_rename`, `renamed_path`: " (2)" when the name is taken, nothing when it already has it; a change of capitals only is a plain rename). Not while it's open: Windows won't rename a folder whose files are in use. A first library without a name keeps "My library". Test: desktoplibs.js.
  - **No backup prompts in the desktop app:** any desktop library counts as a folder library (`libraryIsFolder`), as one a browser keeps in a folder already did: no Heads up before the first import (it offered a backup folder and a shared library), no Time for a backup, the button at the top says In a folder. kr37: don't interrupt a first import with a shared library; whether to encourage a backup or a shared library at all is for later (Open / later).
  - **Converting a track used in place** keeps its original as a reference (it stays where it is and takes no room), so Keep the originals? leaves those out (`showKeepOriginals`).
  - **Switching library while something plays** asks whichever player it is: `switchLibrary` (and the update prompts, and closing the desktop window) use `playingHere()`, which counts Edit Playlists' player and a silence; `isAnythingPlaying()` didn't. Test: switchplaying.js.
  - **Exported silence is MP3** (`buildSilentMp3Bytes`; the silent WAV builder is gone): no encoder, since an MPEG-1 Layer III frame of zeros decodes as silence; 44.1 kHz stereo like the rest, 32 kbit/s, about 240 KB a minute (the WAV was about 10 MB). Tagged Title ">>> SILENCE: … <<<", Artist "Silence", Album "Silence" (stops: Album "Stops"), and filed as any track: `music/Silence/Silence/`, `music/Silence/Stops/`. Imports still know the old `_Silence`/`_Stops` WAVs. Test: silencemp3.js.
  - **Settings → Library in the desktop app** said "the desktop app's own storage (IndexedDB)" and gave the browser's quota (10 GB "available"): both left from before 2.0. Now: 37 Player (the desktop app, its version), Library folder (its path, with Show: `fs_root_show` opens it in Finder or Explorer), Recordings (how many in the library's folder, how many used in place), Syncing with, Backups ("None needed from 37 Player: the library's folder is ordinary files"), and Storage (what's in the folder, what's used in place). The browser's rows are as before.
  - **A folder chosen at the very start** could be forgotten by `tidyPlaces` (four seconds after the start) while its mode question was open: it waits now (`placeChoosing`).
- **A library's name in its folder** (2.0.001): a library that takes its shared library's name on joining (`libraryAdoptSharedName`) now writes it into its folder's marker too (`desktopMarkerName`, as a rename does), so it's found again under that name. The folder's own name is still the one it was made with ("My library" for a first library, named before anything else).
- **m and d builds** (7 Nov 2026, kr37): an app build follows one page channel, fixed when it's built (`P37_PAGE_CHANNEL`): `page-test` (dev's pages) or `page-latest` (main's). A push to `desktop` (then `tauri-spike`) makes a **d** build ("37 Player 2.2.000 for computers (build 28d)"); started by hand (Actions → Desktop app for the Mac → Run workflow) with channel **main**, an **m** build. Builds up to 27 have no letter and follow dev. desktop-latest.yml puts an m build on `desktop-latest` (37player.com's home page) and a d build on `desktop-latest-dev` (test.37player.com's: index.html swaps its links there). Friends get m builds, so they get only pages kr37 has merged to main. Both are the same app to the computer (`com.player37.app`): installing one replaces the other, and they share settings and libraries; each keeps its channel's downloaded pages apart (`pages/latest`, `pages/test`, from build 29), so switching builds switches pages at once.
- **The desktop app on the home page** (7 Nov 2026, kr37): index.html's "The desktop app" links to fixed addresses on GitHub, `releases/download/desktop-latest/37-Player-mac.dmg`, `…/37-Player-windows-setup.exe`, `…/37-Player-windows.msi` (the repo is public; the droplet never holds them), and shows the version from GitHub's API. `.github/workflows/desktop-latest.yml` (on main) copies a test build's installers there under those names, run by hand with the test number, so a build reaches visitors only once kr37 chooses it. First: Test 24 (2.0.000).
- **2.0.000** (7 Nov 2026; kr37: "Use in place functioning" is 2.0.000, his tests 2.0.001 on, then friends). The first page that uses the shell's file commands (bridge 2): the desktop app keeps every library in a folder of its own in a place the person chooses, big files read where they lie, libraries found again by their markers, Use in place / Copy / Move, the device's settings in device.json, a library's small values in local.json, Google Drive sign-in through the browser. Desktop shell 2.0.0 (identifier `com.player37.app`). Before it: the full suite (101 tests, on 1.4.197, all passing, three re-run on 1.4.200) and the `STORE=desktop` set (17, on 1.4.200). In a browser, 2.0.000 behaves as 1.4.200 did.
- **Google Drive in the desktop app** (1.4.200, shell `oauth.rs`; kr37, 7 Nov 2026: a cloud library other than the droplet is important, soon). Google refuses sign-in in an app's own web view, so the desktop app signs in in the person's browser: `driveBeginSignIn` asks the shell (`p37_google_signin`) to open token.php's start address there, with `return` set to a one-time address the shell listens on (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/p37auth`, RFC 8252's loopback); token.php sends the person back there with the result after `#`, which never reaches a server, so the little page served there hands it to `/done`, and the command gives it to the page, which carries on as after the website's redirect (`driveTakeSignInResult(given)`, `driveHandleSignIn`); playback isn't stopped, since nothing leaves the page. token.php accepts that return address (exactly that path, on 127.0.0.1) and the desktop app's origins (`tauri://localhost`, `http://tauri.localhost`) whatever its config says; Google's own redirect stays token.php, so nothing changes in Google Cloud. `GDRIVE_TOKEN_URL` is 37player.com's in the app. `driveCanSignInHere`: the website, or a shell with bridge 2 (`driveNotInDesktop` on 1.4 shells). `p37_open_url` opens an https address in the browser. **Not yet tried on a real machine:** whether Google's Drive API answers the app's page across origins as it does a website (it should; if not, the shell can make Drive's requests itself with `ureq`). **Deploy:** token.php 1.4.200 on the droplet before trying it.
- **Use in place, Copy and Move (the desktop app)** (1.4.199; desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md, "Use in place"; kr37, 7 Nov 2026: Use in place before rolling 2.0 out, "real ease of entry"). Import / Export → **Choose a folder…** in the desktop app (`desktopImportFolder`) opens the system's folder dialog: the folder becomes a root on the shell's list (`in-<library>-<n>`), and a question asks how to take its recordings: **Use in place**, **Copy into the library**, **Move into the library** (each explained; a folder on another drive, `fs_root_info`'s `other_drive`, makes Copy the suggestion and says why). Its files are listed through the shell (`placeListFiles`): recordings as DiskBlobs marked `inPlace` (references, whatever their size), covers and playlists read here, each with the `webkitRelativePath` a browser's picker gives, then the usual import review. **Stored as references:** this window's library store (`keepsPlaces`) writes a whole `inPlace` DiskBlob as `{$place: root, $at: path, …}`, in any record (audio, a kept original, Undo's trash); `blobNames` never lists one, so it's never orphaned, renamed (`renameRecordings`) or deleted; `revive` gives a DiskBlob of the file, or null when its folder can't be reached (`placeOffline`). Another library's store copies it instead. **kr37's b):** `rewriteFileTagsIfPossible` never touches a recording used in place; edits live in the records, and exports, .37p and the shared library's copy get them written into their own copies (as before). Anything made from one (a split's pieces, a conversion) is the library's own. Deleting such a track only forgets it (the confirmation says so). **Copy and Move** are the same import, then `placeIntoLibrary`: each reference rewritten as the library's own file (the shell copies it, `fs_copy` across roots, into `music/` under its readable name), read back at its size, and with Move the original deleted (`fs_remove`); one that fails stays in place, and the folder is forgotten only when none is left. The folders are the library's state record `places` ({root, label: its path, added}); Settings → Library lists them (`renderPlaceFolders`) with **Look for new files** (`placeLookForNew`: recordings not referenced yet go through the import review; ones no longer there are counted), **Copy into the library**, **Move into the library**, and, when it can't be reached, **Find this folder…** (`fs_root_choose` with the same id). A track whose folder can't be reached plays as "On a drive that isn't connected" (`showAudioMissing`) and isn't counted as missing (`tracksWithMissingAudio`). `playlists/*.m3u8` reach them with `../` from the library's folder, or the full path on another Windows drive (`placePathFrom`). `tidyPlaces` (a few seconds after the start) forgets folders nothing points into, and roots of imports given up. Only Import → Choose a folder… is in place: dropped or picked files, and folders imported into a playlist, are copied. Shell: `fs_root_info` (bridge2). Test: `tests/inplace.js`. Help: Libraries → In the desktop app.
- **The desktop app's libraries: in folders from the start, and found again** (1.4.195–196, step 4 of 2.0; `desktopLibrariesAtStart`, before the window's library opens). kr37's test: delete the app's webview storage (CCleaner, CleanMyMac), start again, nothing is missing. In the desktop app with bridge 2 **every** library is a folder of its own (`libraryInDesktopFolder` is simply `STORAGE_KIND === "desktop"`), made in the libraries' place, asked at the first start (**Where should your libraries live?**, `Music/37 Player Libraries` shown as the suggestion, **Use this place** / **Choose another place…**; kr37: the place is the person's choice). **No move from browser storage (kr37, 6 Nov 2026):** 1.4.195 copied libraries out of the webview's storage at the first start of 2.0, with a 30-day fallback; dropped in 1.4.196, because only kr37 had used the 1.4 desktop app (test libraries, to be deleted) and the 2.0 app's new identifier (`com.player37.app`) starts with an empty webview storage anyway. Libraries from a browser come over as a shared library (partial sync is fine) or a .37p. **Found again:** with no list at all (a new install, or the app's own data folder emptied too), or a list whose folders aren't on the shell's list, the shell looks in the libraries' place for folders with a `37 Player library.json` (`fs_find_libraries`) and puts them on the list; renaming a library writes its name into the marker, so it comes back under it. A place chosen elsewhere is forgotten with the data folder, so for that, and any library elsewhere, Settings → Libraries has **Find a library folder…** (`fs_root_choose_library`: the system's folder dialog; the shell reads the marker and puts the folder on its list as that library's). **A folder that can't be found** (a drive not plugged in, a folder moved) is asked about before the library opens (`ensureDesktopFolder`: Try again / Find a library folder… / Open another library), never made again empty: `desktopMade` in the list says the folder existed. **A library's own small values** in localStorage (its shared library's sync log, `syncPendingSince`, `dailyAsk`, `autoRestoreDeclined`, `autoRestoreSent`) are kept in its folder too, as `37 Player's own files/local.json` (`libraryMirrorKeys`, through the same `setItem` hook as device.json), and put back as the library opens if the webview lost them; crash markers aren't. **Also:** the device's own records (the converter) live in the webview's database `37player-device` in the desktop app (a cache, fetched again when missing), not in the main library's folder. A folder library's "37 Player's own files" is now found whoever opens the folder (another library's store, which has no recording names, looked at the top of the folder: copying tracks into a library kept that way would have written beside its records); a new library made from another window gets that layout too (`ownFiles`). **Shell** (bridge2 branch): `fs_place`, `fs_place_default`, `fs_find_libraries`, `fs_root_choose_library`, `fs_root_default` making folders in the place; the stand-in shell has them (its dialog is `__fakeShellPick`; a new one starts with the place chosen unless `__fakeShellNoPlace`). **Test:** `tests/desktoplibs.js` (the question; folders, readable names, a 3 MB recording read where it lies, nothing in browser storage; the webview wiped, the sync log and a reminder back from local.json; the data folder wiped too; a folder moved to another drive; Find a library folder… in Settings; Choose another place… on a second computer).
- **`sync.php cleanup` for one library, and a dry run** (sync.php 1.4.197). kr37 ran `php sync.php cleanup rinzin` to free the droplet of test WAVs: the word was read as 0 days (`intval`), and only the file permissions (run as himself, not www-data) stopped it removing all but the newest 300 versions and every file they didn't use. Now `php sync.php cleanup [library] [days] [--versions N] [--dry-run]`: each word is checked (an unknown one is refused), a library's name limits it to that library, `--versions` lowers the newest-N floor (300), `--dry-run` only reports; each library's line says how much it frees and names files over 100 MB; a file less than an hour old is never removed (an upload in pieces under way, with 0 days); it stops at once if it can't change the files (run it as `sudo -u www-data`). A library with no `logs/` folder no longer warns. **2.2.001** (8 Nov 2026): three of the droplet's libraries (ikrcgc, kmctx, tharpaland) were owned by `rinzin`, made with `php sync.php create` run as himself, so the web server (`www-data`) couldn't save into them; `create` and `newcode` now refuse to run as anyone but `www-data` (the fix for those: `sudo chown -R www-data:www-data /var/lib/37player/<library>`), and cleanup says so for such a library and goes on with the others. **2.2.002** (kr37): the summary's total counts older versions and every unused file (recordings, covers, waveforms, unfinished `.part` uploads); under it, "Files of 100 MB or more that would be deleted" (or "were deleted"), and with `--list`, "Recordings that would be deleted", every one, then one line for the rest (older versions, covers, waveforms). (Note: sync.php has no shares yet: "Cleanup must respect shares" below is for when sharing by link is built.) **`php sync.php list`** (1.4.198, kr37) has two more columns: **newest**, the space the newest version needs (its record and every file it uses), and **all**, everything the library's folder takes; the difference is about what cleanup can free. Freeing a big recording: delete its track in the app, then clean up with few enough days and versions that no kept version still lists it.
- **The device's settings kept by the desktop app's shell** (1.4.192, step 2 of 2.0; desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md, "Everything the page keeps in browser storage"). kr37's test for 2.0: delete the app's webview storage (CCleaner), start again, nothing missing. The shell keeps `device.json` in the app's data folder (`device.rs`: only known keys, string values, written beside and renamed, the one before kept as `device.json.bak`) and hands it to the page before it runs (`window.__p37Device`, a plugin init script). The page (`DEVICE_MIRROR_KEYS`: the list of libraries, the device's settings, the Library table's columns, storage kind, automatic restore, Drive sign-in, the playback log) puts back any of them missing from localStorage before anything reads them, says so in the playback log, and sends all of them to the shell a second after any changes (`Storage.prototype.setItem` watched in the app only). An older app without `p37_device_put` simply ignores it. **A key the app refuses is left out** (2.5.046, `deviceMirrorRefused`). Shell 2.5.3 doesn't know `37player.install`, which the page sends too, and refused the whole write for it. kr37's MacBook's device.json had stopped changing at the first start (14 Nov), so its playback log was only in the webview's storage. Now the page drops the key the app names ("not a device key: …") and sends the rest again. The shell itself takes `37player.install` from shell 2.5.4 on, and leaves out keys it doesn't know without refusing the rest. tests/noloop.js, tests/desktop.js (the stand-in shell keeps device.json in sessionStorage, and a `localStorage.clear()` stands for CCleaner).
- **Two copies of one recording** (1.4.190, kr37's five cases, 6 Nov 2026). With no shared history (joining with tracks already here, or rejoining), a track both have with different recording revisions is decided before merging (`sharedReconcileAudio`, only on what's merged; `remote.doc` stays the server's so the result is saved): (1) the same recording (same size, the same first and last 64 KB, read with Range requests): this device takes the shared revision, nothing moves; (2) the shared revision isn't on the server (`op=list`): this device's copy wins and is sent; (3) the server's copy is damaged (wrong size, or starts like a .zip or a doubled WAV): this device's wins; (4) both good and different: the newer revision wins (`revTime`: a revision begins with `Date.now()` in base 36; device clocks are trusted), this device's older copy kept a day in Undo's trash (there's no button to bring it back yet); (5) this device's copy missing or damaged: the shared library's. A copy kept from before joining with no revision gets one, so the change is plain. Each decision goes in the sync log. Can't list the server: as before (the shared library's). And in the download pass, a track the shared library is waiting for **this** device to send, when this device has a good copy, takes a new revision from that copy and is sent (the Mac mini's case after rejoining). tests/reconcile.js. Help: Shared library → Two copies of one recording.
- **Never waiting for itself** (1.4.189; the Mac mini, 6 Nov 2026: its sending of a 1.35 GB rewrite kept failing, then the track's audio was replaced there, and after leaving and rejoining the shared library a track said "waiting for SKBC Mac Mini to send it" on the Mac mini itself). The shared library's record named this device as the sender of a copy no one has. When the device named is this one, the row now says it was to send this copy but no longer has it: replace its audio or delete the track. tests/miss.js. (1.4.190 then uses this device's own copy where it has one; see Two copies of one recording.)
- **Web Audio kept running while a turned-up track plays** (1.4.188; the Mac mini, 1.4.186: while a 1.2 GB WAV was imported, the playing track went silent, its time still running, until the next track's play started the context again). A track turned up (Loudness, or its own volume above 0 dB) plays through `levelCtx`; its statechange handler only answered "suspended", not WebKit's "interrupted". Now any state but running restarts it while such a track plays, and a watch checks every second (`levelRoutedPlaying`), saying so once in the playback log. tests/levelwatch.js. Not yet known what stopped it: the import's analysis makes short-lived AudioContexts for decoding (OfflineAudioContext would be safer there).
- **Big recordings go up in pieces** (1.4.187; the Mac mini, 6 Nov 2026: a 1.35 GB WAV failed with "Load failed" again and again, and WebKit's page restarted, the "white flashes"). Audio of 48 MB or more goes to a droplet library as 16 MB pieces (`sharedSendInPieces`; `PUT op=file&…&at=N&total=T`), which `sync.php` (1.4.187, `put_piece`) appends to `<name>.part` only when it holds exactly N bytes, answering how much it has; at T it becomes the file. A lost piece is sent again, and a break carries on from what the server has. Needs sync.php 1.4.187 on the droplet; with an older one, files go whole as before. Google Drive libraries are unchanged. `cleanup` removes stale parts. tests/piecesup.js.
- **Very big recordings aren't rewritten on WebKit** (1.4.187, kr37 agreed): a rename or other detail change doesn't rewrite the details inside a recording over 256 MB in WebKit's browser storage (`BIG_REWRITE_BYTES`): that read it all into memory, and in a shared library sent all of it again. The library's details, a folder's file names and exports are current; other players reading the file see its old details. Rewriting in place, piece by piece, in a folder (bridge 2) can come later.
- **Merging keeps this library's details, if you say so** (1.4.187, kr37): merging a .37p or backup in which tracks already here have other details (name, artist, album, number) or another copy of the recording lists them and asks: **Keep this library's** (suggested) or **Take the file's**. Before, merging always took the file's (and its audio), so an older .37p undid a rename. tests/p37.js.
- **Reading a desktop crash** (6 Nov 2026): the Mac app crashed once opening the file chooser (inside wry's open panel, a Rust panic, so an abort) while it played, uploaded and rewrote a 1.3 GB file; it didn't happen again when idle. The app is stripped, so from shell 1.4.187 each Mac test build carries its symbols beside it (`37-Player-mac-test-symbols.zip`, .dSYM, `split-debuginfo = "packed"`): `atos -o <dSYM>/Contents/Resources/DWARF/player37-desktop -arch x86_64 -l <load address> <addresses>` reads a report.
- **Feedback from the desktop app** (1.4.187): the page isn't at a web address there, so it sends to `https://37player.com/feedback.php`, which lets in `tauri://localhost` and `https://tauri.localhost` (with CORS headers; any other site is still refused). Needs feedback.php 1.4.187 on the droplet.
- **A recording that isn't one** (1.4.186): a stored "recording" that starts like a .zip, or with Safari's doubled-WAV pattern, counts as damaged on every engine (`audioLooksWrong`), so it's never sent to a shared library or exported (19 whole-zip "WMA"s from the Mac had reached the droplet library, where size and data agree). Not the MP3 two-ID3 pattern: good files have it too (dbl.js). Damaged files offers Repair only where the old file is inside (`looksDoubled`); otherwise it says at once that it can't be repaired.
- **The page's height in the desktop app** (1.4.184): on the Mac mini (Monterey's WebKit in the Tauri window), `100vh`/`100dvh` came out 28px (the title bar) taller than `innerHeight`, so the bottom of each screen, the Library's player too, was cut off, though the window itself fit the screen (`fit_window`). In the desktop app `html.fit-height` sizes `#app` from `--app-h`, set from `window.innerHeight` at start and on resize. Measured with a console line kr37 ran in the app (right-click → Inspect Element works in test builds).
- **The desktop app's page updates itself** (built 6 Nov 2026; `desktop/PAGE-UPDATES.md`): the shell (`pages.rs`) fetches pages signed by `.github/workflows/page-release.yml` (main → `page-latest`, dev → `page-test` for test builds), keeps the newest two in its data folder, and serves the chosen one as the app's own `index.html`/`help.json` (same address, same storage). Used from the next start, or at once with Update now (the window reloads). A page that doesn't call `p37_page_ok` within a minute, or over two starts, is put aside and the previous one opens (`swReverted` says so). The page (1.4.182) carries `<meta name="desktop-bridge">`; a page needing a newer shell isn't used and Check for updates says to download the new app. Off until kr37's key pair exists (`page-key.pub` says "none").
  - **Update now that didn't switch** (2.5.017, 15 Nov 2026). kr37's Mac mini (shell 2.5.0, bundled 2.5.000) offered 2.5.016, and each Update now reloaded into 2.5.000 without a word: the kept 2.5.016 had lost `index.html.sig` (the daily check and Check for updates ran together and shared one temporary folder), so the shell refused it, while `ready` still named it. Fixed in the shell (2.5.1, the `desktop` branch's NOTES). The page now reloads only when `p37_page_switch` names a different version; otherwise it says so (`desktopSwitchFailed`), stops offering Update now, and the playback log has the line. Check for updates writes its answer to the playback log too ("checked for a newer page: ready 2.5.016"), so a report shows what the shell said. Test: desktop.js.
- **What the numbers mean** (kr37, 6 Nov 2026). First: big eras; 2 = the desktop app keeping libraries as files through the shell. **2.0.000 is the first build whose page uses the shell's file commands (bridge 2)**, when the shell really does something; kr37 doesn't download x.0.0 software himself, so friends get 2.0.001 or later, with the first fixes in. Second: a new bridge from then on ("2.1 needs the new app"), or a notable set of features; a shell change that leaves the bridge alone (window, menu, closing) doesn't move it. Third: everything else. Until 2.0.000, 1.4.x carries on: the only bridge change ahead is bridge 2 itself. **Past versions** (kr37, 6 Nov 2026, to compare old and new behaviour): `.github/workflows/tag-versions.yml` tags each version on main (`v1.4.181`), and on its first run every past one from 1.1.088, so any version's `37-player.html` can be downloaded from GitHub without git (opened from disk, it has its own browser storage). The proxy in Claude's sessions refuses tag pushes, hence a workflow. Old versions aren't a way back for people: one-way paths such as Move to a folder stay in the app. The app's identifier will be `com.player37.app` (kr37, 6 Nov 2026; hidden, it names the app's data folder; set before friends' tests, never changed).
- **Phone columns are shares of the screen** (1.4.053–1.4.055). At 900px and under, Edit Playlists' and Library's columns are percentages of the screen (`--nw900`, and `--nw600` on a portrait phone), kept per device in `settings.narrowColumnWidths`, one set per band; the row scrolls sideways with snapping. A line sizes only the column to its left, 20–90% (`NARROW_MAX`), so its line and a sliver of the next column always show; the last column gets a phone-only line (`.narrow-only`). Snapping is off while a finger is on a line. Computers keep pixel widths traded between neighbours.
- **Reloads keep the look and the place** (1.4.052). A script right after `<body>` applies the device's theme from localStorage before the first paint (a light theme used to flash dark). On `pagehide` the tab, the Settings section or the Help topic go into sessionStorage (`PLACE_KEY`) and come back after a refresh or library switch, through `requestAppMode`, so a PIN is still asked.
- **Dropping a folder** (1.4.155; found in the Tauri spike): a folder dragged from the file manager onto the Library tab, a playlist or its drop area imported nothing, in every browser: `dataTransfer.files` holds the folder itself, and the import keeps only audio. Now `takeDrop` reads folders through `webkitGetAsEntry` (taken during the drop event, the only time a page may; read after): every file, sub-folders included, with the path the folder picker would give (`webkitRelativePath`), in path order with numbers compared as numbers. On the Library tab that goes to the import review like a picked folder; on a playlist, straight in. Test: tests/dropfolder.js (stand-in entries shaped like the browser's).
- **The first Play after a start, in the desktop app** (2.5.004; kr37's T470, 14 Nov 2026: "the play button doesn't respond to the first couple of clicks; at about the third it behaves"). `placeAwarePlay` waited for `placeFileThere`, whose `placeRefs()` reads every track's record once to learn which are used in place: seconds for his 551 tracks. Meanwhile the button stayed a triangle, a second click queued another play, and a `play()` so long after its click can be refused. In a bare WebKitGTK window the player itself starts at once. Now, while that isn't known yet, the track starts inside the click and is looked at after (a file used in place that has gone stops and says so), and `placeRefs()` is read ahead 2.5 s after the shell answers. A refused `play()` there is now in the playback log. Test: firstplay.js (fails without it).
- **The desktop app converts big files like any other** (2.5.001; kr37's 8 GB Mac mini, 13 Nov 2026, the test the lazy converter of 2.2.039 was waiting for). A 2.53 GB WAV imported, converted, played and sent to a shared library at once: the webview's memory about 300 MB importing, 210 MB sending, 650 MB at most; memory pressure green, briefly yellow after. Before 2.2.039 the same file took 4–6 GB and macOS restarted. **What he noticed:** with "convert uncompressed files" on, the WAV began uploading as it was, and only after he converted it by hand (through "This file is big") did the MP3 replace it: big files (over 500 MB) were left out of conversions 37 Player starts itself, since reading one whole was the danger. Now, in the desktop app, where a file is converted from the disk a block at a time (`bigFilesConvertFromDisk`: desktop, and this webview hasn't shown it can't), a big file is converted by itself after an import, without the warning, and so isn't sent first (`convertSoon`). If the webview turns out not to read that way, a big file nobody was asked about is left as it is, never read whole (`converterBigUnasked`). Browsers keep the warning and the asking. Test: bigfile.js.
- **The desktop app updated itself on a Mac** (13 Nov 2026, kr37's Mac mini): 44d (shell 2.3.0), installed by hand over 2.2.1, offered 2.5.0 by itself within minutes, and "Update and restart" brought 2.5.0 up. The Size setting was blurred above the smallest on the Mac too before shell 2.4.0, and is sharp with it; Settings and dialogs follow. The Dock kept the old pinned icon beside the running one after the update.
- **About is also Help's last topic** (2.5.006, 14 Nov 2026; kr37: people without the PIN can't open Settings, so they couldn't see the version). Just above Send feedback in Help's list, made by the app, not from `help/*.md` (`HELP_ABOUT_ID`, in `renderHelpScreen`), since it says what's running here: the version, where it runs from (website, local copy, or the desktop app and its version), the converter's version, Check for updates, the link to 37player.com. One function fills it and Settings → About (`aboutSectionInto`); Help's leaves out the second Send feedback button. The ☰ About card's About button (Edit Playlists and Library) now opens Help's About, not Settings'. Settings → About is unchanged. Test: helpabout.js.
- **The Linux app's seekbar ran a second ahead of the sound, for a track turned up** (2.5.005, 14 Nov 2026; kr37's T470 on 2.5.004: "a lag of most of a second before the music starts, but the seekbar is already progressing"; a pause took as long to be heard, and after it the two agreed; not in Chrome). Only with the player routed through Web Audio (Loudness, or a track's own volume above 0 dB; his log has "player now turns tracks up through Web Audio"). **Reproduced in a bare WebKitGTK window** (2.52, PipeWire 1.0 and PulseAudio, a track of beeps every 2 s recorded from a null device): the sound starts on time (0.2 s after play), but within 50 ms of a start the clock reads 1.05 s: the engine hands about a second of sound ahead to Web Audio at once and counts it as played. It stays 1.15 s ahead until a pause (sound runs on for that second, then both stop together; 0.1 s after). The same after any seek, playing or paused. Without Web Audio: 0.02 s. Not the latency hint (the same with "interactive" and none), not cured by waiting for `canplaythrough`, by a pause-and-play at once, or by seeking to 0 (which left the clock at 0 for the rest of the track). **Cure** (`holdClockForSound`, set up when an element is routed, only in the Linux app: `desktopFilesHttp()`): 250 ms after `playing`, `seeked` or `play` it compares the clock with where it should be (0 after `loadstart`, else the time at the last `seeking` or `pause`); 0.25 s or more ahead, the speed goes to 0 for that long (at most 1.5 s), which stops the clock and leaves the sound already handed over playing, then back to 1. Once per start or seek; a pause, seek or new track during it lets go. Speed 0, not `pause()`: nothing else in the page sees a pause (the button, the lock screen, the log). Measured with the app's own function in that window: 0.10 to 0.15 s ahead after a start, a seek while playing and a seek while paused (PipeWire), 0.25 s (PulseAudio); a steady tone recorded through it has no gap. While held the engine shows the time of the sound itself, then steps to the clock: a jump of about a second in the first second, on the seekbar a hair. The playback log says "the clock was 1.0 s ahead of the sound: held until the sound caught up". **Not done:** the previews (Library, Edit Playlists, duplicates) get it too if routed, untested by ear; a track's last second: `ended` comes when the clock ends, which with this is when the sound does (before, the next track will have cut the last second of a turned-up one; not measured). Mac and Windows are untouched. Test: clocklead.js (Chrome has no leap, so `__fakeClockLead` says how far ahead; `__clockLeadTest` sets it up outside the Linux app).
- **Recordings in the Linux app come over http://127.0.0.1** (2.5.000, shell 2.5.0; kr37's T470, 13 Nov 2026). What he heard: many tracks "skipping": the right length, but the first stretch was sound from the middle of the track with split-second dropouts, then the same part again; fine in Chrome and VLC, Loudness off, smallest Size. The file was an ordinary CBR MP3 (no Xing header, a 900-byte tag). **Reproduced in a bare WebKitGTK window** (2.52, GStreamer 1.24; nothing of 37 Player's in the page), recording a null sound device and matching it against the decoded file: from a **blob:** address the clock ran 0→35 s while the sound came from 68, 22, 11 and 80 s; from **p37lib://** the player refuses at once ("FormatError": WebKitGTK's player takes only http(s) and blob, so recordings over 32 MB, which went that way, couldn't play on Linux at all); from **http** it plays correctly, seeking included, also from a page at a custom-scheme origin, and through Web Audio when the element has `crossOrigin = "anonymous"` (silent without). So on Linux the shell runs a small server on 127.0.0.1 (a port of its own, a secret first path part new at each start, the same roots and checks as p37lib, Range, straight from the disk: `fileserver.rs`) and says where in `p37_shell_info` (`files_http`); the page uses it for every recording read from a library's folder (`desktopHttpUrlFor`: a whole DiskBlob, or a File from `DesktopFileHandle.getFile`, which now remembers its place in `p37at`), doesn't keep such an address between plays (it names the file's place, which a rename changes), and (2.5.000 only) `applyLevel` marked the element cross-origin. Mac and Windows have no `files_http` and play as before. 2.5 by the minor-version rule (the page uses something new in the shell). **2.5.000 never used it for real tracks (found 14 Nov, fixed 2.5.003).** `FolderLibraryStore` hands a stored file back as a *new* File when its record has a name or a type, as every imported track's does, and `p37at` was lost there: his log (2.5.002 said how each track reached the player) had every track "from memory". So what he heard on 2.5.0 and 2.5.002 was still the blob fault. fileshttp.js passed because the test copy reads files from 1 MB up where they lie (DiskBlobs, which carry their place); it now reads them whole as the real app does, and fails on 2.5.002. 2.5.002's change (no cross-origin mark, no Web Audio route in the Linux app) rested on that false evidence and is undone: the mark is set only for a track that is to be turned up. The log's lines stay (`desktopPlayerDiagnostics`). **Confirmed on his T470 with 2.5.003** (14 Nov): no dropouts, every track from the right place, each "from the app's local address" in the log, through Web Audio too. **Not covered:** a file not yet in a library (an import's check, the splitter's source before import) is still a blob there; a recording renamed while it plays may stop at the next seek. Tests: fileshttp.js; fileserver.rs's own; by hand, `cargo test --lib fileserver -- --ignored` serves a folder for a WebKitGTK window.
- **Taking a library off the list, and the undecided device** (2.4.003, kr37, 13 Nov 2026).
  - **A library kept in a folder is only forgotten**, and the wording now says so: the row's button is **Remove from list…** (not Delete…), and the question says its folder and everything in it, recordings included, stay, and that Find a library folder… brings it back (`libForget*`; always in the desktop app, and for a folder library in Chrome). A library in a browser's own storage is still deleted, with the old wording.
  - **A bug found on the way:** in the desktop app, removing the *first* library (ID `main`) went through `deleteLibrary`'s clearing for the main library, which emptied its folder's records (state, track and artwork records; 15 files to 7 in the test). Now the desktop app only forgets, whichever library. A browser wasn't affected (the list entry is gone by then, so the store opened is the browser's own).
  - A library taken off the list isn't put back by a later search of the libraries' place (`reg.deleted`); a first start lists all.
  - **Is this another device?** has a third button, **Decide later (it won't sync until then)**, since closing it left it unclear whether it was syncing; and while undecided the top bar says **Not syncing** (`sdHeldIndicator`, with Settings' sentence as its tooltip), not "In sync" or "In a folder".
  - Tests: desktoplibs.js (fails on 2.4.002 for the first library), copied.js.
- **Files promised and never sent** (kr37, 13 Nov 2026: "those unsent files live forever on all devices that are on full sync"). A track is in the shared library's list as soon as its device syncs; its audio follows. If that device leaves, has its library deleted, or is renamed, every other device shows "Waiting for X to send it" for good. What existed: the named sender recovering from its own copy (1.4.190, by its name), and a leaver removing, on its own side, tracks that never arrived to it. Any device holding a file in the listed version already sends it whatever `audioFrom` says (`sharedUploadFiles`). The plan, three parts:
  1. **Any device that has the very recording sends it** (2.4.002): the recovery in `sharedDownloadPendingHere` no longer needs this device to be the named sender, when its stored copy is the size the shared library lists, to the byte.
  2. **A leaving device takes back its promises** (2.4.002): the Leave question says how many of its tracks were never fully sent (`sharedUnsentHere`: held here, in the listed version, not on the server), and leaving removes them from the shared library first (`sharedWithdrawUnsent`: the doc without them and their playlist rows, saved against its version, retried on 409); they stay in the leaver's library. Offline, leaving goes ahead and the others wait as before. A device that had edited such a track brings it back (the merge's "edit wins"). Test: leaveunsent.js.
  3. **Not built yet: "Stop waiting"** for a sender that vanished without leaving. kr37's decisions: in Library cleanup (less likely to be accidental), with a link from the affected tracks in Library; and **a sender that comes back must not lose its tracks**: they're sent again. So the removal has to be marked as given up on, not deleted, for the returning device to tell the two apart and put its tracks back. **Their places in playlists become Stops** (his choice, later that day), named for the track, so it's plain that a practice is missing a piece; the returning sender's track goes back in place of its Stop.
- **The bar names the file being downloaded** (2.4.001, kr37, 13 Nov 2026): "Shared library: downloading 1 of 2: <track's name>" (`sharedStatus.transfer.name`); a long download gave no way to tell which file it was.
- **The Size setting in the desktop app is the webview's zoom** (2.4.000, shell 2.4.0; kr37, 13 Nov 2026). In a browser, sizes above the smallest magnify `#app` with a CSS transform (`--ui-scale`; chosen over CSS zoom, which made scrolling stop short of the bottom). On his T470 the .deb drew that blurred (WebKitGTK; sharp at the smallest size, where nothing is magnified; his iPad looks fine, so not all of WebKit), and Settings, Help and dialogs, outside `#app`, never followed the size, which he wanted. In the desktop app the shell now zooms the webview (`p37_zoom`, as Ctrl-plus in a browser) and `--ui-scale` stays 1 (`desktopShellZooms`, `desktopZoom` in `applyDisplaySettings`; `currentZoom()` reads `--ui-scale`, so coordinates follow). Chosen over sizing everything in font-relative units (sharp everywhere, Safari too, but most of the stylesheet) as the more coherent within one app: everything grows together and narrow-window rules go by the real room. Whether the shell can is its `zoom` in `p37_shell_info`, kept in localStorage `37player.shellZoom` so the next start zooms at once; an older shell keeps the transform. 2.4 by the minor-version rule (a new shell command the page calls). **Not done:** in a browser, dialogs, Settings and Help still don't follow the size; whether the Mac app was blurred before isn't known. Tests: shellzoom.js.
- **Wrap wraps the album too** (2.3.005, kr37, 13 Nov 2026): in track rows (Playing, Edit Playlists) with Wrap and Album both on, the album column kept one line at up to 170px and didn't shrink, so on a narrow window the title was two characters wide and many lines tall. With Wrap on it wraps and takes at most 28% of the row, so the title keeps the larger share. Test: wrapalbum.js.
- **Apple Lossless is converted wherever it's imported** (2.3.003, kr37, 13 Nov 2026). It plays in WebKit (Safari, iPads, the Mac app) but not in Chrome, Edge or the Windows app, so where WebKit imported it, a shared library's other devices got a file they couldn't play. The two ways weighed: convert at import on every device (an Apple-only library gets conversions it didn't need, its originals kept), or each device converts what it receives (the same file converted on several devices, and sent back or kept apart). Chosen: at import, one conversion where the original is. `isAppleLosslessFile` (the first `stsd` entry's codec, in the first or last 8 MB) puts an imported track in `alacImported` when this browser can play it, and `convertAfterImport` converts those along with the policy's, whatever the policy (the mark stays until `applyConvertedAudio` has put the MP3 in place, since the queue asks `trackCanBeConverted` again: 2.3.004; 2.3.003 marked them and converted nothing); it isn't marked unplayable, so if the converter can't be had it still plays here. Tracks imported before stay as they are. Test: conv3 (in WebKit, the whole path; in Chrome, the detector).
- **The Mac app is signed** (2.3.002, 13 Nov 2026): the Install guide (`sgWarnMac`), the Setup help page, the home page and desktop-latest's notes no longer say the Mac app is unsigned (builds since 39d are signed with Developer ID and notarized: macOS only asks whether to open an app from the internet). Windows' still isn't signed, and still says so. 37player.com's downloads (desktop-latest) were build 35m, from before signing, on 13 Nov: the wording needs a signed m build promoted with it (desktop-latest.yml by hand, kr37's choice).
- **Pause after a passing error** (2.3.001, found by the WebKit tests, 13 Nov 2026): `watchPlaceDrive` marked a player "stuck" (`data-p37stuck`) on any error event, but only a track used in place ever had the mark taken off (`placeAwarePlay`), so after one passing error Pause, the space bar and the lock screen's pause all played instead (`togglePlay` reads the mark), until a reload. WebKit (Safari, iPads, the Mac app) reports such errors more readily. Now only a track used in place, in the desktop app, is marked (after `placeRefs()`, since the cache can be empty), and `playing` or `loadstart` takes the mark off. Test: stuckpause.js (fails on 2.3.000).
- **The Linux app on the home page and in the Install guide** (2.3.000, kr37, 13 Nov 2026, after the .deb worked on his T470): on Linux the guide offers the desktop app as on a Mac or Windows (`desktopAppOffered`): **Download .deb** (Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12 or newer and what's built on them: WebKitGTK 4.1 is the floor) and **Download .rpm** (Fedora, openSUSE), with how to install either; "I can't install apps on this computer" still leads to Chrome's way. Linux on ARM, when the browser says so, has no build: Chrome's way. The builds make both packages (`tauri build --bundles deb,rpm`), and desktop-latest carries `37-Player-linux-amd64.deb` and `37-Player-linux-x86_64.rpm`. Two packages because between them they cover most Linux computers; Arch and its kin (AUR, debtap) and Flatpak/AppImage left out (kr37: avoid Flatpak and AppImage). The .rpm hasn't been tried on a Fedora computer yet. The Linux app doesn't update itself (Tauri's updater on Linux is for AppImage only); its pages do, so the guide says to download it again now and then. Test: setup.js.
- **Copies of one library, and the first start's note** (2.2.041, shell 2.2.6; kr37's T470, 13 Nov 2026, the first run of the .deb). Music/37 Player Libraries held two folders with the same library ID: the one in use, and an older copy from 9 Nov made with a file manager, read-only (folders `dr-xr-xr-x`). `fs_find_libraries` took the first by name, which was the copy; when the library opened, its folder was renamed to follow the library's name, and with that name taken it became "Rz restored library (2)", which looked like a library the app had made; then every save failed with "Permission denied (os error 13)", and the message blamed a full device (22 GB free). Now: the shell's `Found` carries `writable` (no read-only folder among the library's, its own files' and its records' folders) and `changed` (its `state/root.json`'s time), and of copies it takes one that can be saved in, then the newest (`best_copy`); the page asks **Copies of “…”** with each folder's date and a read-only mark (`askWhichLibraryCopy`; closing it keeps the shell's choice; the choice is `fs_root_use_found`, only a folder in the libraries' place holding that library). A folder isn't renamed beside a copy of the same library that has the name (`name_held_by_copy`). A refused save (`isDeniedError`: EACCES, EROFS, Windows' access denied, the browser's NotAllowedError) says 37 Player isn't allowed to save in the library's folder, names the folder (`libraryFolderShown`) and suggests permission or another copy (`notSavingDeniedBody`). The libraries found at a first start are said in a note that stays, naming them and saying that a shared library may ask whether this is another device (it's the same device when 37 Player also runs in a browser there); the toast it replaces was missed under the first start's own messages. **Still so:** the shell looks only directly inside the libraries' place, and a folder without a marker (a pre-2.0 backup) isn't a library. Tests: desktoplibs.js (copies), denied.js, files.rs `of_two_copies_the_one_that_can_be_saved_in`.
- **Folder import** (1.4.035–1.4.045): "Make a playlist of each folder" builds ordinary playlist plans (`folderPlaylistPlans`), ordered by track number at commit time. Playlists whose audio isn't all there get one choice in the review (all / only complete / none). Stop leads to a dialog: carry on (the loop resumes from `nextIdx`), or finish with complete / all / no playlists. Full storage (`isQuotaError`) ends an import at once; `commitFolderImport` wraps the whole import so its dialog can never hang.
- **A full device** (1.4.060, found on kr37's iPad 22 Oct 2026). Every write then fails as an aborted transaction, and until now only the console knew: a renamed playlist looked fine and was gone after a reload, and a shared library's download pass fetched the same file every 30 seconds without ever storing it (with "receiving 1 files" as the only trace). Now a failed save (`saveState` → `noteSaveFailed`) shows **Device full: not saving** (or **Not saving**, naming the error) in red in the top bar, with a dialog the first time unless something is playing, and is retried every 30 s and when the app comes back on screen; the first save that works clears it (`noteSaveWorked`). Nothing is lost while the window stays open. A downloaded file that can't be stored stops the pass (`err.storing`) and receiving waits 2, 4, 8 … 30 minutes (`sharedStoreBackoff`; a tiny save can still work when a recording can't), or until Sync now; "received" is logged only once a file is stored, and other download failures are logged once per file and reason. A failed audio or artwork write takes back the new revision `idbPut` gave the track. `isQuotaError` also knows the usual "disk full" wordings. Safari on that iPad reported a 32.4 GB quota while the disk was full, so `storage.estimate()` can't warn ahead of time. Test: tests/full.js.
  - **Safari's error** (1.4.061, seen on the iPad): the write's transaction is aborted with no reason given (`tx.error` null). 37 Player never aborts a transaction itself, so `settleTx` marks such an abort (`noReason`) and `isQuotaError` counts it as full. A request's own AbortError no longer rejects with null before `onabort` gets to say so.
  - **Exports and backups on a full device:** a 243 MB "New full backup" ran through and then nothing was saved, and the page can't tell (a download that fails says nothing to the page). `exportScope` now tries a save first and, if the device is full, asks before building the file.
  - **Sync failures** in the sync log name the error and say "(this device is full)" when it is (1.4.062). What Settings and the bottom bar say fits the cause (1.4.064, `sharedFailText`): a full device; no answer at all ("Couldn't reach the shared library", the only message before, which on the full iPad pointed at the droplet); the server's error code; anything else with its error.
  - **Only storage that's really empty** (1.4.154; seen in the Tauri spike while making, joining and deleting libraries, and, kr37: a part of losing his backups when the T470's disk filled). A Replace restore and "Get the current shared library" clear the library record and write the new one later (a Replace copies all the audio in between); cut off there (a reload, a crash, a full disk), the next start found no record while the device's list said it had tracks, and said "Your library didn't load" with no way on. Now `idbClearAll(what)` writes a marker first (`REPLACING_KEY` "replacing": restore / rejoin), kept through the clear and removed by the first library write after; a start that finds it and no library record opens the library empty and says so (`interruptedReplace`: "A restore didn't finish … Restore the backup again", or join again). And `storageLooksUsedBefore` says no when the state store still holds any record of this library's (name, settings, backup note; not the converter or copies waiting, which can be written while a library hasn't loaded): storage a browser lost or handed over empty holds nothing. A new library's name record is written the moment it's made (`createLibrary`), before the switch. Test: tests/interrupted.js.
  - **Storage that comes back empty** (1.4.065, seen 23 Oct 2026). On the full iPad, Safari couldn't open the site's storage and gave the page empty storage instead, with no error: the live app hung on a blank, dark Playing screen, then opened as "Nothing imported yet", one library, not joined. Once there was room it was all back, untouched (Safari's Website Data never changed). Importing, joining or restoring in between could have replaced the real library. Now:
    - **An empty library with signs of earlier use** (`storageLooksUsedBefore`: the device's list of libraries says it had tracks or was shared, or storage is persistent around a database created just now, since 37 Player asks for persistence every time it opens and a browser drops it only when it clears the site; that last sign only in WebKit since 1.4.181: Chrome lends an incognito window the persistence it gave the normal profile, so a fresh incognito window said "didn't load", kr37, 6 Nov 2026, `persisted()` true there) is treated as not loaded (`libraryNotLoaded`). One dialog that can't be dismissed, and comes back if anything closes it: "Your library didn't load … Don't import, join a shared library or restore a backup now …" with **Try again** (reload) and **Start with an empty library anyway** (asks once more; sessionStorage `37player.acceptEmpty` for the reload). Nothing is written meanwhile: `idbPut`, `idbDelete`, `idbDeleteMany` and `idbWriteLibraryIfCurrent` refuse, `doSaveState` returns, no starter track, no welcome, no files opened with the app, no syncing.
    - **A library meant to be new** (New library: `37player.newEmpty`, or a pending join) is never warned about.
    - **Storage that doesn't answer**: after 8 seconds, "This device's storage isn't responding", with Try again; gone by itself if it answers.
    - Test: tests/notloaded.js (deleting the database while localStorage stays, pretending persistence, an `indexedDB.open` that never answers). Whether Safari keeps reporting persistence while it hides the storage is still to be seen on the iPad.
  - **A download that closes the page** (1.4.066, seen 23 Oct 2026): on the iPad, one file closed 37 Player every time it was received, eight times in a row (opened, "receiving 1 files", gone a minute later); most likely a very big recording held whole in memory by `res.blob()`. Like `guardedAnalysis`, the file being received is noted first (localStorage `37player.downloading`, per library); a note still there at the next start means the page died: the sync log names it, and that file isn't received again by this version on this device (`37player.downloadCrashed`), not by the background pass nor by pressing play, until Sync now. A file over 50 MB is named with its size in the sync log as it starts. Works the same for the droplet and Google Drive (both go through `sharedFetchFileOnce`). Test: tests/dlcrash.js. The file was a 1.2 GB recording (the WAV that once hit Apache's 1 GiB upload limit).
  - **Receiving in pieces** (1.4.067, `sharedReceive`): files arrive 16 MB at a time (Range requests: sync.php answers them, `driveFetch` passes them to Google), each piece stored as state record `dlpart:<id.rev>:<n>` before the next is asked for, waiting between pieces while anything plays. A cut-off download carries on from the pieces already here; pieces of files no longer waiting are dropped at the start of each pass (`sharedDropStalePieces`). At the end the file is put together from the stored pieces (read back from storage, not held in memory), stored, and checked: readable length, and Safari's doubling pattern (1.4.016). If it came out wrong the old file is put back, the pieces go, the sync log suggests converting the file on another device, and it isn't tried again until Sync now. A file of one piece or less, or a server that sends the whole file, is read as before. Confirmed on the iPad (23 Oct 2026): the 1,186 MB recording arrived in pieces in under two minutes, three times over (renamed on purpose to make it travel again), and the crash loop of 1.4.059 ended with the update. Test: tests/pieces.js (256 KB pieces: whole, carried on after a cut, a size that doesn't add up).
  - **The top bar wraps at every width** (1.4.061): on the iPad, "has moved", "new in the shared library", the backup button and the warning ran off the right edge. The warning comes first among them.
- **Library → Table** (1.4.069): Browse | Table at the top of the Library page. Table shows every track as one sortable table (`renderLibraryTable`): Name (pinned while scrolling sideways), Artist, Album, Length, Type, Size, Bit rate, Sample rate, Channels, Loudness, In playlists, and, in a shared library, On this device; # and File name can be turned on (Columns). Click a heading to sort (numbers start largest first), ties by album, track number, name. The search box and chips filter it, with two chips of its own: Can't be played, Not on this device. Selecting is the browser's own `libSelectedTracks`, so ✎, Delete, Enter, Space, Ctrl-A and Export act on it unchanged; `renderLibraryTracks` hands over to the table while it's on. Only rows in view (± 30) are in the page (`libTableDraw`, padding rows above and below): all 2,000 rows of a big library took about a second to lay out; drawing the visible ones takes about 20 ms. View, sort and columns are kept per device (localStorage `37player.libTable`). Refined in 1.4.070: Browse | Table is the header's first item (`#libViewToggle`), so it doesn't move between the views; "missing artist, album or artwork" shows only in Table; Columns sits beside the totals line; selected tracks that all can't be played and can be converted get **Convert** in the bar above (`#libConvertSelBtn`), and a track's details (✎) say why it can't play, with **Convert it now**. The Library's player is one row (⏮ ▶ ⏭, name, time, the selection's buttons, Split track) above its waveform, wrapping on a narrow screen. Next: Library cleanup's lists as saved filters of this table. Test: tests/table.js.
- **Joining or making a shared library** (1.4.071, `openSharedJoinDialog`): one dialog for Settings → Shared library (whose not-joined page is now an introduction and **Join or create a shared library…**), the welcome's join step, and New library → Join a shared library, which had three slightly different copies of the same fields. Step 1, where it's kept (radio buttons): **I have a join code** (37player.com or another server; a new library there is made by whoever runs the server) or **Google Drive** (join or make one). Step 2, only what that needs: for a code, the code, this device's name, partial sync and the server address folded away; for Drive, what happens next and **Use Google Drive…** (the chooser after signing in asks the library, the device's name and partial sync). What Join does is the caller's: Settings may offer a new library first, the welcome carries on with setup, New library opens the new library and joins there (and no longer asks a name for it: it takes the shared library's). A failed join comes back to step 2 with the message and the code still filled in. `SHARED_WHERE_KINDS` is where pCloud would join. Tests: share, libs, e2e, drive. Joining from a library that has tracks (`askJoinWhere`, 1.4.072): "library" means two things there, the one on this device and the shared one, so the question names and counts both ("On this device, “My library” has 664 tracks. The shared library “IKRC” has 1,234."), having looked at the shared library with the code first, and offers radio buttons: **Make a new library on this device for “IKRC”** (recommended) or **Join from “My library”** (the next step then asks combine or replace). An empty shared library suggests joining from this one, to fill it. A wrong code goes back to the code; an unreachable server gets the plainer question. Partial sync is chosen once (1.4.073), at the step with the sizes (Use the shared library / Combine / Only what I choose, or Everything / Only what I choose): the "Bring only the playlists I choose" box beside the code, and in the Drive chooser, only pre-selected that step, so it asked twice. The code step's button says **Next** (1.4.074): joining is decided at the step after. That step's sending line names the library: "Sends everything from “My library” that isn't in the shared library yet" (one library of several on a device). Radio choices in dialogs (`label.export-format`) act as one button: no text selection, a hover highlight. **Next until it happens, then Do it now** (1.4.075): through joining, every go-ahead button says Next (where it's kept, the code, the Drive chooser, new library here or join from this one, New library's Join) and the step that actually acts says **Do it now** (Use the shared library / Combine / Only what I choose; Everything / Only what I choose; the size to confirm; New library → Start empty). New library's button switches with its radio. When there's nothing to transfer, joining happens straight after a Next, with no last step. The three-way question names the library on this device: "The library “My library” on this device already has tracks … On this device, “My library” has 3 tracks, and 2 of them are already in the shared library “Test”, which has 2."
- **Opening from the saved copy** (1.4.104, row 6; sw.js): the page opens at once from the copy the service worker keeps ("new" if one is waiting, else "good"), with no wait for the server, and the worker fetches the site's page in the background (`refreshFromServer`, 30 s limit). A different, complete version is saved as "new" and the open page is told (`37-update-ready`): the version at the top becomes two lines, the version and **Update now** (on a phone, the "Update to …" button). Update now asks the worker to fetch and save it (`37-fetch-now`) and reloads into it; otherwise it opens at the next start. All the 1.4.091 safety stays (a new version that doesn't start is refused and the good one opens). A hard reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) skips the worker; when that version starts, the worker adopts it as the good copy (`37-up`). Test: tests/swfallback.js (now about a minute longer: it waits out a server that doesn't answer).
- **Check library** (1.4.103, row 11; Library cleanup, first): a fixer meant to grow as 37 Player changes. It never touches names, artists, albums, artwork or track numbers (edits are already written into the files, so re-reading tags would mostly give back the same, and would undo edits in formats without a tag writer). Now: undated tracks get today's `addedAt`; every stored file is checked for damage (`checkStoredFiles`); tracks that look wrong (no length, loudness or sample rate, only the flat stand-in waveform — `isStandInPeaks`, since 1.4.107; before, a stand-in counted as a waveform and was skipped — or marked unplayable) are read again (`recheckStoredAudio`). A reading that gives a stand-in or failed loudness never replaces a real one; a track that plays but still has only the stand-in is reported on its own line (`chkNoWave`), not as fixed, and listed by name with its format (an .m4a's codec, or why it couldn't be read in pieces) and the reason, too long here or couldn't be decoded (`whyNoWaveform`, 1.4.109), with **Show them in the Library**. `isStandInPeaks` means 0.15 all along (the only stand-in value ever used), not just "all one value": a silent track's real waveform is all 0, and kr37's three silent tracks were listed as having none (1.4.110). Progress and Stop in the status bar; a summary at the end; state record `checkLibrary` keeps when it last ran. New checks go at the end of `checkLibrary`.
- **The status bar shows every job** (1.4.102, row 36): `activeOps` (a Map, key → {text, stop, dialog, stopping}) instead of one `activeOperation`; each job's part sits side by side with "|" and its own Stop. The key is the Stop function a job passes for its whole run, or a third argument (`"split"`, `"shared"`, `"rebuild"`, `"restoreAudio"`; a folder import uses its analysis object). `clearActiveOperation(key)` clears only that one; with no key, all. `activeOperation` is the newest, for old checks. While the bar shows, `#app` is shorter by its height (`--opbar-h`, 1.4.107), so every screen ends at the bar's top instead of under it (Edit Playlists' player, the Library's last row). Choose where a track starts (1.4.101, row 34): `selStart`, `drawStartWave`, `seekNewlyStarted`.
- **Added and Changed** (1.4.100, row 21): tracks carry `addedAt` and `changedAt` (ms), synced with the track. Stamped in `stampTrackDates`, from the comparison Undo already makes after each save (`undoNoteSaved`), so every way a track arrives or changes on this device is covered without touching each import path; the two fields are left out of Undo's comparison (`TRACK_DATE_FIELDS`), so stamping isn't a change of its own. A conversion stamps Changed too (`applyConvertedAudio`). What comes from the shared library or another window keeps its dates (those paths rebase Undo). Library → Table: Changed shows by default, newest first on a click; Added is under Columns. Tracks from before have no dates until Check library gives them one.
- **Converting carries on** (1.4.099, rows 13 and 17): the list waiting to be converted is kept in the library's own storage (state record `convertQueue`, saved after every file), and the next start of that library picks it up six seconds in (`scheduleConvertResume`), once nothing plays. A reload, switching libraries or a crash no longer loses it; Stop ends it. Switching libraries while sending audio or converting asks once, naming both (`libSwitchBusy*`). Test: tests/convresume.js.
- **Context toolbars** (1.4.097, rows 22–25 of kr37's 28 Oct list; mocked up first on a Design canvas, "37 Player context toolbars"). A thin bar of icon + word on each screen: the word beside the icon on Playing and Library, under it on Edit Playlists (a busier bar) and on any screen 600px or narrower. kr37 wants the words kept.
  - **Playing** (`buildPlayingCtxBar`, re-added by `renderPlayer` each draw): Wrap, Album, Even out, then Playback (Settings → Playback, asking for the PIN when locked: `withPin`).
  - **Edit Playlists** (`#edCtxBar`, across all four columns, above them): Undo, Redo | Wrap (1.4.147), Waveforms, Player (the bottom preview bar, device setting `showEdPreviewBar`) | Edit, Split | Copy, Paste (n), Delete | Silence, Stop. Column B lost its Copy/Delete/Paste buttons and its "+ Insert from library / silence / stop" row (the selection count stays). Column labels: A "Organize playlists here" (slate blue), B "Edit playlist here" (purple), C with D "Choose songs here" (plum; D says "and here" in the same colour).
  - **Library** (`.library-header`, now a bar; reworked 1.4.112 to kr37's layout): the top bar chooses what's shown: Browse | Table, search, **Show** (`renderLibShow`, a drop-down replacing the chips: All, Last import, Missing artist/album/artwork, Can't be played, Missing audio, Damaged files, Kept originals, Not on this device in a shared library; each with its count, those with none disabled; the same `libQuickFilter` in both views; Missing audio from the stored files' keys, `refreshLibMissingAudio`, not reading every file), Duplicates (Browse), and the totals (Table). Browse: each column has a heading and a bar under it (`libGroupTop`, sticky): Artists and Albums, Edit and Delete for those selected (Edit with several opens `mergeLibraryGroups`: pick one of the names or type one, all their tracks get it, tags rewritten; Undo undoes it; E does the same); Tracks (`#libTracksTop`): the selection's count · time beside the heading (`#libBrowseSelCount`, 1.4.113; kept current from `refreshCtxBars`, which every selection change calls), and on its bar Undo, Redo | Wrap, Player, Loudness | Edit, Split, Delete (1.4.148). Bars too narrow lose words (`fitCtxInline`: `.tb-view` ones first, here Undo/Redo, then all); on a phone the Tracks bar wraps. **The player is one line at the bottom of the page** (1.4.168, kr37, from a mockup; `#libPlayerBar`, styled like Edit Playlists' `.ed-preview-bar`): ⏮ ▶ ⏭, album · artist over the name, the time, the waveform; on a narrow screen the waveform takes a second line. The same in Browse and Table; it stays when nothing is selected (empty, or the track still playing); the Player button still hides it (kept so kr37 can try shapes of screen). The panel at the top of the tracks (`#libPreviewPanel`) now holds only what the selection offers (Convert, Done) and goes when there's nothing (`.only-player`). A selected artist or album that no longer exists (Undo of a merge) is dropped in `renderLibraryScreen`. With More Contrast, unavailable buttons are faded (opacity .38): its faint text colour was nearly white. Library cleanup: Show in Table links (`showInLibraryTable`). **Show → Kept originals** (1.4.115): Browse puts "Current: file" and "Original: file" under each name; Table shows, for length, type, size, bit rate, sample rate, channels, loudness and file name, one value when the original and current agree (centred in the row) and "c: …" over "o: …" when they differ. Conversions since 1.4.115 record the original's details in `tr.original` (`detailed: 1`); older kept originals have only name and size, and the rest shows as "unknown" (kr37: not worth reading each kept file to find out), as does anything an unplayable original never had (loudness). Rows are drawn whole (`free`) in this view. The first time it's chosen on a device, Type and File name are unhidden (`libTable.keptColsShown`). Missing audio in Show is the quick check (is there a stored file at all, from the store's keys) plus whatever Library cleanup's thorough check (`tracksWithMissingAudio`, which opens each file, so it also finds files there but unreadable) found when it last ran (`libUnreadableAudio`); Audio not in the library lists each file (`renderOrphanList`: tags, length, size, play, Add, Delete with a second press). Before 1.4.112: Undo, Redo | search | Edit, Split or Copy track, Delete | Duplicates, Loudness, Player. Browse's Player switch (1.4.111, `libTable.browsePlayer`, separate from Table's `libTable.player`; `libPlayerShown`) hides the play buttons, title and waveform but keeps the row with the chips and the count. Browse's Artists and Albums columns have headings (`.lib-col-head`, sticky, a faint slate tint; quieter than Edit Playlists'). The Library player's row lost Edit / Delete / Split track (count, Convert and Done stay). Browse puts the chips (Last import) on the player's row. In Table (1.4.107) the upper bar is about *what's shown*: Browse | Table, search, the chips, and the totals (tracks · size · time, `#libTableCount`) at its right; it has no actions. The lower bar (`buildLibTableViewControls`, above the player) is *how it's shown* and *what to do with the selection*: Fit / Scroll / Full, Wrap, Columns, Player, Waveforms | "4 selected · 15:23" (always there, `#libTableSelCount`) | Undo, Redo, Edit, Copy track, Delete. When it would wrap, `fitLibTableView` drops the words from the display half first (`.tight1`), then from all of it (`.tight2`); the words stay as tooltips. One row at 1000px and up. Keys (1.4.110): ↑/↓ (and Shift) go by `libTableIds`, the table's order as shown, not Browse's album order, and scroll it as little as needed (`libTableScrollTo`, which works with rows not drawn yet); Page Up/Down scroll a page less a row.
  - **How:** `fillCtxBar` builds a bar from defs (`{icon, label, tip, act, on(), enabled()}`), each screen rebuilds its bar as it draws (so the words follow the language), and `refreshCtxBars()` updates on/off/unavailable after a selection change without a redraw.
