Undo and Redo are the same two buttons as before, moved into whichever bar is showing (held by reference in `undoRedoEls`: clearing a bar to rebuild it takes them out of the page). The display switches that moved (wrap, album column, waveforms, loudness) are still in the ◐ menu's markup, hidden (`.popover-section.moved-out`); the bars click them, so their handlers and All settings are unchanged.
  - **Even out loudness:** Settings → Playback is now an on/off switch plus By track / By album; `root.settings.levelKind` remembers the kind while it's off, so the Playing bar's Even out only turns it on and off.
  - **Library → Table:** since `libTable.v` 2, type, size, bit rate, sample rate and channels start hidden (also on devices that had chosen columns before), and In playlists comes right after Length. Width: Scroll is how it always was (cells up to 300/360px, sideways scrolling), Fit squeezes the table to the window (`table-layout:fixed`), Full shows every name whole. With Wrap on, rows differ in height, so the table is drawn whole instead of only the rows in view (`libTableView.free`): fine for hundreds of tracks, slow for thousands.
- **Small changes of 28 Oct 2026 (1.4.095–1.4.096, 1.4.107):** Shared library → More has a ▸/▾ triangle, on the line of the word, and once open its contents sit behind a thin line on the left, so it's clear what came out of the fold; switching libraries while only converting (or only sending) names just that one in "carries on by itself" (`libSwitchBusyLaterOne`); re-sorting the Table keeps the selection and scrolls to it (the row clicked last, `libTableLastClicked`); Playing's playlists have no triangles, only the folder icon, with spacing that groups them (tightest within a wrapped title, the same between all rows since 1.4.133, folders included); "During playback" (whether converting and transfers run while playing) lives in Settings → Playback, with its value and a link in File format and Shared library → More (`idleSwitchHtml`, `idleLinkHtml`); a sideways swipe on the splitter's whole-track waveform moves at that waveform's scale. **L** in Edit Playlists shows the selected tracks in the Library (`showTracksInLibrary`: their artists and albums selected, the tracks highlighted). Where one track is needed and several are selected, Edit, the preview and Paste use the one clicked last (`selectionFocus`, marked with an amber edge).
- **The bar at the bottom** (importing, converting, syncing…: `#globalOperationBar`, fixed to the bottom) takes its height off `#app` (`--opbar-h`, set by `trackOperationBarHeight`), so every screen ends at its top rather than under it (1.4.107; before, it hid Edit Playlists' player and the Library's last rows). Settings, Help and toasts already sat above it.
- **Column widths to begin with:** Library's Artists 270px and Albums 300px (1.4.058), Edit Playlists' columns 210 / 480 / 210. Display settings (◐) → **Reset column widths** clears both the computer's and the phone's saved widths (1.4.057).
- **Deleting a library can delete its backup folder** (1.4.051): only 37 Player's files, and only while the folder's meta.json still names that library's copy. Manual backup files carry the library's name (`backupNamePart`).

## Browser quirks and workarounds

**Ways to run it, and what they really are** (6 Nov 2026, kr37 worried quirks get lost). Eight ways (with Android), but three engines, two places for the library, and a shell or not. Quirks belong to the engine or the storage, so code tests for what a browser *can do* (`window.showDirectoryPicker`, `WEBKIT_ENGINE`, `IN_DESKTOP_APP`), never for a device's name, and each quirk is written below under its engine.

| Way | Engine | The library is in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| iPad, on the Home Screen | WebKit | browser storage | first-class; no folders; touch |
| iPad, in a Safari tab | WebKit | browser storage | Safari may erase it after 7 days unused (37 Player warns) |
| Firefox, Safari on a computer | Gecko, WebKit | browser storage | works; no folders, no automatic backups |
| Chrome, Edge, Brave on a computer | Chromium | browser storage, or a folder (readable names) | first-class; Brave needs its File System Access flag |
| Desktop app, Mac | WebKit (the system's: Monterey's has no SIMD) | browser storage; a folder from bridge 2 | first-class |
| Desktop app, Windows | Chromium (WebView2) | browser storage; a folder from bridge 2 | first-class |
| Linux | Chromium (Chrome's installed app) | as Chrome | the desktop app on Linux would be WebKitGTK (playback through GStreamer plugins); not built |
| Android tablets and phones | Chromium (Chrome) | browser storage | no folders on Android; touch, as the iPad |

**WebKit, everywhere it runs** (Safari, iPads, the Mac desktop app; WebKitGTK too):
- IndexedDB stores a blob that is a slice of another, or made from slices, as the start of the whole original: re-tagged files (1.4.016, `putRewrittenAudio`), and tracks of an imported .zip (1.4.173, `webkitOwnBytes`). Anything stored there gets bytes of its own first.
- No WebAssembly SIMD before Safari 16.4, and the Mac desktop app on Monterey always has the old engine: the converter is built without SIMD (1.4.166).
- Timers in a hidden or covered window run late, so silences are timed by a silent audio clock (`runSilenceClock`, 1.4.158).
- No folder access (`showDirectoryPicker`) at all.

- **Clear a file input's value only after copying its files.** Chromium 141 empties the input's FileList in place when its value is cleared. Copy with `Array.from()` first.
- **Playlist files report as audio.** `.m3u8` reports as `audio/x-mpegurl`, so audio is picked by extension, never by MIME type.
- **Safari:**
  - it clears a site's data after 7 days without use, unless the app is on the Home Screen or Dock;
  - it uses dynamic viewport (`dvh`) heights;
  - the window's scroll position needs resetting, except while typing.
- **iPad touch:**
  - no hover, so row icons appear on selection;
  - drag and drop is synthesized from touch for the folder tree;
  - native `draggable` is turned off on coarse pointers.
- **Folder access:** `showDirectoryPicker` works only in Chrome and Edge on desktop. Brave needs `brave://flags` → File System Access. iOS Chrome is WebKit, so it has no folder access either.
- **Codecs:**
  - ALAC and WMA play in no browser, so they're converted with the scripts in `scripts/`.
  - What MediaRecorder writes as "AAC" varies by browser. Some write Opus, which `findMp4AudioCodecFourcc` catches.
- **Long recordings and memory:** decoding a whole file holds it all as raw samples (a 2-hour stereo teaching is about 2.5 GB), which crashed a phone's tab ("Aw, Snap!") and made a laptop give up with a flat stand-in waveform. Past a per-device budget, MP3s are decoded about 3 minutes at a time, cut at frame boundaries (`analyzeMp3InPieces`), and WAVs are read a few MB at a time with no decoder (`analyzeWavInPieces`); both feed `makePieceAnalyzer` / `makeLoudnessMeter`. Since 1.4.108, AAC in an .m4a/.mp4 too (`analyzeM4aInPieces`; kr37's long Zoom recordings had stand-ins and no loudness): `m4aAacFrames` reads the sample table (stsz, stsc, stco/co64) and the AudioSpecificConfig (esds), and about 3 minutes of frames at a time (45 s on a phone) each get a 7-byte ADTS header and go to `decodeAudioData` as plain .aac. Each piece after the first starts one frame early and drops that frame's sound (AAC overlaps frames); the edit list's lead-in (`elst` media_time) and its duration are honoured, so the result matches decoding the whole file exactly (tests/m4apieces.js). Apple Lossless and other non-AAC throw, keeping the stand-in; on phones an .m4a still decodes whole when it fits (it may be Apple Lossless, which Safari decodes but can't be cut this way). Since 1.4.114 also FLAC (`analyzeFlacInPieces`: a minimal header from STREAMINFO, total and MD5 left unknown, then frames; cut where a frame starts, found by sync code and header CRC-8), Ogg Vorbis and Opus (`analyzeOggInPieces`: the header pages, then whole pages starting with one that doesn't continue a packet), WebM/Matroska (`analyzeMkvInPieces`: EBML header and the Segment's children up to the first Cluster, without SeekHead, Cues and Void, the Segment's size left open, then whole clusters; open-sized clusters, as browsers record them, found by scanning for the Cluster ID followed by a Timecode) and plain ADTS AAC (`analyzeAdtsInPieces`, one frame of overlap). A decoder starting mid-stream may lose or add a little at a piece's start, so each piece is placed by the file's own timestamps (`makeAlignedAnalyzer`: its end lands where the granule, cluster timecode, frame count or FLAC sample number says; short is padded with silence at the start, long is trimmed from the start), decoded at the file's own rate (Matroska at 48 kHz). All match decoding the whole file (tests/morepieces.js, including a WebM rewritten to open-sized clusters). Everything else (1.4.116: Apple Lossless where it plays, FLAC in Ogg, MP2, AC3, CAF, AMR…) goes through the converter when it's too long to decode whole, when reading it in pieces failed, or when it plays but won't decode here (`analyzeWithConverter`: ffmpeg decodes two minutes at a time, 45 s on a phone, with `-ss`/`-t` into 48 kHz 16-bit samples, read in place by WORKERFS, measured like any piece and placed by time; about 4 s per 5 minutes of audio on a computer). Used by import (`analyzeAudio`), Check library, the background loudness and stand-in repair. It steps aside (null, stand-in kept, the background repair comes back) while a conversion holds the converter, and does nothing without the converter on the device (normally fetched quietly at setup). tests/convpieces.js. Other formats past the budget get a stand-in waveform and no loudness, marked with the budget of the device that tried (`loudness.tooLong`, in MB); a device with a bigger budget (a computer) tries again, and the result reaches the others through sync. Budgets: 2000 MB on a computer reporting 8 GB, otherwise 1000 MB (about 45 minutes of stereo). Splitting a WAV or MP3 copies bytes instead (1.4.079); other formats go through the converter (1.4.086), and only decode whole on a device without it. On phones and tablets MP3/WAV always go in pieces (1 MB pieces for MP3 there). The first piece starts after an MP3's Xing/Info header frame (`mp3AudioStart`): with that and smaller pieces, an iPad that had crashed on four 2-hour MP3s analyzed each in about a minute (1.3.042, 19 Oct 2026). A background analysis that gets the page closed is remembered (`guardedAnalysis`, localStorage `37player.analyzing`) and that track is skipped by that version (`analysisCrashed` = version). The server's `list` reports stand-in waveforms (`flatPeaks`, exactly 3601 bytes) so a device with the real one replaces it. Waveforms travel separately from the library document (720 numbers a track would make every save megabytes): each is a small file on the server named after its audio version, sent with the audio and fetched with it. A flat stand-in is never sent. A device with a stand-in (`repairStandInPeaks`, once per track via the local `peaksTried`) first asks the server, else makes it and sends it with `replace=1`, the one case the server overwrites a file. Loudness is in the library document (the audio group), so it syncs directly.
- **Long work freezes the page:** decoding and loudness loops yield to the page (`yieldToPage`). A 90-minute track used to freeze the page for 9 s.

## Tried and abandoned (don't go back)

- **Server libraries** (ready-made collections to download, from `libraries/` on the site, with Settings → Remote for their address): removed in 1.4.006. Publicly hosting recordings isn't something kr37 can do; shared libraries (joined by code, not public) replace it, and Import / Export points to Settings → Shared library instead.

- **Screen off:** filling only the gap between tracks with silence didn't stop the drop-out. Routing all playback through Web Audio didn't either: the phone also paused tracks itself.
- **Wi-Fi-only syncing:** removed. Devices that quietly stayed out of date were worse than the data use.
- **Phone columns trading width with their neighbour** (1.4.053): a pair's total stayed fixed and the line to their left ate into it, so columns 3 and 4 often fit in less than the screen. On a phone each line sizes one column.
- **Merging two people's reordering of the same playlist:** that's where automatic merges go wrong. Keep both copies instead.
- **Master/follower roles for sync:** a joined device can edit, and can have things of its own.
- **Google Drive via OAuth as the *only* storage:** Google Cloud setup, sign-in tied to one site, and uncertain sharing across accounts. It comes back as one storage among several, per library (see Open / later).
- **A localhost helper for backups:** too much to install. Backups are automatic in Chrome/Edge and manual zips elsewhere.

## Testing

- **One file, one name space:** every function in 37-player.html is in the same scope, so a new helper with an existing name silently replaces the old one. 1.4.114's `concatBytes(parts)` replaced the WAV splitter's `concatBytes(a, b)`, and split WAV parts lost their header (caught by the full run; renamed `joinBytes`, 1.4.123). Before adding a top-level function, grep for its name.
- **Tests and arguments:** run-all.sh passes every test two library names as arguments, so a test that picks things by `process.argv` must not; morepieces.js and convpieces.js take `ONLY=` instead.
- **Full run, 16 Nov 2026 (2.5.027, kr37's go-ahead):** 131 tests and the five engine tests; 128 passed at once. The other three were the tests, not 37 Player: setup.js still expected the Mac's Control-click warning (gone since 2.3.002, the app being signed), reconcile.js the sync log's words before 2.4.002, and desktop.js timed out now and then waiting for the network to go quiet after a reload: a track had been playing, the page restores it, and its audio element can keep a paused load of the recording open (now it waits for `load` there; six runs in a row passed).
- **Ask kr37 before running the full suite** (`run-all.sh` with no names): it uses a good share of his weekly usage. Single tests for what changed are fine; suggest a full run when it seems a good time, and he may add things first.

- **Environment:** Puppeteer driving real Chromium, on a test copy with debug hooks injected. Never on the shipped file.
- **Suites:**
  - two-device sync scenarios run against `php -S` with a scratch data folder;
  - the merge engine has standalone tests.
- **Simulating the screen off:** override `document.hidden` and send `visibilitychange`. This tests the logic only. Real phone behavior has to be checked on a phone, with the playback log.
- **In WebKit and Firefox too** (6 Nov 2026, after WebKit's .zip fault went unseen by Chrome-only tests): `tests/engines/` runs the same Puppeteer test files through Playwright (`ENGINE=webkit|firefox`, `tests/run-engines.sh`), and `.github/workflows/tests-engines.yml` runs both on every pull request to main and when asked. Free on GitHub's standard Linux machines (public repository). The adapter (`engines/adapter.js`) gives each test's browser context a profile on disk (`launchPersistentContext`), not a private one: WebKit's private sessions can't keep a Blob in IndexedDB ("Error preparing Blob/File data to be stored in object store"), so the first run failed nearly every WebKit import. Safari's Private Browsing has failed the same way on and off for years, on iPhone and iPad especially (WebKit bugs 188438, 198278, 268037), so a library in a private window there may not be able to keep recordings. The few DevTools commands the tests send (downloads, picking a folder for a file input, network throttling, which is skipped) are done Playwright's way (`FakeCDP`). A request handler may answer later (held or delayed requests), as in Puppeteer. **Chrome only** (skipped in other engines, `SKIP` in `run-engines.sh`): `ipad` and `phonecols`, whose touch gestures only Chrome's emulation gives a desktop browser; the iPad itself is tested by hand. **They had never passed** (6–13 Nov 2026: 21 tests failing in WebKit, 27 in Firefox); most of it was the setup, not 37 Player. What it took (13 Nov): a **sound device** on GitHub's machine (PulseAudio's null sink; without a sound card audio never left 0 s in either engine); the adapter routing **only http(s)** (in WebKit a route on everything caught the page's own `blob:` addresses: "Blocked by Web Inspector" for every recording a test had intercepted requests around); Puppeteer's `mouse.wheel`, `waitForNavigation` (a navigation still to come), double clicks, `emulate` (user agent, and a touch screen as matchMedia sees it), network throttling (e2e's slow upload), and a blank page kept when a context's last page closes (a persistent context ends with it); and tests that took Chrome for granted (`performance.memory`; Chrome cases given a folder picker and a Chrome user agent in welcome and setup; a Chrome-only case in notloaded skipped elsewhere; a `<select>`'s text). **Engines really differ** (tests now allow it, saying so): WebKit's MP3 decoder keeps the encoder's ~25 ms delay (splitter); AAC pieces decoded at their edges differ by up to ~1.3%, and more in the first 0.5 s and the last 0.1 s (m4apieces, allowed 2% outside Chrome; loudness and length equal); WebKit won't tell the adapter a Blob upload's size, so e2e's "files still on their way" isn't measured there. **Found in 37 Player:** the pause bug above. **Open:** in WebKit, Undo of a deleted track brings it back without its audio (undo.js): Undo keeps the database's Blob and the record is deleted, and WebKit can't read such a Blob afterwards (as in dbl); keeping a deleted track's audio stored until its Undo step expires (kr37's to-do, "Undo keeping audio 7 days") would fix it. A double-click in Table doesn't start the Library's own player in WebKit (table). **16 Nov 2026, on 2.5.027:** WebKit 10 failing (bridge3, conv3, hist, levelwatch, openwith, pausesync, stopimport, table, undo, undofiles), Firefox 3 (drive, hist, openwith); awk2, setup, webkitstore, reconcile, place and clocklead pass now. hist stopped at Puppeteer's `setOfflineMode`, which the adapter lacked (now `context.setOffline`); undofiles (new, 2.5.012) is the WebKit Blob limit above; stopimport and bridge3 passed in WebKit on 14 Nov and in Chrome now, not looked into. Drive's sign-in round trip in Firefox (drive) still loses its way. WebKit plays Apple Lossless, so it wasn't converted there (conv3), and a shared library's Chrome and Windows devices got a file they couldn't play: now converted at import everywhere (2.3.003, below); the lock screen's state after a pause while syncing reads back "playing" in WebKit (pausesync), to be looked at on the iPad.


## Open / later
- **The direction from here** (kr37, 18 Nov 2026): stability first. Clearing up what's convoluted, and cutting memory use and extra processing, serve that, as long as the changes aren't too disruptive. The audit of whole files through the page, the sync that downloads nothing new (2.5.043) and the waveforms file are the first of these.
- **Files with feedback** (kr37, 18 Nov 2026): Send feedback can attach files; and the ⋯ menu on a track (see the ⋯ action menu) gets "Send this file as feedback", opening Send feedback with that file (or the selected files) already attached.
- **Waveforms, the start** (kr37, 18 Nov 2026, the Mac mini's white window at start). The waveforms have been out of the list since 2.5.044 (see Non-obvious decisions). Still to do: the start reads both before drawing anything. The next step could be to read the waveforms after the list is on screen, with a save never writing a waveform the window hasn't read; it changes the start, so first the numbers: since 2.5.045 the play log's "started:" line says how much of the library's read was the waveforms ("…, 1.2 s of it the waveforms"; folder libraries, so the desktop app, only: IndexedDB reads both at once). If that's small, the white window is elsewhere; if it's most of it, reading them later is worth it, or (C) storing each waveform compactly, now easy with them in one record. The 3-minute spare copy of the library (`rootSpare`) still holds the waveforms inline; it's the recovery copy, so it's left as it is until that's decided.
- **iPad lock screen after pausing shows the silent loop's time** (kr37, 17 Nov 2026, iPad, a long playlist mostly with the screen locked: no regression, it played to the end). (1) After the end, the last track stays on the lock screen: check whether anything keeps running then (the silent loop, the media session) that we'd rather stopped, or whether it's only iOS keeping the last Now Playing. (2) Play from the lock screen then restarts the playlist from the start: fine. (3) Paused, the lock screen's elapsed time keeps counting, back to 0:00 after about 15 s (or to about 0:05), and the button shows Play/Pause wrongly until tapped once; paused at 2:00, it read about 0:05 later. It's most likely the keep-alive silent loop's time, not the track's. Resuming is right (place and time). Cosmetic; fix if cheap: while paused with the loop running, keep the media session on the track (`playbackState` "paused", `setPositionState` with the track's position) rather than the loop element's.
- **A ⋯ action menu on track rows** (kr37, 17 Nov 2026). Where a row has several action buttons (Library's Browse list: Edit, Copy track, Delete), one ⋯ menu instead, with more in it: Show in Finder / Show in Explorer (on Linux, a generic name such as "Show in file manager" rather than a list of file managers), Convert (when it applies), Check for duplicates.
- **The 8-second size check, to explore and clean up** (kr37, 20 Nov 2026; report 2026-11-20-213051-0e2f8a). Nothing was damaged; 23 files were downloaded for nothing. **What happened:** the MacBook converted 23 tracks and sent them (version 978). The Pixel (2.5.055) opened at 4:04 PM, took 978 and started downloading them; 8 s after its start `reconcileFileSizes` set each track's `fileSize` from the file it still held, the *old* unconverted one, and saved that as 979. The MacBook took 979's sizes as different audio (`sharedSameAudio` compares revision and size) and downloaded all 23 again at 4:29 (same revisions, same bytes). The shared library still carries those old sizes, so a device that corrects them back may set off one more round of the same. **What the check is now:** once per start, each track's `fileSize` is overwritten with the stored file's size, silently, and saved (so synced). Its comment's reasons (older versions not recording the size; tag rewrites not updating it) are fixed in the code, so it's a repair pass for old data run at every start, and nothing is done with what it finds beyond overwriting. kr37 approves of an integrity check, but it needs a purpose and an answer for what an inconsistency means. **The deeper point (kr37): it isn't sync-state aware.** While a device waits for new audio (for minutes, or months if the sender never sends it), its track records (root.json) are the shared library's description of the *new* file, and its disk holds the old one; only `sharedPending` (the revision wanted) says they differ. Nothing records which revision a stored recording *is*: in browser storage a recording is `{id, blob}` (the `rev` tag rewrites add is a random ID, not `audioRev`); in a folder library its `.json` holds path, type and size, not the revision. `sharedBase` is the last shared version merged, the whole library, not a per-file record of what this device holds. **Direction to explore: each stored recording records what it is,** in the record that already sits beside it and is written with it (file first, then its record; never synced, like the waveforms): a folder library's `37 Player's own files/audio/<id>.json`, which already has the file's path, type and size; browser storage's `audio` entry. Add the `audioRev` it was stored as (perhaps its `fileType`). Not in root.json: a second write that could drift from the file, the very problem. Then: the track record is the shared description and the recording's record is this device's file; the start check compares each file with *its own* record, and a mismatch is damage on this disk (Library cleanup → Damaged files), never a silent rewrite of the shared description; "the same audio" becomes "the revision held is the revision described", exact, with no size heuristic, so the Pixel's mistake can't happen; the downloads still to come follow from held ≠ described, so that list can't drift from the files; a device on an old revision for months knows which one it plays and whether it's intact. **Old data, without a repair pass** (kr37's rule): a recording with no revision recorded counts as unknown; with nothing pending and the size matching, it's taken as the track's current revision, and recorded the next time it's written. **To look into before building:** what reading every recording's record costs at start (the size check's `blobSize` already reads them in a folder library); every place that writes a recording (import, Replace, conversion, tag rewrites, receiving, Undo, restore, copies between libraries, files used in place) records the revision; how `sharedSameAudio`, `sharedQueueAbsentAudio`, the absent-audio check and the waiting notes use it; what becomes of `fileSize`'s other uses (sizes shown, "how much will travel"); whether the size check stays, as the integrity check, or goes.
- **Edit several tracks at once on Edit Playlists** (kr37, 20 Nov 2026, report 2026-11-20-163324). With several tracks selected in a playlist (column B) or the browser (D), Edit applies to all of them, as it does in the Library, but never changes the titles. A slightly different edit screen there: no track number, which could be taken for the item's place in the playlist.
- **Stitch: the opposite of the Splitter** (kr37, 17 Nov 2026). On Edit Playlists only: with several consecutive items of a playlist selected, Stitch makes one track of them. Then, as the Splitter does, it offers to put the new track in place of the selected ones or just leave it in the library. The main use: Tharpa's recording of a sadhana comes as 2 tracks holding three practices. Track 1 is 9 verses of prayer. Track 2 is one mantra recited 3 times out loud, then a second mantra recited softly once; you then recite that mantra yourself for a while, and the nicest way to finish is to play the soft recitation once more. So track 2 is split and a silence put in, making a 4-track sequence. Every Center plays this in a prayer every day (usually Heart Jewel), and any retreat or big event has the 4 tracks somewhere: stitched, they'd be one track. A silence in the selection would become that much silence in the new track. Questions for later: what happens to stops in the selection, and what the stitched track's name, artist, album and artwork are (probably the first track's, editable in the offer).
- **From kr37's tests on his new Mac, 17 Nov 2026 (not yet looked into; written down at the end of a week's usage):**
  - **Deleting a track should stop work on it.** He deleted a 650 MB file that turned out not to be audio (a .WAS renamed .wav), and the upload to the shared library carried on to the end; the server then keeps it for a month or so. Between pieces, `sharedUploadFiles` (and conversion, waveform making, receiving) could check that the track still exists with the same audio revision and stop, and drop what the server has of an unfinished upload.
  - **Converting at import: "reading the file: HTTP 404"** for a 299 MB WAV (Fall2024_M04_EN_unedited.wav, 59 min), desktop app, page 2.5.04x: "Some tracks couldn't be converted … reading the file: HTTP 404". The track stayed as WAV and plays. The converter reads from the app's file address; a 404 there means it asked for a path that wasn't there at that moment. A guess to check first: it was handed the file where it was first written, before the record settled on its readable name (`writeRecord`, `freePath`).
  - **Memory at start:** the window's process ("tauri://localhost" in Activity Monitor) was 783 MB a few moments after a start and briefly 1.2 GB, then settled at 275–365 MB, while playing and importing. Likely parts: parsing the 22 MB list of tracks (with their waveforms) and its spare copy, drawing the Library's table with waveforms, and before 2.5.039 the start-up size check. Fine on a 32 GB Mac; worth measuring for an 8 GB one (Gen Palden's).
  - **The 22 MB library file written at every save** (five times in 13 s while editing a playlist): the waveforms are most of it. Saving them apart from the list (they rarely change) would make each save small.

- **Where things stand (11 Nov 2026, page 2.2.009 on `dev`, shell 2.2.1 on tauri-spike/bridge2), for a new conversation:** done today: the Drive join on old WebKit, the file commands off the main thread, pieced reads (below), and the `chat` branch merged. Not yet on `main`; the Mac test build of shell 2.2.1 (d) is on the Releases page. kr37's to-do file of 11 Nov 2026 is `desktop/TODO-2026-11-11.md`. **His priorities, in order:** (1) the Mac desktop fixes and Google Drive as usable as possible; (2) the messages about backups: what each way of running 37 Player (desktop app; Chrome, Safari, Firefox; installed or not; phone, tablet) should do about where its library lives, backups and sharing, written up first as a table to agree, with ChatGPT's suggestion of a matrix of what survives each failure, as tested; (3) rejoining a library on disk, in the cloud or both, done right, including seeding a new device from a USB stick without sending everything again (check how sync matches tracks before answering). - **The plan for where libraries live, backups and shared libraries** (11 Nov 2026, for kr37 to agree; nothing built): `desktop/PLAN-library-backups-sync.md`. One row per way of running 37 Player, what survives each failure (tested, tried, or only expected), how to rejoin, and the USB-stick answer. **How sync knows a file:** by track ID and recording revision (`audioRev`) and size, never by content (`sharedSameAudio`); so a copy that keeps IDs and revisions (a whole library folder in the desktop app, which arrives still joined and with the same device name; or a .37p or backup folder restored into an empty library, then Join → Use the shared library here) moves only what changed since, while plain files imported afresh get new IDs, and on Combine the matched ones (name, album, artist, length) are removed here and downloaded again, the unmatched ones sent up as duplicates. **Found, and fixed in 2.2.019** (item 1 of the plan's order of work): Chrome/Edge's Keep it in a folder… pointed at an existing library folder with the same library ID (every first library is `main`) wrote this library's records over that folder's (`moveLibraryToFolder`; folderlib.js showed it before the fix). **Then, 2.2.020 (item 2):** Chrome/Edge can open an existing library folder (Settings → Libraries → Find a library folder…), and Keep it in a folder… offers to open the library it refuses to write over.

The second, straight after it: pressure red, 1.69 GB of swap, and the memory was in WebKit's **Networking process** (`player37-desktop Networking`, 4.38 GB, 3.74 GB of it compressed), not the page (`tauri://localhost`, 388 MB): WebKit keeps a Blob's bytes there, so that is the converter's input (`converterFile` → `realBlob`), about 2.7 GB, plus most likely the first file's 1 GB not yet let go. It got very laggy while the first file's upload overlapped the start of the second conversion, then carried on (49 % when last seen). The warning appeared and named the files and sizes; playing and moving between playlists went on while it converted (on a computer converting doesn't wait for playback unless set to, so the warning's "Starting playback sets the conversion aside" is now said only where that's so, `convertBigPlayNote`). **2.2.013:** a fresh converter straight after each big file (`runConvertQueue`, `wasBig`), so the old worker can't still hold its file. **During the third** (18 %, with the converted second file, 351 MB, being sent: the status bar's "(176 MB / 351 MB)" seen working): the Networking process was at 6.02 GB (5.65 GB compressed), swap 3.61 GB, which is about the three inputs together, so the earlier files' Blobs had not been let go. WebKit frees a Blob's bytes only when the page's garbage collector gets to the Blob, and that memory isn't the page's, so the collector is in no hurry; the fresh converter of 2.2.013 drops the worker's hold but can't make the page collect. Playback had a gap of about 0.2 s as the second finished and about 5 s as the third began (the file being read into memory from the same slow disk). Until the converter reads from disk: big files are best converted one at a time, with 37 Player quit in between. **And then the Mac restarted itself:** the third stayed at 99 % for five or ten minutes, the screen went black and macOS said it had restarted because of a problem; the Networking process was at about 6.0 GB when Activity Monitor froze. kr37: not very worried ("we warned them", and a solution is coming). Since a carried-over queue never takes up a big file again, reopening doesn't repeat it; the first two had converted, the third stays a WAV. macOS's report (`desktop/crash-reports/2026-11-11-mac-mini-watchdog-panic.txt`): "userspace watchdog timeout: no successful checkins from WindowServer in 120 seconds", 7 swapfiles: the Mac was swapping so hard that the window system stopped answering and the watchdog restarted it; not a fault in 37 Player's own code. The mac job imports the certificate into a keychain of its own and takes the identity from it (`security find-identity`; the secret typed by hand isn't relied on), writes the .p8 to a file (APPLE_API_KEY_PATH), and Tauri signs the app; the .dmg is then signed and sent to Apple's notary service (which notarizes the app inside it too), waited for up to 45 minutes, and stapled if accepted. The first submissions (13 Nov 2026) waited hours "In Progress" at Apple (a new team), and Tauri's own notarizing waits without end, past GitHub's 6-hour limit: so not back in 45 minutes, the build is published signed anyway, the release notes say so, and the notarization lands by itself (a Mac asks Apple online when it first opens the app); refused (Invalid), the build fails with Apple's log. `notary-status.yml` (main, run by hand) lists the submissions and their state. The bundled page was this branch's own `37-player.html`, 2.2.031 since main was last merged in: a new install ran that until its first page check (with, on 13 Nov, the bug that emptied "My library" when it was removed), and it's the page gone back to when a newer one fails. Now `copy-app.mjs` fetches `37-player.html` and `help.json` from the branch the build's pages follow (`P37_PAGE_BRANCH`: main for m, dev for d) and refuses a page that needs a newer bridge than this shell's. The release's title takes its version from the same branch. A build at a desk (no `P37_PAGE_BRANCH`) uses this branch's copy. The three jobs fetch separately, so a push in between could give them different pages: not guarded.
  - **The branch `desktop`** (renamed 13 Nov 2026, kr37; it was `tauri-spike`, after the experiment of 4–5 Nov, `desktop/SPIKE.md`, and older notes and briefs still say so): where the app's own source lives (`desktop/src-tauri`, and `desktop-mac.yml`, which builds the apps on a push to it); not on `main`. Page work goes on `dev`/`main`; the app gets its page from their releases, and `main` is merged into `desktop` now and then so the page it carries is current. The workflows that must run from the default branch (desktop-latest.yml, notary-status.yml) are on `main`. The app's identifier was `com.player37.spike` in the 1.4 test builds and has been `com.player37.app` since 6 Nov (2.0 on), for d and m builds alike: the two differ only in which pages they follow, and share one data folder, so installing one replaces the other. Folders named `com.player37.spike` on a Mac (`~/Library/WebKit`, `~/Library/Caches`, `~/Library/Application Support`) are left from those early builds and can be deleted.
- **From the Mac mini, 8 Nov 2026 (2.2.000 → 2.2.002; kr37's notes, not yet looked into):**
  - Fine: the first start's question; a folder used in place; the wipe test both ways (nothing noticeably lost; after the second, back on the bundled 2.2.000 page until it updated).
  - **Fixed (2.2.003): joining an existing Drive library.** The Mac mini's macOS 12 WebKit is Safari 15.6's, without DecompressionStream (16.4), so the state files other devices write gzipped (`.json.gz`) couldn't be read and the join stopped. `driveGunzip` now falls back to `gunzipBytes` (a small inflate, `inflateRawBytes`, also used for zip imports there). Test: drive.js's device B runs without (De)CompressionStream. (The desktop app uses the system WebKit, so on macOS 12 it is as old as that Mac's Safari.)
  - **Fixed (shell 2.2.1, page 2.2.003): the frozen window with big files.** The shell's file commands (`fs_read`, `fs_write`, `fs_copy`, …) were plain Tauri commands, which run on the main thread, the one that draws the window and handles clicks: the crash report caught it in `read()` there. They're `#[tauri::command(async)]` now, on worker threads (Tauri's shared pool; since shell 2.6.1 each on a thread of its own, see "Shell 2.6.1"). And `DiskBlob.arrayBuffer` reads in 8 MB pieces instead of one request for the whole file. Still true: converting a file used in place reads it whole into memory (`converterFile` → `realBlob`), so a 2.7 GB WAV needs 2.7 GB of memory; to decide (kr37): convert automatically only below a size, and whether WAV is converted at all in the desktop app.
  - **Google Drive:** sign-in in Safari works ("Done…"). Choosing an *existing* Drive library didn't offer full or partial: ~10 s, then back to the bare Shared library page (Join or create). Making a new one worked.
  - **A 2.7 GB WAV on an 8 GB, 5400 rpm Mac:** the automatic conversion started at once and made everything crawl; Stop in the status bar didn't respond, the app couldn't be closed (Force Quit), and after a restart it started converting again before Don't touch my files could be set. Later, uploading it to the shared library, and the splitter reading its waveform (at 15 %), made the app unresponsive again. Ideas: never convert automatically above a size; Stop must work while converting; nothing heavy right at the start.
  - **That WAV plays with loud metronome-like taps** (about 40 in 20 s, the first of each four slightly higher) in 37 Player; other tracks are fine. Solved (kr37, 8 Nov): GarageBand put them in; QuickTime plays them too. Not a 37 Player bug.
  - **The message for a file that's gone** says "On a drive that isn't connected" also when the file was only renamed; better: "Was this on a drive that's no longer plugged in, or was the file renamed or removed?" And "couldn't be converted … its audio isn't on this device yet" for a renamed file used in place is misleading.
  - **Opening an unsigned build the first time** (macOS on the Mac mini): double-clicking gives only Move to Trash / Cancel, every time; right-click → Open gives that once, then on the second try "macOS cannot verify the developer… Open". The home page should say: right-click → Open, twice.
  - **A crash with a 1 GB WAV** (8 Nov, 20:23, 2.2.0 shell): imported while syncing was on, so it started converting/uploading; then (he thinks) he played it, and the app quit (SIGABRT, `abort()` on a shell thread). In the crash report the main thread was inside WebKit's custom URL scheme handler, in a `read()` in our code: the shell serving a file to the page, on the main thread. Likely (not yet checked): the shell reads the whole file into memory to answer one request (no Range, no streaming), and a 1 GB file on top of the conversion's memory on an 8 GB Mac made an allocation fail, which aborts. Also explains the frozen window while it reads. To look at: serve files to the page in pieces (Range requests, off the main thread), and check the page never asks for a whole big file at once. The report is `desktop/crash-reports/2026-11-08-mac-mini-1gb-wav.txt`; symbols weren't kept, so a build with symbols or `RUST_BACKTRACE` would name the frames.
  - **The converting status line** (kr37): after Stop, add **Settings** (and maybe **Help**): someone new knows nothing about why 37 Player converts.
  - **Converting .wav to .mp3 by default in a library used in place** (kr37): reconsider. With one copy, outside IndexedDB, the reasons to convert people's files are much weaker.

The volume slider: the top half is fine, the bottom goes silent too soon (all versions; needs a gentler curve). A page update reset the volume to full.

- **History and Undo, the next piece: agreed 14 Nov 2026 (late), not built.** kr37's decisions, replacing the three-tab plan above where they differ:
  - **Two tabs, not three:** "Undo on this device" is merged into **Roll back the library**. Column A is one list: this device's changes **not yet synced** at the top (marked so), then the versions. Choosing an unsynced step undoes back to it on this device (Redo brings it back); choosing a version rolls back as now. A library that isn't shared, or one that has been left, lists only this device's undo steps. A shared library that's offline shows the unsynced steps and says the versions need a connection. So the page comes to every library and needs its own entry in Settings (not under Shared library). Why only unsynced steps are listed: once synced a change is part of a version, and listing older steps brings in the partly-undoable case (a step undoes only the records nobody else has changed since), which he found messy on a page.
  - **↶ and Ctrl-Z stay fine-grained and may undo past a sync** (his choice: steps are small, and on Edit Playlists they cost other devices nothing). An undo that syncs is a new version, with its time and device, so undoing too far is mended on the Roll back tab. Undo holds only this device's own changes, never another device's; the risk is a shared device (a new person at IKRC undoing what someone did on that iPad last week).
  - **A warning by age:** when Undo is about to take back a change **more than 2 hours old**: "This change was made more than 2 hours ago. Undo it?" with **Undo it**, **Cancel**, **View detailed history** (which opens the Roll back tab: he wants people steered to the history display, "much safer than trying to remember what you did hours ago"). Proposed, not yet confirmed by him: after Undo it, no more asking for that run of undos.
  - **A long press on ↶ opens History** (his idea). To add when built: the same from the button's tooltip and a right-click, since a long press isn't discoverable.
  - **Rollback stays a new version on top** (a revert), not "treat version 920 as current" (a reset, his thought, dropped after talking it through): devices remember the version they last synced with and versions only count upward, so a device at 928 has no rule for "current is 920", while 929 equal to 920 merges the usual way, offline edits included; nothing becomes a side branch; and the rollback itself is on record and can be rolled back.
  - **Artwork changes and "Replace audio file" become undoable** (he: closer to what people expect of Undo). Today they aren't because Undo compares tracks without their audio and artwork details (`undoDoc`), so that background work (waveforms, loudness, converting: each once per recording, mostly around an import) never makes a step, and because the replaced file is overwritten. So: a step for these two when a person does them, and **the old file moved aside, not overwritten**: his idea, a Trash folder in the library's folder (`37 Player's own files/Trash`) where the library is a folder, the same kept inside the store elsewhere; today's `undotrash` records (a deleted track's audio, one day) are the starting point (where they sit in a folder library: not checked). Conversions stay not undoable ("Keep the originals?" is for that).
- **Built from the agreement above: 2.5.011 and 2.5.012 (15 Nov 2026).** Not built: the Trash as a folder of its own (below).
  - **History is for every library** (`openSharedHistory` no longer needs a shared library; `histState.local`). Reached as before, and from Settings → Backups → History… in a library that isn't shared, from a long press or right-click on ↶, and from the Undo question.
  - **This device's steps in the list** (`histStepEntries`): newest first, then **Before these changes** (the point that takes every listed step back). A library that isn't shared lists every step Undo has kept, with no tab bar, "Undo keeps the last 30 changes." under the list; a shared one lists only the steps **not yet synced**, under "This device, not yet synced", above "Versions of the shared library". A step is marked `synced` when a sync has sent this device's changes (`undoMarkSynced`, at the two places `runSharedSync` saves its base after agreeing with the server: nothing to send, or sent). Since History sends anything waiting when it opens, a shared library that's online normally shows no steps. **Offline** (the versions can't be fetched): the unsynced steps alone, and "The shared library can't be reached, so its versions aren't listed."
  - **A step chosen:** column B is the library with the steps above it undone, worked out without touching the library (`undoPreviewDoc`, which runs `undoPlan`, the part of `undoApply` that decides what a step does, on a copy; `undoApply` now calls `undoPlan` too, so the page shows what Undo would really do, the kept-as-it-is cases included: "Some of it has changed since, and would stay as it is."). "Now" is this device's library (`st.rbNow`). The button is **Undo back to this** (`histUndoTo`): no question; History closes, the steps are undone one at a time and go to Redo, and a toast says "3 changes undone. Redo brings them back."
  - **A version chosen while this device has unsynced steps:** no button; "This device has changes that haven't been sent yet. Undo them (the list above) or sync first, then roll back." (My decision: rolling back the shared library under unsent changes would merge them back in afterwards, which the struck-through list would not show honestly. This arises with Review changes before syncing, or offline with an old list; not when online.)
  - **Step points have negative versions** (`-(index + 1)`; `HIST_BEFORE` for Before these changes), so nothing takes one for a version of the shared library. Bring back works on versions only: choosing it while on a step moves to the newest version, and choosing a step moves to Roll back.
  - **The 2-hour question** (`doUndo`, `UNDO_OLD_MS`): "Undo an older change?" / "This change was made more than 2 hours ago (Yesterday 10:12 PM): new playlist “Evening”. Undo it?" with **Undo it**, **Cancel**, **View detailed history**. One yes covers the run (`undoOldOk`), until the next change made here or a reload. Redo never asks.
  - **A long press (0.6 s) or a right-click on ↶ opens History**; the tooltip says "Hold, or right-click, for the history". Not when the button is greyed (nothing to undo): a disabled button gets no presses; Settings is the way in then.
  - Tests: `tests/undohist.js` (an unshared library: the list, a step chosen, Undo back to this, Redo's order, Before these changes, the 2-hour question and its three buttons, the long press, the right-click); `hist.js` adds the offline case (only the unsynced change listed, undone there) and that a sync marks the steps sent; `rollback.js`, `undo.js`.
  - **2.5.012: artwork changes and Replace audio file are undo steps.** The three places a person does it (`commitEditFields` in Edit track details, the Edit several tracks dialog, Replace audio file's confirm) call `undoKeepFile(track, "art" | "audio")` first: the file as it is goes aside as an `undotrash` record (`undotrash:<time>:<id>:<kind><token>`, with the record in `file`), together with the details that describe it (`UNDO_FILE_AUDIO`; `hasArtwork` for artwork). The save that follows makes a step carrying them (`step.files`), even when nothing else Undo compares changed; its words are "audio replaced" / "artwork changed". Undo and Redo swap (`undoFileSwap`): what is there goes aside, what was aside comes back, so it works any number of times. The file put back goes through `idbPut`, which gives it a **new revision**, so a shared library sends it on as the newest (the old revision number is not put back: other devices would take it for older). Kept one day like a deleted track's audio (`UNDO_TRASH_MS`); after that Undo leaves the track as it is and says "some of it … was left as it is". Background work still makes no step, and conversions are not steps. History shows such a step ("different audio", "different artwork" beside the track, from `st.stepFiles`) and offers Undo back to this even when that is all it changed.
  - **The Trash folder: not done as he pictured it.** He suggested moving the old file to `…/My Library/37 Player's own files/Trash`. What's built keeps it with Undo's other kept audio: in a library that is a folder that is already inside `37 Player's own files` (under `state/`, as `.bin` pieces of a record, the way `FolderLibraryStore` writes any record that holds a file), so nothing is overwritten and it is on disk, but it isn't a folder called Trash holding files one could open. Making it one means a place of its own in the files store (its reading, listing, deleting and orphan handling), which Claude left for a piece of work by itself. Also not done: keeping longer than a day.
  - Tests: `tests/undofiles.js` (the calls those three places make, made directly: artwork added, replaced, undone twice, redone twice; a recording replaced with its name, undone from History, redone; a step that only changed artwork shown in History; a kept file gone). **Not tried through the dialogs themselves**, nor in the desktop app's files store.
- **Bring back tracks or playlists: kr37's first thought for its redesign (15 Nov 2026, not designed further, nothing built).** Organised by the thing, not by the version: find the playlist, album or track you want, then look through the versions of it that exist, and choose. 37 Player would go through every kept version for all playlists, (artists?), albums and tracks. **Column A** like Edit Playlists' column C (browse: playlists, albums), **column B** like its column D, showing what the library has now (or the deleted item, when that is what was chosen), **column C** the times of every version there is of what was chosen, **column D** that earlier version of it.

The other languages' section names are changed; their passing mentions elsewhere were not searched for.

His reason: the switch looked like a main switch that would override the control on Playing, when it is the same setting. Since 2.5.014 it is Playing's own control (`playingLevelControl("levelModeSelect", true)`), so the two look alike (he noticed the plain drop-down of 2.5.013 looked different); each updates the other (`select._update`).

- **Bring back, his second round of thoughts (kr37, 15 Nov 2026; rough, "some aspects might need a spike"; nothing designed or built).** Two things to bring back, **playlists and tracks**: they have ids; artists and albums are only text on tracks, so they aren't things to bring back (he is unsure whether to offer them at all). A selector at the top, Playlists or Tracks, and for each two ways to look: **flat**, an alphabetical list of every name there is a record of (so one id appears once for each name it has had, and something remembered by an old name can be found though nothing has that name now); and **hierarchical**, for tracks like Library → Browse (artist, album, track), for playlists like the tree on Playing. Not settled by him: how a renamed artist, or an earlier arrangement or naming of folders, shows in the hierarchy (one thought: the artist twice, with the same albums and tracks). He is setting aside for now the cost of going through 30 days of versions. Claude's replies, for when this is taken up: the hierarchy as the library is **now**, with what no longer exists added where it last was and marked deleted, old names being the flat list's job (and the flat list could simply be a search box over every name ever); albums and artists only as ways to find and select tracks. The cost: each version is a whole document, and there may be hundreds, so finding "the versions in which this item changed" by downloading them all is the part that needs the spike; the likely way is to record the ids each save changed in the version's info from now on (as `what` records counts and a first name today), with a one-time, cached pass over older versions. And to decide: bringing back a playlist as a copy beside the present one, or in its place.
- **Bring back, third round (kr37 and Claude, 15 Nov 2026; nothing built).**
  - **The person chooses** when a current one exists: **Replace the current playlist** or **Bring back as "Morning (12 Nov)"**; a deleted one has only **Bring back**. Tracks: Claude leans to Replace only (a copy of a track copies its audio too), unless the track was deleted.
  - **What each version changed, recorded as it's saved** (his idea: a changes.json holding every change that can still be reverted, its tail removed as cleanup removes old versions). Why not in the summary each version already carries: on Drive that summary is a file property, at most 124 bytes per key and value (`driveHistoryProps`), and to sync.php it's a header capped at 1000 bytes (`X-Change`); a list of ids fits neither. Why **one file per version** rather than one file for all: Drive has no lock, so two devices adding to one file at once can overwrite each other's lines (sync.php serialises saves, Drive doesn't). So: **on Drive** a small `<version>.changes.json` beside each version, written by the device that saves it; **on the droplet** sync.php works out the changed ids itself (it has the old and new documents at each save) and keeps them in the version's info file. Cleanup already removes old versions and their info files, so the tail goes by itself; on Drive the tidy needs to remove the companion files with their versions. Each device keeps its own combined index and fetches only the files of versions it hasn't seen.
  - **"Build list from history (slow)"** (his idea: a button for versions saved before this exists, removed after a month): it builds the missing entries once, by comparing each older version with the one before it, and keeps the result on the device. It needn't be removed by hand: 30 days after this is built every kept version has its own file, so the button has nothing left to build and hides itself.
  - This is the spike to do first: the rest of Bring back's design depends on column C (the versions of one item) being quick.
- **The spike: what each version changed (2.5.015, 15 Nov 2026; built and recording from now on).** The first step of Bring back: answering "in which versions did this playlist (or track) change?" without opening any version.
  - **What's recorded:** for each version, the ids that differ from the version before: tracks (`t`, compared whole) and folders and playlists (`n`, compared without their children, with the folder they're in; so an entry added to a playlist marks that playlist, and moving it marks it). Rules in `syncChangedIds` (37 Player) and `changed_ids` (sync.php), the same on both sides (the test checks they agree).
  - **On the droplet:** sync.php works it out at each save from the previous version and the new one (it holds the lock anyway), into the version's info file as `c`. So it never depends on a device and has no size limit. `op=history` leaves `c` out (History's list stays small); **`op=changes&since=N`** returns `{kept: [...], versions: {v: {t, n}}}` for versions after N. sync.php is now 2.5.015; **an older sync.php records nothing and answers op=changes with "Unknown request"**: deploy it with this.
  - **On Google Drive:** the saving device works it out (`sharedPostState` sends it as `X-Changed` to the Drive layer, never to a server) and writes `<version>.changes.json` beside the version, after the version's number is secured; a failure there is logged and the save stands. Version lookups and the tie check ignore those files; Free up space keeps the newest *version* (not the newest file) and deletes a version's changes file with it. `op=changes` on Drive downloads only the changes files after `since`.
  - **On the device:** `histChangeIndex(cfg, {build, onStep})` keeps an index in the state store (`histchanges:<library>`), fetches only versions it hasn't seen, drops versions no longer kept, and names those with no record (`missing`: saved before this, or by an older 37 Player). `build: true` is **"Build list from history"**: it opens each such version and the one before and works it out here. `histVersionsOf(idx, "n" | "t", id)` answers the question.
  - **Measured** (test server on this machine, `tests/changes.js`): a library of 2000 tracks and 120 playlists; the server's extra work per save (read the previous version, compare) about **23 ms**; an info file at most 20 KB (the first version, which lists everything); `op=changes` for 36 versions 22 KB in 11 ms; the device's index from nothing **9 ms**, again 6 ms; the question itself under 1 ms; **building from history for 36 versions 1.1 s** (so a few hundred versions: some seconds on the droplet, more on Drive, where each version is a separate download). Drive's numbers were not timed, only checked to work (`tests/drive.js`, against the fake Google).
  - So **column C can be quick**: the design of Bring back can rely on it.
  - **Names too (2.5.016):** each changed id also carries its names, `tn` / `nn`: `{id: [name after, name before]}` (one name when unchanged; a deleted one has only the name it had). With the names in the library now, `histAllNames(idx, doc)` gives Bring back's flat list: every name a track or playlist has had in a kept version, old ones marked (`now: false`), so something remembered by a name it no longer has can be found. Cost: the first version lists every item, so its info file grows (118 KB for 2000 tracks, from 20 KB) and `op=changes` for 36 versions is 125 KB; the device's index from nothing 13 ms. An empty name list reads back from PHP as `[]`, so op=changes casts it to `{}`. sync.php is 2.5.016.
  - Not built: anything on screen. The Build button and Bring back's columns come with the design.
  - Tests fixed on the way: `drive.js` had stopped at "the sign-in is revoked" (its Settings helper closed a Settings page left open by the step before) and needs a folder `tests/www/ovt` that wasn't in this session (made here: three short tones, one recording over 1 MB, playlists Morning in Daily and Evening; not committed, as `www/` isn't).
- **Bring back, first two sketches (15 Nov 2026; shown in the conversation "History page redo", nothing built).** Top: **Playlists | Tracks**, and **Every name | By folder**. Column A: every name there is a record of (`histAllNames`), with a search box; old names grey with "now “…”", deleted ones green with "deleted"; By folder is the library as it is now with deleted items where they last were (tracks: artist · album; whether that's right is his open question 1). B: the item now (entries added since in red); **a deleted item shows only "Deleted"** and when (his decision). **C lists only earlier states that could be brought back** (his decision): not the current state, not the save that deleted it, and **one entry per distinct state**, the most recent of the versions that had it. D: that state in a box, entries only there in green, buttons inside: **Replace the current playlist** / **Bring back as a copy, "Morning (14 Nov)"** (the copy's name uses the date, as "Yesterday" would soon be wrong; on screen, "Yesterday" is fine: he has never seen a playlist at a Kadampa center depend on the time of day), or **Bring back** for a deleted one; a deleted track says how much audio comes down again. An item unchanged for over 30 days: "No earlier state is kept." For building: the index gives the versions where an item changed; the state after each is in that version's document, so C needs those few versions opened when an item is chosen (and is where identical states are merged).
- **Built: Bring back tracks or playlists (2.5.018, 15 Nov 2026), from kr37's fourth sketch and his answers on it.** Replaces the old tab (today's rows with Keep now / Restore, and "What changed at this point", which is **gone**: say so if it's missed; Roll back compares a version with now instead).
  - **Layout** (`histBringMainHtml`; the History page's list of versions is hidden on this tab, `.hist-body.hist-bring`): title **"Bring back since {date}"** (the oldest version kept); **Playlists | Tracks** (page-tab style), **By folder | Flat list** and **Show: Changes | Everything** as radio buttons, **Wrap** (on to start with). Three columns: A finds the item; the middle is **Now beside Earlier, row for row**, the earlier side in the box with the buttons; on the right **"Earlier, as it was"**, the earlier states.
  - **Column A** (`bbColumnA`, from `bbModel`): Playlists by folder: the tree as it is now, a deleted playlist where it last was (green, "deleted"). Tracks by folder: **By playlist** (the folder tree, each playlist opening to its tracks, a deleted track under the playlists it was in, and a last section **"Not in a playlist"** with a header bar in the Table header's blue) or **By album** (artist small above the album, an album icon `ICON_ALBUM`). Flat list: every name each item has had, old names italic and grey with "now “…”". **Search** matches every name an item has had and keeps all its names in the list ("also called “…”"); in Tracks by playlist, matching a playlist's name shows all its tracks. **Changes**: only items with an earlier state to bring back; **Everything**: the rest in grey, groups without a change closed to start with (`bb.flip` holds the ones toggled from that).
  - **Which items have an earlier state** (`bbModel`): every version whose record lists the item, except where it was added there (`ta`/`na`), and only if the version before is still kept. The record now also carries, for what was deleted, what's needed to show it without opening a version: a playlist's kind and folder (`nx`), a track's artist, album and playlists (`tx`), worked out on both sides (`syncChangedIds`, `changed_ids`; sync.php 2.5.018). Versions recorded by 2.5.015–2.5.017 lack these: a deleted item from then shows at the top level or in Not in a playlist.
  - **Choosing an item** (`bbChoose`) opens the versions just before each of its changes (`histDoc`, cached), and keeps **one entry per different state, the most recent**, leaving out the one it has now (kr37: C lists only points that could bring something back). None: "Always the same since {date}." (his wording) or, for something added and deleted inside the window, "Nothing earlier to bring back since {date}." A deleted item's Now side says only **"Deleted"** (his decision). Playlists line up entry by entry (`histRollAlign`): a renamed entry (same id) on one line with "renamed", an entry only now in red with a blank opposite, only then in green. Tracks: name, artist · album, and "different audio / artwork" when so.
  - **Buttons** (`bbApply`): a playlist that exists: **Replace the current playlist** or **Bring back as a copy, "Morning (13 Nov)"** (dated by the state's version; the copy goes just after the current one, with new entry ids, and any tracks it needs that are gone come back too); a deleted playlist: **Bring back** (with its folder and tracks, via `syncRestore`'s dependencies); a track: **Replace the current track**, or **Bring back** for a deleted one, saying how much audio comes down again. Each is a new version of the shared library (as Restore was), checked against a save meanwhile ("The shared library changed meanwhile…"), then History closes, a sync runs and a toast says "Brought back “…”". No question first (the current state is itself a version). A receive-only device sees the states but no buttons.

  - **Shell 2.5.7: p37lib answers on the main thread** (kr37's SKBC Mac mini, macOS 12.7.6, x86, 20 Nov 2026; report 2026-11-20-050726-96a6fa). Playing the Italian festival WebM (time moving, no sound), the app aborted: macOS's report shows abort() on a thread the shell started, and the shell logged "panic in a function that cannot unwind". The p37lib handler (`register_asynchronous_uri_scheme_protocol`) read the file and answered from its own thread. WebKit cancels requests (`stopURLSchemeTask`) on its main thread, and wry 0.57.1 checks that a request is alive before each step of an answer (`check_task_is_valid`). Answered from another thread, a cancel can land between check and step; WebKit then raises inside code that can't unwind. WebKit's WebM player asks for a long file in many small, often-cancelled pieces, and a busy old Mac widens the gap. Now the file is read on that thread and `responder.respond` runs on the main thread (`run_on_main_thread`), where the check and the step can't be split. Also: the panic hook (watch.rs) kept only the last panic message, so the second ("cannot unwind") replaced the first, the one naming the cause; it keeps the first and adds later ones. Not reproduced (no Mac here); the d and m builds after it are the test. Whether macOS 12's WebKit can play Opus/WebM at all (the missing sound) is still open; if it can't, converting is the answer for such Macs.
  - **2.5.057: p37lib recordings are marked for Web Audio, as Linux's are** (report 2026-11-20-054746-594a1e, SKBC Mac mini on 70d: the festival WebM played from a playlist with its time running and no sound; the log: "player now turns tracks up through Web Audio"). The recording is quiet, so `applyLevel` routed the player through Web Audio to turn it up. Before routing, it marks a recording `crossOrigin = "anonymous"` and reloads it, so that Web Audio may hear a recording from another origin. That was done only for the Linux app's http address (`isDesktopHttpUrl`), not for p37lib (Mac p37lib://localhost, Windows http://p37lib.localhost), which is another origin from the page too: unmarked, Web Audio may output silence, and macOS 12's WebKit did for this WebM. Now `isP37libUrl` gets the mark too; the shell already answers with Access-Control-Allow-Origin (and OPTIONS). Ruled out first: macOS 12's Safari plays this WebM, with sound, from a page of kr37's on the disk, also when routed through Web Audio (same origin there). Still to see: whether turned-up MP3s on Windows had been silent the same way (Chromium mutes cross-origin media in Web Audio too).

So Web Audio isn't the cause there (2.5.057's mark stays right for Mac and Windows). Suspected instead: macOS 12's WebKit, as built into an app, lacking an Opus decoder that Safari turns on for itself, so the WebM is read (length, time) but nothing decodes its sound. If its log says "WebM Opus no", such tracks should be treated as not playable on that device (offered for conversion), not played in silence.

  - **The Mac mini's second crash, symbolized: the cause is in wry, for every answer** (report 2026-11-20-061426-70be7f, app 2.5.7, page 2.5.058, 20 Nov 2026 01:12, 17 s after a window reload). Release builds attach `*-mac-symbols.zip` (dSYMs); its x86_64 UUID matched the crash report's, and `llvm-symbolizer --obj=…/DWARF/player37_desktop --inlining` with 0x100000000 + each frame's offset names them. The chain: one of the shell's async commands answering (`InvokeResolver::respond_async_serialized_inner`, `player37_desktop_lib::run::{closure#13}::{closure#19}`) on a `tokio-rt-worker` → Tauri's IPC protocol (tauri-2.12.5 ipc/protocol.rs:140) → wry-0.57.1 url_scheme_handler.rs `response` → `objc2::exception::catch` around `task.didReceiveResponse` (line 268) → `panic_in_cleanup` → abort. The first crash (report 2026-11-20-050726) has the same inner frames, reached from p37lib's own thread. No Rust panic came first (2.5.7 keeps the first message, and "cannot unwind" was the only one), so WebKit raised while being handed an answer off the main thread, and objc2's catch couldn't contain it. Likely a request WebKit had just stopped (a window reload, a media player's cancelled pieces) racing wry's check-then-call from another thread. Shell 2.5.7 moved only p37lib's answers to the main thread; Tauri's command replies still come from tokio. wry 0.57.3 (newest) has the same url_scheme_handler.rs. **Planned:** patch wry (a copy kept in the repo, `[patch.crates-io]`) so its responder hands every answer to WebKit on the main thread, and report it upstream.
  - **Shell 2.5.8: wry patched, every answer handed to WebKit on the main thread** (20 Nov 2026, kr37: "build it all, including the wry patch"). wry 0.57.1, as on crates.io, is kept in `desktop/vendor/wry` and used through `[patch.crates-io]` in desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml (same version, so nothing else moves). The one change, in `src/wkwebview/class/url_scheme_handler.rs`: the responder runs the original answer at once on the main thread, and from any other thread queues it there (`NSOperationQueue::mainQueue().addOperationWithBlock`, hence the `NSOperation` feature), so wry's "is this task still valid" check and its calls into WebKit happen on the thread where WebKit stops tasks, and can't race it. That covers Tauri's command replies from tokio as well as p37lib (whose own move to the main thread in 2.5.7 stays). `desktop/vendor/wry/PATCHED-FOR-37-PLAYER.md` says what changed and how to carry it to a newer wry. Built as **71d** (page 2.5.058, app 2.5.8; the Mac job compiled the vendored wry); not yet tried on the Mac mini. Still to do: tell wry's maintainers (an issue with the symbolized chain); an m build when kr37 approves.

Turning a track *up* needs the player hooked into Web Audio, which browsers start only at a click or key press, and a player was hooked in only when it loaded a track needing a boost while the engine ran. So after a start or reload, a track already loaded (Playing's put-back track) played without its boost until loaded again: his Mac mini's log, the Italian translation put back at 8:42:47, his click on Play at 8:42:50 starting the engine, the boost only at 8:43:27. Now the click that starts the engine also hooks in every player holding such a track (`hookInLoadedPlayers`, on pointerdown/keydown, and when the engine reports running): a paused one at once (loaded again for the cross-origin mark where its address needs it, put back at the same spot unless moved meanwhile or another track loaded); a playing one at its next pause, to make no click in the sound. Only where a boost is wanted, as before, so phones aren't routed through Web Audio any more than they were. `levelCtxReady(gesture)`: inside the click, a context asked to start counts as ready. The Library's, Edit Playlists' and the duplicates' players now take Playing's slider too (`applyLevel`'s base, `setMainVolume`), where before they played as if it were at the top. The rule: a track that wants turning up never plays until it can be. `guardLevelOnPlay` (on Playing's, the Library's and Edit Playlists' players): at a `play` of such a track on a player not hooked in, the player is paused before any sound, the engine is asked to start (the media-session play handler also asks, as a play from outside the window may be enough for the browser), and once it runs the player is hooked in and plays from the same spot (logged: "held … a moment to start the sound engine"). If it isn't running within 1.5 s, the player stays paused with a note asking for one click in the window (`levelNeedsClick`, nine languages). If hooking in itself fails, the track plays as it is, logged, rather than never (`_levelGiveUp`). Considered and not done: playing it quiet (the old behaviour) or switching evening out off for the session; both make the level depend on how playback was started. With 2.6.005 (the first click hooks in a paused player), Playing in a window people click in is never unboosted; 2.6.007's rule stays for the give-up case. Still to do: the desktop app starting sound without a click (shell), so even a media key at start needs nothing.
