  - **The audit of every place the page and the shell talk: the report, and its fixes** (kr37 asked for it on 20 Nov 2026, after the wry fix: "finding a place where the communication between two processes can fail seems like one of the most important stability improvements"; done 20–23 Nov 2026). **The report is `desktop/AUDIT-boundaries.md`.** It grew to every place 37 Player's processes and threads meet: the shell's main thread and its other threads, the page, WebKit's media and Networking processes, the system, the iPad and Windows. Each crossing says who waits and for how long, its failure modes (the other side gone, reloading, slow, answering twice or never, off the main thread), what happens today, and a fix with its risk; kr37's decisions are marked "kr37 (23 Nov)"; its table "The fixes, in order" is the plan. **How they're built** (kr37, 23 Nov 2026): one step per branch, shell changes from `desktop` made into a d build for him to try, page changes from `dev`, in the table's order, with fix 22 (the format check, and Check library's status) before fix 18 (M). Fixes 10 (measurements on the Mac mini) and 20 (the iPad test) are his. New page–shell calls are optional (the page checks `p37_shell_info` and otherwise behaves as today; the bridge stays 2), and the first page that can call one is 2.7.000 with shell 2.7.0. **Done:** fixes 1 and 2, shell 2.6.1 (next entry); fix 3, shell 2.7.0 with page 2.7.000 (the entry after); fix 4, shell 2.7.1 with page 2.7.001 (after that); fixes 5–7 are the page's alone (2.7.002–2.7.004, `dev`'s NOTES); fix 8, shell 2.7.2 with page 2.7.005 (after fix 4's entry); fix 9, shell 2.7.3 with page 2.7.006 (after that); fix 11, shell 2.7.4 with page 2.7.007 (after that; fix 10 is kr37's measurements). **Each shell branch builds on the one before** (`audit-fix1-2-shell` → `audit-fix3-shell` → …), and each page branch likewise from `dev` (`audit-fix3-page` → …), so the versions follow one another; merging the newest takes in the ones before. A d build that carries a page branch's page is started with `desktop-mac.yml`'s **page_branch** input (Run workflow on the shell branch, channel dev, page_branch the page's branch): the shell half and the page half of a fix are tried together before either is merged; the pages it updates to afterwards still follow dev.
  - **Shell 2.6.1: nothing that waits for a disk on the main thread or on Tauri's shared pool** (the audit's fixes 1 and 2, E and D; branch `audit-fix1-2-shell`, from `desktop`). A command not marked async ran on the shell's main thread, the one that draws the window and takes in every message from the page; an async one ran on Tauri's shared pool, a thread per core (4 on the Mac mini), where the file commands did slow disk work without letting go, so a few slow ones (a 2 GB copy, reads from a drive waking up or gone, an open folder chooser, which holds its thread until it closes) held up every other command, saves and the device's settings included. Now each one's work goes to Tauri's pool for blocking work (`blocking` in lib.rs: `spawn_blocking`, which grows as needed, up to 512 threads): every fs_* command and both folder choosers; and the ones that were on the main thread: `p37_device_put` and `_get`, `p37_page_info`, `_ok` and `_switch`, `fs_place_default`, `fs_root_default`, `fs_root_forget`, `fs_root_use_found`, and Open With's look-ups (a short-lived thread; paths that come before the roots are loaded are still put aside on the main thread, as setup is, so none slip between). Left as they were: the commands that don't touch a disk (`p37_shell_info`, `p37_zoom`, `p37_watch`, `p37_opened_take`, `fs_roots`, `fs_place`, `p37_quit`), and `p37_open_url` (starting a program is quick). **What keeps the old order, now that these can overlap:** each device.json put takes a turn as it comes in, and one held up by a slow disk never lands after a newer one (`Device::ticket`, `write_as`; each put is the whole set, so one passed over loses nothing); roots.json is saved one at a time, each save writing the list as it is then; `fs_root_default` makes one folder at a time (two at once could take the same free name); `pages.rs` reads and checks kept pages (megabytes each, with their signatures) before taking its state lock, never while holding it (`choosing` keeps one choice at a time), so About's question no longer waits for the hourly check. `fs_write`'s body is copied for its thread (at most 8 MB). No page change: every answer is the same. Tests: `cargo test` (54; new: a device put held up never lands after a newer one, sixteen at once end with the last; the pages' check, choice, question and "started" at once agree).
  - **Shell 2.7.0 and page 2.7.000: saving goes on after Tauri's switch to its slower route** (the audit's fix 3, A; 1.1; branches `audit-fix3-shell` and `audit-fix3-page`). When one request on Tauri's own route (a `fetch` to `ipc://localhost/<command>`) fails, Tauri sends it again through the webview's message route, and every later request in that page goes that way until it reloads (`ipc-protocol.js`, tauri 2.12.5). Not seen in a log yet; this is insurance. On that route a write's bytes come as a JSON list of numbers, which `fs_write` refused ("the file's bytes must be the body"), so every save failed until a reload; and a read's answer comes back as a plain list of numbers (on the Mac; the answer is evaluated as script there), which `getFile()` put in a File as is, so a file of 32 MB or less read that way became the text "82,73,70,…". **Shell 2.7.0:** `fs_write` takes either (`files::body_bytes`) and answers `"slow"` when the bytes came the slower way (nothing, as before, otherwise); `p37_shell_info` says `slow_route`. **Page 2.7.000:** see the page's entry (`desktopIpcSwitched`); the page works with either shell. Tests: `cargo test` (body_bytes by either route).
  - **Shell 2.7.1 and page 2.7.001: closing when the page doesn't answer** (the audit's fix 4, F; 1.8; branches `audit-fix4-shell` and `audit-fix4-page`). Every close, the app menu's Quit and the system's quit are held back and the page asked (`p37-close-requested`); a frozen page never answered, so the window couldn't be closed, Quit did nothing, a log-out waited until macOS gave up on it, and only Force Quit worked (inferred; it fits the 18 Nov freeze, where the menu bar still answered). **Shell** (`closing.rs`): the page answers each ask at once with `p37_close_seen` (before its own question; at its start too, which is how the shell knows this page answers, with the dialog's words in its language), and each page load starts over (`on_page_load`, Started), so a page too old to answer closes as before. When an ask has gone unanswered for 5 s (`NO_ANSWER_MS`), the next close or Quit shows the system's own dialog, **"37 Player isn't responding"**, Quit / Wait (one at a time); when the system asked (`ExitRequested` with no code: a log-out, a restart, the Dock's Quit), it comes by itself after 10 s (`SYSTEM_MS`). Quit there is p37_quit's (`quit_now`). `p37_shell_info` says `close_seen`. Tests: `cargo test` (closing: answered, unanswered for five seconds, too old to answer, a page load starting over and the page's words).
  - **Shell 2.7.2 and page 2.7.005: the page's process ending is logged, reloaded, and never loops** (the audit's fix 8, J; 1.14; branches `audit-fix8-shell` and `audit-fix8-page`). On the Mac, when WebKit's page process crashed or macOS ended it for memory, Tauri reloaded the page by itself (tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.3's default handler) and nothing was logged; a page that died at every start would have reloaded in a loop. **Now** the shell's own handler (`page_process_ended`, Tauri's `on_web_content_process_terminate`, Mac only) keeps the time for the page's log (`p37_watch`'s new `page_ended`; the reloaded page logs it) and reloads the window, but after a third within two minutes (`watch::should_reload`, `RELOADS_WITHIN_MS`, `RELOADS_AT_MOST`) it stops and the system's dialog asks **"37 Player's window keeps stopping"** (Reload / Quit), in the page's language from page 2.7.005 (its words come with `p37_close_seen`'s, `closing::Texts`' `ended_title`, `ended_body`, `reload`; English before). **Not yet: Windows.** WebView2 reports the same as `ProcessFailed`, and nothing in wry, Tauri or the shell handles it (searched), so the window would likely stay blank; handling it needs `webview2-com` in the shell, which a session can't compile here (no Windows target): to check on the HP first. Tests: `cargo test` (twice in two minutes reloads, a third stops; the page's words, English when a page before 2.7.005 sends none). The Mac-only handler compiles only in the Mac build (its body was checked on Linux).
  - **Shell 2.7.3 and page 2.7.006: the page not answering is recorded, and a hang that never ended keeps its start** (the audit's fix 9, I; 1.13; branches `audit-fix9-shell` and `audit-fix9-page`). The stall watch saw only the shell's main thread, and only once a stall ended: a frozen page left no trace but "didn't close normally" after a force quit, and a shell hang ended by Force Quit left only that too; on 18 Nov the log's last line was about ten minutes before the page actually froze, worked out only from samples taken by chance. kr37 had declined a page watch on 18 Nov (it would only log, and he was wary of more background work); **this version (his decision, 23 Nov 2026) adds no thread, no timer and no new page call**: the page already asks `p37_watch` every 20 s and the watch's thread already runs. The shell keeps the time of the page's last ask (`page_asked`, `Watch::page_asks`), and the watch's loop (`check_page`, every tick) notes when it's over a minute old with the window in front (`PAGE_SILENT_MS`), or 5 minutes when it isn't (`PAGE_SILENT_AWAY_MS`; the system slows a hidden window's timers): at once into running.json (`page_silent_since`), so a force quit still leaves when the page stopped answering. When the page asks again, the silence goes to its log (`page_silences`: {at, ms}). Every 30 s running.json also says whether the main thread is held up right now and since when (`main_stalled_since`, `Finder::stalled_since`). **Only recorded, never acted on** (kr37: for the 18 Nov freeze a reload would very likely meet the same stuck media process, and a restart by itself, perhaps mid-puja on a false alarm, is worse than a frozen window someone can see; reconsider only if records show freezes a restart reliably cures). **Linked to fix 4:** a close or Quit while the page is noted silent offers "isn't responding" at once (`ask_page`). Tests: `cargo test` (63: silent after a minute in front, five away; noted once, in running.json at once, reported when the page asks again; a stall under way has its start).
  - **Shell 2.7.4 and page 2.7.007: silences from silent audio the shell makes to length** (the audit's fix 11, L; 2.1; branches `audit-fix11-shell` and `audit-fix11-page`; kr37, 23 Nov 2026: "the shell's silence, made to length", not a folder of silent files). On a computer a silence plays a silent recording exactly as long as the time left, up to 30 s at a time, as a timer that keeps time for a covered window (its `ended` comes on time; 1.4.158); the page made those WAVs itself, so its own memory held audio the media process waited on, the arrangement of the 18 Nov freeze. **Now** the shell serves `p37lib://localhost/_silence/<ms>` (Windows `http://p37lib.localhost/_silence/<ms>`, Linux the file server's `/<secret>/_silence/<ms>`): a WAV header and zeros, 8 kHz 8-bit mono as before, made on request (`files::silence_wav`, at most 2 minutes; Range answered), no file kept anywhere; `_silence` is never a root's name. `p37_shell_info` says `silence`. A side benefit: the sound output stays active through a long silence, so a sleeping USB, Bluetooth or HDMI output can't clip the next track's start. kr37's folder of silences was also a way for the library to be read by other players; that's a separate idea (a library other players can read: playlists plus real silence files), not part of this. Tests: `cargo test` (65: the WAV, the asking, the Linux server's answer).

The candidate: WebRTC between devices, the records still through the sync server (small), the files sent directly while both have 37 Player open ("Waiting for X to send them" would mean just that); on the same Wi-Fi at network speed, from the https page. Needs its own design talk.

- **Use in place in Chrome and Edge: agreed approach, not built** (kr37, 14 Nov 2026). Possible, since the browser can read recordings where they lie through a folder it has been given (as folder libraries and the backup folder do). The trouble is permission: unless the browser remembers it, each folder must be allowed again on each visit, and asking needs a click, so a playlist drawing on three folders could stop mid-session. His worry: too dicey with an inexperienced operator. So: **offer Use in place only where the browser already remembers access for 37 Player.**
  - **How we know, silently:** Chrome 122 and later can grant a site persistent access ("Allow on every visit", offered when it asks again for a folder on a later visit); it is one grant for the site, and folders opened afterwards are persistent too (developer.chrome.com/blog/persistent-permissions-for-the-file-system-access-api). So at startup, `queryPermission()` on a folder 37 Player already keeps (the library's or the backup folder) answering "granted" before any click means this browser remembers. Checking never prompts.
  - **Remembered:** Use in place beside Copy and Move at Import → Choose a folder…. **Not remembered:** not offered, with one line saying why and that the desktop app has it.
  - **Safety net only:** access can be taken away later in Chrome's site settings, so a top-bar "folders need access" indicator (the way "Resume backups" works) with the tracks shown as waiting, and a warning when a playlist that needs such a folder is queued. Never a prompt at Play, and never waiting for a track to fail (his four options, the last two ruled out).
  - A gain over the desktop app's page-side gate: the browser gives read-only access unless more is asked for, so "never change a folder used in place" would be enforced by the browser itself.
  - **To check on real machines first:** Edge and Brave behave as Chrome does; the Mac mini's browser (macOS 12 can't run recent Chrome); whether one click can cover several folders' prompts. Comes after "Open a library folder…" for Chrome and Edge (desktop/PLAN-library-backups-sync.md, 5.2 and question 4). It adds no row to the recommendations table: one yes-or-no check decides whether one import option shows.

    - **First run** (3 Nov 2026, laptop, Firefox, test.37player.com): the API works from the page (CORS), uploads work (30 MB at 3.4 MB/s), and two devices saving the same version at once works by `renameifexists`: the loser's file comes back renamed ("000002 (2).json"), so it deletes that and merges, like a 409. Failed: download links (`getfilelink`: "Invalid link referer", for all three fetches), resumable uploads (a reply that wasn't JSON; optional) and `diff` (needs full access; not needed, listing the state folder does). The test page now asks for and fetches links without a Referer (`referrerPolicy: "no-referrer"`), tries `gettextfile` as a fallback for state files, and says what a non-JSON reply was. Second run (4 Nov 2026): still "Invalid link referer" without a Referer, and `gettextfile` says "Log in required". **pCloud can't serve files back to a web page:** its docs for `getfilelink` say it "can't be used from web applications. Referrer is restricted to pcloud.com", and a link it gives is tied to the IP that asked for it (so a server can't ask on a page's behalf). Sending works from a page; reading back doesn't. So pCloud is possible only where requests aren't a web page's: the desktop app (v2), from Rust. The resumable upload's `upload_info` answered HTTP 400 (an HTML page); optional.

Only part of the 700 came in, and no playlists were made (folder playlists are only made when the import finishes, so an import that dies makes none). The library is intact on Drive. Chrome's storage for 37player.com was only 31 MB that afternoon, so Chrome may have discarded the site's storage as damaged when the disk filled (to confirm in DevTools → Application → IndexedDB). Wanted (kr37): the dialog offers **Open another library** when there's more than one (today only the console can: `sessionStorage.setItem('37player.library', id)` + reload). Also: when the library that didn't load was shared, offer **Get it back from the shared library** (start empty and join again, Use the shared library). That needs the join details, which lived in the lost database: from automatic restore's cookie, or keep them (not the code) in the device's library list. Confirmed in DevTools: both of 37player.com's databases were there but empty (Chrome had discarded them). **Automatic restore** doesn't help here today: `offerAutoRestore` only runs on what looks like a first start, and the library list in localStorage was intact. The not-loaded dialog should also offer the cookie's entries (**Bring it back**) when there are any.

- **v2.0: Tauri 2 for storage** (under discussion since 4 Nov 2026, nothing built). Aim: on computers, the library lives as ordinary files in one folder, not in IndexedDB, so there's one copy of each recording, not three (the originals, IndexedDB, the backup folder). The JavaScript keeps every decision (merge engine, sync, playback, import); Rust only stores files safely, behind a storage layer shaped like today's `idb*` functions. kr37's decisions so far: **Windows and Mac first**; Linux needn't be first-class (WebKitGTK's media playback is the weakest of the webviews). **No continuous backups** in v2: one stable, readable library, plus the native .37p export as on Firefox and Safari today. Planned order: the storage layer in the browser first (no change in behaviour, full suite passing); a files backend; a Tauri spike on Windows (HP, Surface) and the SKBC Mac mini, audio only; then Rust behind the same layer. Readable artist/album file names are part of the move itself (kr37, 5 Nov 2026: integral, ideally the same week), built into the files store; the three import modes (Copy, Use in place, Move) come after. kr37's answers on the files backend (5 Nov 2026, `desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md`): default folder `Music/37 Player Libraries/`; libraries move out of the webview's storage by themselves (the webview's copy kept 30 days); Rust reaches only roots the person chose (library folders and folders used in place); every call names its root. Use in place (kr37, 5 Nov 2026): its files are never touched; edits live only in the library's records, explained at the start of the import (to get them into files: export, or turn the folder into Copy/Move). The mode belongs to each import, not the library; anything 37 Player makes (split pieces, conversions) and anything not imported as a folder in place (dropped files) goes in the library's own folder; removable drives default to Copy, and their tracks show as not connected while the drive is out. iPad stays on the web version plus a shared library for now. **Tauri spike on Windows** (4–5 Nov 2026, the HP and the Surface, `tauri-spike` branch, desktop/SPIKE-RESULTS.md): go with conditions. WebView2 played everything like Chrome, minimised, covered, asleep and with media keys too; `showDirectoryPicker` exists there (so folder libraries work in the Windows app as they are). Conditions: closing asks (done, 1.4.156: the shell holds the close back and sends `p37-close-requested`; `closingWouldStop`: playing, sending, a copy; then `p37_quit`), one instance, the converter bundled (`desktop/copy-app.mjs`), Drive says it isn't available in the desktop app yet (`IN_DESKTOP_APP`, `driveNotInDesktop`), the app's address chosen once (`useHttpsScheme`). **Version numbers in the desktop app** (1.4.160, kr37's choice A, 5 Nov 2026): the page keeps its own version (`APP_VERSION`), on the website or in the app; the desktop shell (`desktop/src-tauri`, `version` in tauri.conf.json and Cargo.toml) takes the page's version as of the shell's own last change, like sync.php's `SYNC_VERSION`, and that's the installers' version (MSI allows only three parts, at most 255.255.65535; a four-part 1.4.xxx.yyy was considered and dropped). About shows "Running in the desktop app (version …)". The shell also reports a **bridge number** (`p37_shell_info`: what its Rust side can do for the page; 1 = closing asks first, 2 will be a library's files, …); a page needing more (`DESKTOP_BRIDGE_NEEDED`) says to download the newer desktop app (`desktopShellCheck`). Raise the shell's version whenever desktop/src-tauri changes, and the bridge number whenever the page starts relying on something new there. **Why the desktop app, where Chrome or Edge would do (kr37, 6 Nov 2026):** on a computer with Chrome, its installed app with a folder library already has most of what v2 is for. What the desktop app adds that counts: folders without permission prompts (they can lapse in Chrome after a restart), fewer ways for volunteers to go wrong (wrong browser, private window, cleared profile), Macs without Chrome (Safari can't keep folders; old Macs can't install current Chrome), and, still to build, updates that arrive by themselves. pCloud: "not worth much", desktop-only if ever. Proposed for updates (not decided): the shell downloads newer `37-player.html` from 37player.com and uses it from the next start, keeping the bundled page as fallback, using only a page whose bridge it has, and only one kr37 has signed; so GitHub builds a new desktop app only when the Rust shell changes. **Apple (kr37, 6 Nov 2026):** stay out of the App Store (the sandbox, review of every update, and its rules would likely forbid the page updating itself; an iPad App Store app would be a separate, probably rejected, web wrapper). Join the Developer Program ($99/yr) for Developer ID signing and notarization once the remaining Mac tests and friends' beta tests go well. A signed, notarized app keeps installing and running after the membership lapses (Developer ID certificates last 5 years); renewing is needed only to sign a new shell, which page updates should make rare. **Groundwork for bridge 2, built 6 Nov 2026:** the shell's `desktop/src-tauri/src/files.rs` (shell 1.4.175): roots (a library's folder or one used in place; only the person adds one, through the folder dialog or the default `Music/37 Player Libraries/<name>`, kept in the app's data folder as `roots.json`), commands `fs_list`, `fs_stat`, `fs_read` (a range), `fs_write` (raw body, root and path in headers; written aside and swapped in), `fs_copy`, `fs_rename`, `fs_remove`, `fs_mkdir`, every path checked against its root (no `..`, nothing absolute, no `\`, `:` or NUL, no way out through a link), and `p37lib://localhost/<root>/<path>` serving a recording with Range; `cargo test` covers them (12 tests, run in CI before each build). The page's `DesktopDirHandle` / `DesktopFileHandle` (1.4.175) are folder and file handles on those commands, so `FolderLibraryStore` runs over them unchanged (readnames.js runs its whole scenario a second time through them, on a stand-in for the shell). Not used yet: BRIDGE stays 1 until the page relies on them. Still to do: reading big recordings by address instead of whole (the stand-in blob, after the Mac experiment), then switching the desktop app's libraries to folders (the automatic move) and raising the bridge to 2. **The files backend (bridge 2)**, design in `desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md` (for kr37's review, 5 Nov 2026): the desktop app gives FolderLibraryStore handles of its own, backed by five Rust commands confined to the library's folder; recordings are played and read by address (a custom scheme with Range) instead of as in-memory blobs; first an experiment on the Mac on whether WebKit keeps a fetched big blob on disk. On the Mac the app's address is `tauri://localhost` (not http), so the starter track and converter were fetched from 37player.com, which refuses that origin; `servedBesideApp` (1.4.157) takes them from beside the app there too. The Mac's test build comes from `.github/workflows/desktop-mac.yml` (a pre-release). kr37 keeps his ChatGPT conversations on it as a .docx (reviewed by Claude on 4 Nov 2026: right direction; prove the webviews' audio before any storage work; Google Drive and pCloud sign-in need a desktop flow, since their redirects come back to 37player.com).

They don't sync and needn't be up to date (a library catches up when it's next opened). Not built; why the desktop files backend names a root in every call (`desktop/FILES-DESIGN.md`).
- **WebKit stored .zip imports wrongly (found 5 Nov 2026, fixed 1.4.173).** On SKBC's Mac mini, in Safari 17.6 and in the desktop app, no WMA from an album .zip (downloaded from pCloud by choosing the folder) would convert: "no audio found … Invalid data found when processing input". A .zip import keeps each track as a slice of the .zip (`readZipEntriesFromFile`), and WebKit stored the slice as the start of the whole .zip: the first bytes, as both the page and the converter read them (1.4.171 puts them in the error), were `504b0304`, "PK". The one track that converted was converted before it was read back from storage. Tried first and dropped: a retry with an in-memory copy (1.4.169; the page read the same wrong bytes). **The fix:** on WebKit, `IdbLibraryStore.put` copies a recording or artwork into bytes of its own before storing it (`webkitOwnBytes`, in 32 MB pieces): everything up to 256 MB, and anything known to be a .zip slice (`webkitSlices`) up to 1.5 GB; a bigger File is taken to be one picked as it is, which WebKit stores fine. Library cleanup's check calls a recording that starts "PK\x03\x04" damaged (`looksLikeZipStart`); it can't be repaired, only imported again. Tests: zipwebkit.js (window.__p37WebKit), convbytes.js. kr37's affected album: delete and import again.
- **Planned (talked through 31 Oct 2026, not built): joining, sharing by link, housekeeping.** Replaces the older "pull-only cloud library" / "Borrow from another library by join code" ideas.
  - **Joining, two fixes** (found by kr37 joining his Drive library "kr37" from an empty library):
    - **The starter goes up at once.** An "empty" library still holds the starter prayer (Archive → Acoustic). `joinSharedLibrary` counts it as nothing (`libraryIsJustStarter`), so asks nothing, but keeps it; with Only what I choose everything on the device goes up, so the starter is sent before the chooser even opens. Fix: joining from a library that's only the starter removes the starter first, whatever is chosen. And the chooser's Apply needs a change, so on the first visit after joining give it a plain way out ("Nothing for now").
    - **Receive updates only comes too late.** It's only a switch in Settings, and joining always starts with it off (`receiveOnly: false` in `joinSharedLibrary`), so local tracks go up in the first sync before anyone can reach it. Fix: the join step asks two things, set before the first sync: *What comes here:* Everything / Only what I choose; *Does this device send changes:* Yes / No, mirror only. Mirror with "Use the shared library" replaces whatever is local, so a mirror never puts anything in front of anyone.
    - **Rename "Receive updates only" to Mirror** (kr37). It's `rsync -av --delete` every few minutes: each sync takes the shared library's document as it is (`merged = remote.doc`); anything only on this device goes, with its audio, and a local edit is quietly put back at the next sync. The setting should say so: "This device becomes a copy of the shared library. Anything here that isn't in it, or is changed here, is replaced." Modes then: **Mirror**, **Everything (two-way)**, **Only what I choose (two-way)**, and **Mirror only what I choose** (partial + receive-only, refused today by `partialNoReceiveOnly`; allow it).
  - **Sharing by link** ("partial share to others"; kr37: *precise* offering is the priority; sharing a whole library is sometimes valid but not the aim).
    - **Supplier:** select, then Import/Export → **Share by link** → Export's "what" list (`exportChoices`) for the final selection → a **name** (the playlist's or folder's, editable; what the taker and the Shares screen see) → how: **Link to my library** (`.37share`: no copy of the audio) / **Full copy** (`.37p`) / **For other players** (`.zip`). The .37p and .zip say their size and that they take Drive space, and are allowed (with a big Workspace account the convenience can win). → **expiry**: 30 days (default) / 1 year / No expiry → **Copy link**, and **Share…** on phones (WhatsApp).
    - **Static, not living** (kr37: no maintenance after every sync). A share is a snapshot of the files as they were; a newer version means sharing again. Rejected: a living link rewritten after each sync by whichever device saw the change.
    - **Who can share:** any device that sends changes, Everything or Only what I choose (all its tracks are in the shared library once sent). Not Mirror devices. Tracks still waiting to go up can't be in a `.37share` yet ("2 tracks are still being sent").
    - **The link:** a 37 Player address, `37player.com/37-player.html#borrow=<id>`, so tapping it opens Borrow; a .zip's page offers Download. **Trap:** on an iPad or phone a link tapped in WhatsApp opens a Safari tab, not the Home Screen app, and the tab's library is a different, empty one. So a page opened by a link with an empty library says "Open 37 Player where your library is, and paste this there", with Copy. Pasting into Borrow always works.
    - **Taker:** Import/Export → **Borrow from a library** → paste. Read without signing in to Google (see Drive below). The chooser, everything ticked; borrowed playlists go into a **Borrowed** folder. Track IDs kept: tracks already here aren't fetched; a playlist with the same ID is brought up to date (kr37: fine, as Copy to another library does). Audio comes in pieces and carries on after an interruption (as `sharedReceive`). A .37p is read the same way, its table of contents first by Range, then only the entries needed, so for the taker a .37p and a .37share feel alike. Built on Copy to another library's machinery with a remote source; nothing is ever written to the supplier's side, and no temporary library or join/leave is involved.
    - **Google Drive:** the share files live in an **Offerings** folder in the library's Drive folder; that folder *is* the record of shares (no change to the library document or the merge engine). Each `.37share` holds its name, expiry and the Drive files it uses (audio, artwork, waveforms). Each of those files gets "anyone with the link" reader permission, per file, in Drive batch requests (up to 100 per request). **Not the whole folder:** anyone who can see a link-shared folder can list it with the API key, and the share must name the folder, so it would open every audio file in the library. Anyone can read link-shared files with just the app's **API key**, so takers need no Google account. **Turning a share off** deletes its `.37share` (a taker then has nothing to pull) and removes the link from files no remaining share lists (shares can overlap). Drive can't put an expiry on "anyone" permissions, so expiry is done by housekeeping (below). "Anyone with the link" sends no notifications; a Workspace admin would only see the shares in Drive's audit or sharing reports (kr37: no Kadampa Center is thought to restrict link sharing).
    - **The droplet:** easier and tighter. sync.php keeps each share as a record of exactly which files it covers, and serves a taker only those, only while current (expiry checked per request), with no per-file permissions; its daily cron `cleanup` deletes expired shares. A library not shared anywhere can still make a .37p or .zip after a one-time Google sign-in (Drive's Offerings folder).
    - **Cleanup must respect shares:** `sync.php cleanup`, Drive's Free up space and the Drive tidy (below) keep files a current share lists, else a later audio replacement (old file unused) would break the share after 30 days. A track the supplier deletes may stay reachable meanwhile (kr37: don't care).
    - **Shares screen** (Settings → Shared library): each share's name, item count, expiry, **Copy link**, **Turn off**. On Drive it's a listing of the Offerings folder.
    - **Setup and a test first:** enable the Picker API again in the Cloud project (removed after the 29 Oct test) and make an **API key** restricted to the Picker and Drive APIs and to `37player.com/*` and `test.37player.com/*` (it's in the page, so the restrictions are the protection); the project number is the Picker's App ID. Then a small test page: list a link-shared folder's files and download them with only the key, from 37player.com; check whether 37 Player's `appProperties` on its folders can be read that way (else go by folder names). Google may block a much-downloaded link-shared file ("too many users have downloaded"): fine for a few Centers. The Picker (to pick a .37p someone shared with your account) stays possible, but needs third-party cookies (blank in Firefox until tracking protection was off): the pasted link comes first.
    - Considered and dropped: **Borrow by signing in to the supplier's library** (the supplier's Google login on the taker's device; on the droplet a join code that can also write. A read-only join code for sync.php would be small if ever wanted); **sharing the library folder with another Google account** (`drive.file` sees only what that account picked, and a picked folder grants none of its contents, per the 29–30 Oct test); **sharing a whole library by link** (possible, but not the aim).
  - **Housekeeping: one home for periodic work** (kr37: no "subsequent tasks for random things" scattered through the code). `housekeeping()`, called at the end of each sync round (`runSharedSync`, where `sharedSendLogs` is called now), never in the first two minutes after opening, one window at a time (a lock), waiting while anything plays, in short bursts (a couple of minutes per round, carrying on next round). A small table of tasks, each with how often and a "last done" time:

    | Task | How often | Who | "Last done" kept |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Send this device's logs (`sharedSendLogs`, moved in) | 30 minutes | each device | this device |
    | Note this device as a backup keeper (moved in from the sync) | 12 hours | devices with automatic backups | this device |
    | Tidy the Drive library, expired shares included | 24 hours | one device that sends changes (Everything or Only what I choose; not Mirror) | **on Drive**, a tag on the library folder ("tidied at …, by Gompa iPad") |

    The Drive tidy: the device reads the tag after a sync round; over 24 hours old (hours, not the calendar day: devices may be in different time zones), it sets it to now and starts. Two starting together does no harm. It keeps what `sync.php cleanup` keeps: every version from the last 30 days and at least the latest 300; audio, artwork and waveforms no kept version uses are deleted once 30 days old; files a current share lists are kept. So it never touches recent History. **Free up space** stays for the deliberate cases (room now, Current version only). Droplet libraries: skipped, the server's cron does it. The hourly purge of Undo's deleted audio (`undoTrashPurge`) stays on its own timer: it's about this device, not the shared library.
    - **Other libraries on the device:** the tidy also goes through this device's *other* Drive libraries (two-way ones only) and tidies any not tidied for 24 hours by the same tag, so a test library nobody opens still gets tidied by whichever of its devices runs 37 Player at all. Possible because tidying needs only that library's Drive login and its files on Drive, both in its own database (`openDbNamed`, as Copy to another library). Takes that library's sync lock first and skips it if busy; a login that has run out is skipped quietly (a line in the sync log, never a sign-in prompt). Limit: unused audio still waits its 30 days.
    - **Drive space overview, by Google account** (Settings → Library → All libraries; shown only when the device has more than one Drive library): each Drive library's space, with **Free up space** and **Delete from Drive**, also for libraries that aren't open. "Google Drive is full" points there ("Your 37 Player libraries use 9.1 GB of it"). Deleting the last device's copy of a Drive library offers "Also delete it from Google Drive (frees 4.2 GB)": today its folder stays.
  - **Order, when built:** the joining fixes and Mirror (small) → housekeeping (moving the two existing tasks in, then the Drive tidy) → the API key and the test page → sharing by link on Drive, then on the droplet → the space overview.
- **"Checking…" with no way out** (1.4.145; kr37's iPad, 31 Oct 2026, 1.4.143, installed app, partial sync, while 224 files were coming in). What's on this device (and Review changes) showed "Checking…" over the whole screen with no Close until its check finished, and the check never finished (no error, so the existing catch never ran; the state request probably queued behind the big downloads, or IndexedDB was busy with them). Settings and Help still opened, but nothing closed the page, so Playing, Edit Playlists and Library were out of reach until the app was reloaded. Now Close is there from the start (`rvStartLoading`), the check gives up after 90 s (`rvWithin`, `RV_LOAD_LIMIT_MS`) with the usual "couldn't reach" text, a failure in drawing the page is caught too, the reason goes into the sync log ("What's on this device couldn't open: …"), and an answer arriving after Close doesn't reopen it. Test: choose.js (a held state request). Not yet known: why the check hung; the next sync log after a timeout will say.
- **Full test run** (done 24 Oct 2026, in the cloud session, with `make-fixtures.sh`'s recordings): everything passes except `ipad.js`, which failed at its first tap the same way on `main` (fixed 29 Oct 2026: the first-run welcome's "No, just start" was left open over the screen). Tests with non-ASCII file names need a UTF-8 locale (`LANG=C.UTF-8`). With the converter in `tests/www/ffmpeg` (made by `make-fixtures.sh`), the default format setting converts imported WAVs: a test that needs a WAV kept sets `when: 'untouched'` (split.js, conv6.js, damaged.js).
- **Not checked on a real device:** the iPad with 1.4.052–1.4.059 (reload keeping the place, the theme before the first paint). Android was checked. The phone top bar was laid out at 412px; on a 360px phone the ? may move to the second row (still two rows).
- **Undecoded audio in an incognito import (22 Oct):** many "Unable to decode audio data" lines, probably .m4a files or memory pressure. If such files come in marked "can't play" in a normal window, look at them.
- **First-time guidance:** on-screen instructions for new users (Edit Playlists especially: which column is for what). Column B's "Edit playlist here" bar (1.4.056) is the start; quieter labels for columns C and D are a possible next step.

- **File formats and converting** (talked through 20 Oct 2026; keeping unplayable files done in 1.3.048, converting them in 1.3.049, above):
  - **Principle:** keep everything, and let a capable device fix it. A file only needs converting once, anywhere: the result syncs to every device. Works the same with the droplet, Google Drive or no sync at all.
  - **Converter:** ffmpeg.wasm (the community build of ffmpeg for browsers), hosted on 37player.com, fetched by every device in the background and kept locally, so it works offline (phones and iPads turned out to be as capable as computers: see the measurements below). Decodes what browsers can't (ALAC, WMA, compressed AIFF), encodes MP3 and AAC on any browser including Linux Chrome and Firefox, faster than real time, reading big files in place (WORKERFS). Still to do: our own audio-only LGPL build, smaller than the standard 31 MB one.
  - **Measured 20 Oct 2026** with tests/convert/ (ffmpeg.wasm 0.12.10, standard build): MP3 192k at 40–46× real time on the T470 (Firefox), 75–79× on the iPad (Safari 26), 58–62× on an Android phone (Firefox); a 2-hour recording in 1½–2½ minutes. AAC 2–4× slower than MP3 (a 2-hour mono talk took 9½ minutes on the T470). Converter memory 32–55 MB whatever the file's length (it reads the input in place); the finished file is held until stored. So phones and iPads are capable converting devices too: no need to keep the converter to computers. Formats add no gap (0 ms on every device).
  - **Gapless playback is the player, not the formats:** 37 Player gives its one `<audio>` element the next file when a track ends, so there's always a short break between tracks, whatever the format; scheduling the next file sample-accurately (Web Audio) was seamless on every device. Not wanted for now: a month of use never ran into it, since sessions have silences and stops between items.
  - **Done in 1.3.053–1.3.054** (see Non-obvious decisions). What follows is the original plan, kept for its reasoning.
  - **Standardize becomes optional, off by default.** It began for fear of the browser's storage filling up (not borne out) and for Music.app's gapless playback (mismatched rates/channels click between tracks); the latter becomes an **export option** ("Export for Music.app": one rate and channel count in the exported copy), downplayed. Presets: Keep as is (default) / Standard, most universal (MP3 192k, like Tharpa) / Lossless (FLAC) / Very small / Custom (format, bitrate, sample rate, channels: keep by default, thresholds such as "only above N kbps"). A **preferred format** (default MP3 192k) is what files that can't be played are converted to.
  - **Format settings belong to the shared library,** not the device: one device's choice replaces files everywhere. The library's settings win; the join preview says what that means ("40 of this device's files will be converted, 3.1 GB → 0.6 GB"). Every settings change says its effect before applying ("This will convert 42 tracks…").
  - **Older iPads and Macs must keep working:** no Opus in shared libraries (old Safari can't play it). The 336 "wrong channel count" (mono) and 15 "wrong sample rate" files on the T470 aren't problems; they stop being counted as such.
  - **Conversion runs whenever nothing is playing** (not only off the Playing tab), in a worker; playback stops the current file, to be redone.
  - **Sending big files:** a size threshold; over it, ask "Send as is / Convert first / Not now".
  - **Library cleanup becomes one page laid out like Help and Settings:** Library info (stats) → Can't play → Duplicates → Missing artwork and tags (the "571 missing…" selector moves here from the Library page; "Find duplicates" stays on the Library page too) → Storage → Format.
  - **Server conversion** (sync.php with ffmpeg): held back as a fallback; kr37 isn't willing to host many centers' or friends' libraries.

- **Planned: first run and protecting libraries** (kr37, 28 Oct 2026). **Built in 1.4.092** (automatic restore in 1.4.093); the theme tour wasn't built: see Non-obvious decisions. People trying 37 Player were put off by technical questions at installation, while they were still deciding whether they liked it. Principle: ask nothing until it matters. Before the first import there's nothing to lose; the first import is the moment for every protection question. Order of work: (1) the first run cut down, the glowing buttons, the Heads-up at first import, the red-button rules, the prompt on leaving the editing screens removed; (2) the daily prompt, the sync-not-finishing check and the short tour; (3) automatic restore (server work, the most testing); .37p backups alongside (1).
  - **First run:** choose the language, then one question: "Join a shared library?" Yes or no. No: an empty library with the starter playlist (Archive → Acoustic), and nothing about libraries in the browser, IndexedDB or backups. Then ▶ and Import/Export glow until each is clicked once.
  - **A short theme tour** (optional): a drawn pointer that goes to the theme button and shows dark → light → bigger → back, about 5 s, with Skip. Not kr37's full version (every theme, sizes, Chinese and back): too long for someone just looking, and the language change could alarm. Or simply let the theme button glow too.
  - **The Heads-up, at the first import** (paused while they decide). At most three steps, only those that apply, each with its own button, plus Import anyway:
    - iPhone/iPad Safari: add to Home Screen first, then import there (installing later leaves two half-libraries: the installed app has storage of its own); then a shared library or backups.
    - Mac Safari: switch to Chrome or Edge, or add to the Dock first; then backups or a shared library.
    - Firefox: no automatic backups (no folder access), so Chrome or Edge, or a shared library.
    - Chrome or Edge: Install (one click there; the installed app shares the tab's library, so no two-library problem), then automatic backups or a shared library.
    - Android Chrome: Install, then a shared library (no folder backups on Android).
    - Declining Install gets one second dialog, "Are you sure? It's easy", with a third choice, **I'm only using it for a quick job** (kr37's case: a Center that keeps its own playback setup, someone who only wants the splitter). That choice stops the install and backup reminders on this device until the library grows past a few tracks or they open Settings.
  - **A, Safari's 7-day erasure** (Safari on iPhone, iPad and Mac; not apps added to the Home Screen or Dock): raised only at the first import, in one sentence: "If you don't open 37 Player for 7 days, Safari erases everything in it." kr37 may make a short video showing a library being restored after it; subtitles in the nine languages as WebVTT files beside one video, following the app's language (from a transcript of the narration; timing needs one watch-through).
  - **B, two libraries** (installed late on iPhone/iPad or Safari's Dock, or moving browsers): prevented by the Heads-up's order (install first). To combine two, join both to the same shared library and choose Combine; worth a help page.
  - **C, erased at any time** (disk pressure, a cleanup tool, a mistake): "protected" means everything on this device is on the server (synced; "Only what I choose" counts, since everything on the device goes up, but not while changes or audio are still waiting to be sent), or automatic backups on this device. Protected: no prompts. Keep showing which devices keep a backup. Leaving a shared library without automatic backups: warn. **Sync not finishing:** record when sending last finished; if changes or audio have waited more than about 3 days, the button turns red and, at most once a day, a dialog says sync doesn't seem to be finishing: fix it, or set up backups on this device.
  - **D, backups out of date** (only for a library that isn't synced): the backup button turns red as soon as there are changes since the last backup. No timed yellow and red. No prompt on leaving Edit Playlists or Library for Playing (prompts that come too often get dismissed unread). Instead, the first time on a calendar day someone opens Edit Playlists or Library, if there are changes not backed up and the last backup is a while ago, one prompt: back up now, with the ways to stop needing it (a shared library, automatic backups, a browser that can do them).
  - **E, noticing a lost library and bringing it back** ("Set up automatic restore", opt-in in Settings). The service worker can't help: a browser clears its storage together with the library. A cookie set by the server and invisible to scripts (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict) survives Safari's 7-day erasure (which removes only what scripts wrote) and Chrome's clearing under disk pressure; only a person clearing browsing data removes it. 37player.com (sync.php, or a small restore.php) puts the details into the cookie scrambled with a key only the server has, so nothing is stored on the server, and the cookie is useless to anyone else. One cookie lists every library on the device: a droplet library (which, and this device's login: restored in one tap); Google Drive (which library: restored after signing in with Google again); a library that isn't shared (where its backups were: the folder's name only, since browsers never give a page the full path, the date of the last backup and which browser made it). After a wipe the app asks the server and offers to bring each back; for backups, "Your last backup was on 26 October. Search your computer for '37 Player backup', or open the folder 'Backups – 37 Player'", then the folder picker. Matching by IP address was considered and dropped: a Center shares one address, homes change. Works only when the app is opened from 37player.com (or a server with the same script), not from a file.
  - **Backups as .37p** (instead of .zip; still a zip inside, renaming shows its contents): easy to search for; not unpacked by Safari on a Mac ("open safe files after downloading") or by tapping it in Files on an iPhone/iPad, which turns a .zip into loose files that won't restore; an installed 37 Player on Chrome or Edge can claim the extension, so double-clicking a backup offers to restore it; can't be mistaken for the export for other players, which stays .zip. Old .zip backups needn't be read (kr37: libraries change too fast for them to be worth restoring). The restore picker takes only .37p on computers; on iPhones and iPads iOS may grey out an extension it doesn't know, so there it takes any file and checks it ("That isn't a 37 Player backup. Look for a file ending in .37p"). Try the .37p-only picker once on an iPad: if iOS allows it, use it there too. Suggest a distinctive folder name when automatic backups are set up.
- **To consider, the service worker's copies** (kr37, 25 Oct 2026; his own tests, stopping Apache and truncating the file, all opened the saved copy without anyone noticing):
  - **Promote a version to "good" only after real use**, not just "up". With several releases in a row, a bug in one that was hardly used would go unnoticed, and it would become the copy to fall back on. An older good copy is fine. Not difficult: the page sends "up" as now, which ends the watchdog, and a second message, "proven", after some minutes of use (say 10 minutes open with a track played, counted across loads, kept with the fresh copy's hash in the worker's state). Only "proven" moves `?37copy=new` to `?37copy=good`. The fallback copy can then be a few versions old.
  - **Say what's happening while it waits.** Two waits exist. (1) Up to 8 s for a server that doesn't answer: the browser shows only its loading spinner, and the worker can't show anything because the page isn't there yet. The alternative is to open the saved copy at once and fetch the new one in the background, so an update shows on the next load; then there's no wait at all. (2) Up to a minute with a new version that doesn't start, a blank or half-drawn page. The watchdog script at the top could show a small notice after about 10 s without "up": "37 Player is taking longer than usual to start. If it doesn't, the last version that worked here will open."

Needs `lame` installed; skipped otherwise. (`ipad.js` fixed 29 Oct 2026: it didn't know the first-run welcome's "No, just start", added in 1.4.092, so its first tap landed on that dialog; all its touch checks pass.)
  - **Optional, held back (30 Oct 2026):** a length (Xing) header in split MP3 parts for other players' seeking. Held back because a Xing header without a LAME tag may or may not make a browser skip 529 samples at the start (`mp3PlaybackOffset` assumes it does), which would shift the splitter's own cuts in a part split again by about 12 ms; worth doing only with a check of what Chrome and Safari actually do. (Splitting M4A without re-encoding: done in 1.4.086.)
- **Next conversation (from 23 Oct 2026):**
  - **Feedback and logs** (decided 23–24 Oct 2026; Copy all and Share… done in 1.4.077):
    - **Send to kr37:** a form in the app posting to a small `feedback.php` beside sync.php, which mails rinzin@kr37.net through the droplet's own mail (Postfix or Exim: check which; Dovecot only reads). Subject `37-Player: <first line>`, logs attached. Works on every browser (the share sheet can't send files from Linux Chrome). It only ever mails kr37: no "copy to me" at a typed address, which would let anyone use the droplet to mail strangers. Size cap, a rate limit per address, a copy kept in a folder on the droplet. An optional "your email, so I can reply". The device keeps what was sent under **Sent messages**, as the sender's record. GitHub issues need an account, so not for users.
    - **Logs in the shared library:** each device sends a trimmed copy of its logs with its files (a small addition to sync.php; a `logs/` folder on Drive), readable on any device of that library. No privacy question within the library: device and track names are already shared.
    - **The form lists what goes,** each with a checkbox: this device's logs (ticked), and the other devices' logs from the shared library, named with their age, none chosen to begin with, with **Select all** / **Select none** above them, outlined (`btn outline`, 2.6.006: as `btn ghost` they read as plain text on the dialog) (2.6.000; kr37, report 2026-11-20-153553: about 20 listed and the recent ones chosen, which he took off one by one every time; they used to be chosen when from the last three days). Nothing is sent without being shown.
  - **Big WAVs are too expensive and should be shrunk** (kr37's view): handled. New imports are converted before they're sent (`reduce`, the default), and older ones are easy to find and fix (Check library, Library → Table sorted by size → Convert). kr37 converted all of his (29 Oct 2026).
  - **Hosting other Centers:** sync.php's files in DigitalOcean Spaces, or in a dedicated Drive/pCloud account through rclone, with join codes still keeping libraries apart; per-library quotas. Then **pCloud** as a third choice where a shared library is kept.

  - **To check:** the not-loaded guard and the storage watchdog on a full iPad (does Safari still report persistence while hiding storage?); re-adding the iPad's "37 TEST" from test.37player.com. (The full suite: all 74 passed on 29 Oct 2026, conv5 included. The droplet backup to the T470: running, without restore.key.)
- **Google Drive full, part (2) and the start of (3)** (1.4.140, found on kr37's Drive, 30 Oct 2026). Google answers 403 "The user's Drive storage quota has been exceeded." (reason `storageQuotaExceeded`), which `isQuotaError` took for this device being full: Settings said "This device is full" on the T470, and the iPad's partial-sync Apply (which had to send the starter track) said "Couldn't reach the shared library". Now `driveError` marks it `driveFull`; `sharedFailText` says "The Google Drive of <account> is full…"; the sync log says "(Google Drive is full)"; sending waits half an hour between tries (`sharedDriveFullAt`, cleared by Sync now) instead of starting all 136 files every 3 minutes; and Settings → Shared library shows "Google Drive: X of Y used (N%)" (`fillDriveSpace`, `about.storageQuota`), in red with a Free up space link from 90%. Seen meanwhile: the iPad's small state save (version 153) still went through on the full Drive while the T470's failed, so Google's refusal isn't all-or-nothing. **The tidy-up (part 1) for Drive: 1.4.141** (kr37 chose building it over cleaning by hand with `scripts/shared_unused.py`, which found 10 GB of today's experiments unused by the newest version and nothing under a 30-day rule: a library made today). Settings → Shared library → **Free up space…** (`openDriveTidy`, `driveTidyPlan`; not on receive-only devices): keep what History needs for 1, 7 (default) or 30 days, or **Current version only** (1.4.146, kr37: "start fresh right now"; 1.4.142–145 had "1 hour", since a library made today had nothing older than a day, so even "1 day" freed nothing); deletes for good (DELETE, not Drive's trash) versions made before then (the newest always kept) and audio/artwork/waveform files that no kept version names and that were made before then. **Why no grace period is needed** (kr37, 31 Oct 2026; 1.4.142–145 kept the last hour's files on the wrong idea that a device might be sending files for a version it hadn't saved yet): a device saves the version first and records it as agreed only once the save went through, and the background upload (`sharedUploadFiles`) sends only files named in that agreed version; the newest version is always kept, and later versions still name a file unless its track was deleted or its audio replaced. So a file still on its way is never deleted, and files sent after the plan looks aren't in its list. What Current version only gives up is History (and Restore from it). One edge, the same at any choice: a track revived by a merge (an edit wins over a deletion) whose audio was already deleted comes back only if a device still holding the file sends it again (uploads check what's on Drive and send what's missing). Says what it frees first; deletes four at a time, logs its start and end, and asks before the window is closed meanwhile (1.4.144: one at a time took half an hour for 2203 files, and closing the window cut it short unnoticed); runs under the sync lock; a device missing a file sends it again. Still to do: keeping small changes flowing when big files can't. Tests: drive.js (the fake Google's `full`, `ageAll`).
- **A shared library's storage filling up** (kr37, 30 Oct 2026: a Center's sadhana library may need about half of a free account's 15 GB, so it will happen). Today on Google Drive nothing is ever freed: every state version is kept, replaced audio revisions (re-tags, conversions, Replace audio file) stay, and deleted tracks' audio stays; the droplet has `sync.php cleanup`, Drive has no server. When Drive is full every upload fails, the small state save included, so even playlist edits stop syncing, and the device shows only a generic "Drive 403". The 15 GB is shared with the account's Gmail and Photos (another reason for an account just for this). Plan, in three parts: (1) **keep it from filling:** a tidy-up done by a device, mirroring `sync.php cleanup` (versions from the last 30 days and at least the latest 300; audio no kept version uses, once 30 days old), one device at a time, as with backup keepers; Drive deletes made by the app skip Trash, so the space comes back at once; (2) **see it coming:** Drive's `about.storageQuota` (readable with `drive.file`) in Settings → Shared library, "Google Drive: 7.4 of 15 GB used", and a top-bar warning near 90%; (3) **when full:** say "Google Drive is full" naming the account, a button to free space with the tidy-up, and small changes kept safely on the device until there's room. The droplet needs the same once it hosts several Centers (per-library quota, a warning before a library outgrows its share).

Playing a session during a big sync led to the long-recording fixes (1.3.030–1.3.032); worth one more run.

Tests pick with `window.__radio(label)`.
