- **The starter track** (1.4.029; since 1.4.032 `libraries/starter.mp3` is in the repository and fetched from the site's own `libraries/`, same-site, so every site including the test site has it with no CORS; 37player.com's from a downloaded copy; the rest of `libraries/` stays out of git): a new library starts with Archive → Acoustic, its one track fetched from the libraries address as `starter.mp3` (the track's own title tag names it; the file is named after it). `TheLiberatingPrayer.mp3`, the first starter, is fetched if `starter.mp3` isn't there and still recognised by `libraryIsJustStarter` (`STARTER_TRACK_FILES`). Everything else that was in `libraries/` (the old server libraries) is no longer used. tests/fixtures/starter.mp3 is the track ("Spontaneous Accomplishment of Wishes - Acoustic", SLC, 1:48, mono 96k).
- **New library → Google Drive** (1.4.027): the New library dialog's "Join a shared library" also offers Google Drive…: it checks Drive is set up, makes and opens the new library, which (`runPendingLibraryJoin`, `join.drive`) signs in to Google and shows the chooser, with the device name and partial-sync choice carried over; the new library takes the joined library's name.
- **Its default depends on the device** (1.4.026): never set, the switch is "Only when not playing" on phones and tablets (`setupEnvironment().mobile`) and "Any time" on computers. A device that has set it keeps its choice (it's a device setting).
- **One switch for work during playback** (1.4.025): File format's switch (`standardizeOnlyWhenIdle`: "Only when not playing" / "Any time") now also decides whether a shared library's files are sent and received, and waveforms made, during playback (`sharedTransferHold`, `sharedWaitWhilePlaying`). Settings, Help and Review sit above the bottom bar while it shows (`--opbar-h`, set from `#globalOperationBar`).
- **Converting many files** (1.4.024): converting 40 WAVs in Brave, the 39th failed with "memory access out of bounds" (ffmpeg.wasm's memory, one converter for the whole run). Now the converter is started afresh every 8 files (`CONVERTER_FRESH_EVERY`), and a file it crashes on is tried once more with a fresh one. Library cleanup no longer redraws the whole page as each file finishes (that restarted the missing-audio check each time): only Convert to the preferred format updates, and says "Converting now: N of M done" while a run is under way. The keep-originals and failure messages say "1 file" / "39 files", not "file(s)".
- **Opening audio files with 37 Player** (1.4.023). On the website the manifest is now a real file, `manifest.webmanifest` (same id, `./37-player.html`, so installs carry on; a downloaded copy keeps the inline one), with `file_handlers` ("Open with" for audio types, Chromium on computers; `launchQueue`, `launch_handler: focus-existing`) and `share_target` (Android Share: a POST to `37-player.html?share-target` that `sw.js` takes, keeps in the cache `37player-share` and redirects to `?shared=N`; `takeSharedFiles` reads and empties it). `openWithFiles` asks Play it now (`openFilePlayer`: a modal `<audio>`, main playback paused, library untouched) / Import (`bulkImportFolder`, through `requestAppMode("library")` for the PIN) / Play and import (`playAfterImport`, played from `recordLastImport`). Files opened before the app is ready, or over the welcome, wait. Not on iOS/iPadOS, Safari or Firefox. Test: tests/openwith.js (hands the files in where launchQueue and the share POST would).
- **Opening audio files with the desktop app** (page 2.6.000 with shell 2.6.0; kr37's MacBook, report 2026-11-20-153553: Finder → Open with → 37 Player opened the app, and no question came). The question above was only ever wired to the browser's `launchQueue`; the desktop shell listed no file types and passed nothing on. Now: **listed** for audio on the Mac only (`desktop/src-tauri/tauri.macos.conf.json`: `public.audio` and the usual extensions, role Viewer, rank **Alternate**, so it's offered under Open with but never becomes the default by itself); Windows and Linux installers register nothing yet (Windows' installers could take over the .mp3 default; not wanted unasked). **Handed over** by macOS as an Apple Event, `RunEvent::Opened { urls }`, at the start and while running (never on the command line); on Windows and Linux as command-line paths at the start (`files_in_args`) and through the single-instance plugin's hand-over when 37 Player already runs (relative paths taken from the second launch's folder). **Read where it lies:** each file gets a root of its own for this run only, `open-<n>` (`files::Roots::open_file`), never saved in roots.json or listed, through which only that file can be read (`get_read`: fs_stat, fs_read, p37lib://, Linux's http server, and the source side of fs_copy and fs_append_range); `get`, which writing, listing and removing use, doesn't know it. The shell keeps them until the page asks (`p37_opened_take`) and says `p37-files-opened` when more arrive (the window is brought forward); files that came before the roots were loaded wait in `Opened.early`. The page (shell feature `opened`): `desktopOpenedFilesStart` takes any the app was opened with and listens for more; `desktopTakeOpenedFiles` makes each one with `DesktopFileHandle(root, name).getFile()` (a DiskBlob when big) and passes it to `takeOpenedFiles`, so the same question, waiting for the welcome or an open dialog as before. `openFilePlayer` gets each file's address through `audioObjectUrl` when it's first played (a DiskBlob can't be an object URL). Import copies it into the library like any import. Not yet: .37p backups double-clicked in the desktop app (needs an exported type for .37p on the Mac). Tests: the shell's `an_opened_file_is_its_own_root_for_reading_only` and `files_on_the_command_line`; tests/openwith.js through the fake shell.
- **The starter prayer isn't the person's library** (1.4.022): `libraryIsJustStarter()` (at most the one track, `STARTER_TRACK_FILE` or `settings.starterTrackId`, in at most one playlist in at most one folder, nothing else). Such a library gets no "Back up library", no "backup older than your library" warning on restore (moving to 37player.com in Brave hit it), no "join in a new library?" question, and joining a shared library takes the shared one without asking. Kept the prayer: something playable at once.
- **Damaged files found by their pattern** (1.4.018): `looksDoubled` recognises Safari's pattern from the first bytes (WAV: a second RIFF header at byte 12; MP3: an ID3 tag followed by another), so a copy that reached another device through a shared library (its size agreeing with its data) is found and repaired there too, if it arrived whole.
- **Damaged files; converting moved to Library cleanup** (1.4.017). Library cleanup → Damaged files: "Check stored files" streams every stored file (`blobReadableBytes`) and lists those whose readable length isn't their size (`damagedAudio`); **Repair** takes the old file out of Safari's pattern (`salvageOldFile`: WAV = RIFF header + old file at byte 12; MP3 = new ID3 tag + old file), stores it and writes the current tags again (new revision, so a shared library gets it). Export leaves a damaged file out and names it (`zipSkipped`; portable export checks on WebKit before writing tags around it); sending to a shared library skips one on WebKit and logs it; converting skips them. File format's sentence is shorter (no format: it's just above) with "Review in Library cleanup"; Library cleanup → Convert to the preferred format has the whole sentence, Convert them now, the list, and "Change the preferred format". Tests: tests/damaged.js, conv5.
- **Safari and re-tagged files** (1.4.016). Writing tags builds the new file from slices of the stored one (the audio is never loaded). Safari stored each slice as the whole old file: on the SKBC Mac mini, WAVs given a new artist and album became RIFF header + old file + old file + new LIST chunk (size field 5,997,738; 11,995,562 bytes readable), stopped playing, and failed export ("reports … but only … could be read"). Those were then sent to Google Drive as new revisions. Now, on WebKit (`IS_WEBKIT`), `putRewrittenAudio` reads the new file into memory as one piece before storing it, reads it back, and puts the old file back if it's wrong. Not reproducible here (no WebKit in the test machine); tests/webkitstore.js checks the path with `__p37WebKit`. Still to do: export skips a damaged file and lists it; Library cleanup finds damaged stored files; nothing damaged is sent to a shared library. Since 1.4.173 the same family of fault is handled for every recording and artwork stored on WebKit (`webkitOwnBytes`; see "WebKit stored .zip imports wrongly").
- **Sending three at a time** (1.4.013): `sharedUploadFiles` sends audio, artwork and waveforms three at once (`SHARED_UPLOADS_AT_ONCE`). One at a time, a first upload to Google Drive managed about 11 files a minute from the SKBC Mac mini (694 tracks, 4.5 GB, plus artwork): Drive is slow to make each new file. Library info never shows storage use below the audio's own size: Safari's `storage.estimate()` reported 146 MB for that 4.5 GB library.
- **An empty shared library** (1.4.012): one with no tracks and no playlists (`sharedDocEmpty`), such as a Drive library just made to be filled, is joined like a new one: everything here goes up, no "Use the shared library / Combine" (using it would have emptied the device). Restoring a whole backup on a device joined to one skips "This library is shared… leave, then restore": the restore runs as usual between leaving and joining again (`opts.beforeRestore` / `afterRestore` in `offerNativeImportNow`; `sharedKeepForRejoin`, `rejoinWith`). Checked against the shared library as it is now (`sharedGetState`); if that can't be reached, it asks as before. Tested in tests/emptyshared.js.
- **The privacy page** (`privacy.html`, 37player.com/privacy.html; written at 1.4.010 for Google's OAuth review, brought up to date in 2.5.029, 16 Nov 2026, kr37: he noticed, signing up for Apple's signing, that the page said 37 Player sets no cookies). Now covers: the one cookie, Automatic restore's `p37restore` (opt-in, sealed, HttpOnly, Path restore.php, 400 days, nothing on the server; as far as we can tell, a cookie set only when the person turns that feature on, and used only for it, needs no consent banner); GitHub (desktop downloads, the home page's release lookup, the desktop app's daily page check); a shared library's 30 days of versions and the devices' logs (90 days); Send feedback (what goes, emailed, kept on the droplet, filed in the private reports repo, and that a report may be read with an AI assistant's help, Claude); the desktop app. The 30 and 90 days on 37player.com hold only as long as `php sync.php cleanup` is run. **Keep it true:** a change to what 37 Player sends anywhere, or keeps, updates this page in the same commit.
- **No personal name in public-facing text** (kr37, 16 Nov 2026: 37 Player is becoming more public, and Google search knows it). The privacy page no longer says who makes it (contact: Send feedback, or GitHub issues); the page source's first line, Help 125's example device ("Office laptop") and the desktop app's `authors` (Cargo.toml, which a Linux package shows as its maintainer) no longer name him. **The repository too (2.5.030, same day):** code comments, NOTES.md, CLAUDE.md, the other notes, the workflows' comments and test names say **kr37** where they said his name, and commits are made as `kr37 <kr37@users.noreply.github.com>`. Write "kr37" from now on. He isn't concerned about older commits or what search engines have already seen, so history is left as it is. Kept as they are, because they're working names: the mail address `rinzin@kr37.net`, his login `rinzin` on the droplet, and his home folder in `scripts/pull_droplet_backup.sh`. Not changeable: the Mac app's signature, made with his individual Apple Developer ID (only an organization account shows another name).
- **The move to 37player.com** (1.4.010). The site moved from `player.37dakinis.net` (test: `playertest.37dakinis.net`) to `37player.com` (test: `test.37player.com`; `www.` redirects to the bare name in Apache). The droplet serves the same folder under the old names too (`ServerAlias`, one certificate `37player.com` covering all five names), so older devices keep working. IndexedDB is per address, so a device's library stays at the address it used: at an old address (`OLD_HOSTS`, `MOVED_TO`) the top bar shows "37 Player has moved", which opens a notice saying how to bring the open library across (since 1.4.021 nothing pops up by itself: an urgent-looking dialog invites mistakes; the last step is tailored: Home Screen, Android app, Dock, installed app, or bookmark) (a shared library: join again with its code, or Google Drive; otherwise export and import, or restore from its backup folder; Home Screen apps re-added). A browser that never used the old address (no `37player.libraries`/`37player.device` in localStorage, no `temple-sound-library*` database) is sent straight to the new one, keeping path and query. Saved addresses (`sharedLibrary.url`/`tokenUrl`, `sharedLastUrl`, `updateUrl`, `librariesUrl`) are rewritten on load (`moveOldAddresses`), so the old names can be retired later: then also take them off the `kr37.net-0001` certificate, whose renewal would otherwise fail. `token.php`'s `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` lists the old origins too during the move. Tested in tests/moved.js.
- **Shared libraries on Google Drive** (1.4.009; setup in GOOGLE-DRIVE-SETUP.md, not done yet). `driveFetch` answers sync.php's requests (state, save, list, file, peaks) from a Drive folder, returning Responses just as sync.php would, so everything above `sharedFetch` is the same for both (`cfg.kind === "gdrive"`, `cfg.folderId`, `cfg.refreshToken`; `cfg.code` is "gdrive <folderId>" so code paths that need one still work). Folder "37 Player – Name" with `appProperties` {p37: "library", nm, st, au, ar, pk} naming its four subfolders; `state/000123.json.gz` (gzipped with CompressionStream), files named as on the droplet. Save "only if still at N": add N+1, list that number, the earliest (createdTime, then id) wins; the loser deletes its own and answers 409. Uploads up to 5 MB in one request, bigger ones resumable in 16 MB pieces, resuming from where Drive says it got to. Stand-in waveforms found by size (3601 bytes) then checked. Sign-in is a redirect (popups fail in iPad Home Screen apps): app → `token.php?op=start` → Google (`access_type=offline`, `prompt=consent`: a refresh token every time; Google's own popup client can't ask for that) → `token.php` (code → tokens) → app `#p37auth=…`, taken out of the address at once and matched with a nonce in sessionStorage. Refresh through `token.php` (POST {refresh_token}); access tokens in memory only. 401 → refreshed once; a refused refresh → `driveNeedsSignIn`: the top bar says "Sign in to Google". One Google account per library for now; several accounts would need Google's Picker (drive.file only sees what the app made or was opened with it). Tested against a fake Google (tests/fake-google.js) with the real token.php under `php -S` (tests/drive.js). **Which account** (1.4.124): a library remembers the Google account it's in (`cfg.account`, from `driveAccountEmail`: Drive's `about`, which `drive.file` may ask; libraries joined earlier learn it once per start, `driveNoteAccount`). Signing in again passes it to `token.php?op=start&hint=` → Google's `login_hint`, so it's offered first; if another account comes back anyway, nothing is kept and "A different Google account" names both (`driveSameAccount`; unknown on either side counts as the same). The Drive chooser says "Signed in as …", Settings "Stored in the Google Drive of …", and automatic restore carries the account as the hint. The join dialog's Drive step recommends one account made for the purpose (`driveOneAccountTip`).
- **Other storage for shared libraries** (talked through 20 Oct 2026). Each library chooses its storage; the merge engine stays as it is and only a small adapter changes per service.
  - **The save without a conditional write:** never update the library file, add the next one (`state/000124.json`, as the droplet already stores versions). Then list that number: if there are two, the older wins; the loser deletes its own, merges and retries, as after a 409. Works on any service, including Drive v3, which offers no ETag to make a write conditional.
  - **Google Drive:** the narrow `drive.file` permission (no security review). A browser-only app gets only an hourly access token, and renewing it silently is unreliable on Safari, so the iPad would ask to sign in again. Decided: a small `token.php` on the droplet holds the OAuth client secret and trades sign-ins for refresh tokens (kept on the device, never on the droplet; it stores nothing, logs no bodies; one Google Cloud project for everyone, published out of Testing, or refresh tokens expire after 7 days). Library data goes straight between the device and Drive. Sharing one library across several Google accounts needs each to pick the folder once in Google's picker: test on a nonprofit shared drive before promising it; one center account on every device avoids it.
  - **pCloud** (kr37 and friends use it): has OAuth and a browser SDK, and `diff` for cheap change checks; tokens appear not to expire. Old reports of CORS errors and IP-bound download links: an evening's test page first (sign in, upload, download from 37player.com). If browser downloads fail, it offers nothing over the droplet. **Test page written** (`pcloud-test.html`, 28 Oct 2026; checked against a stand-in for pCloud here, not yet against pCloud itself): signs in with pCloud's token flow (`response_type=token`; the answer names the API host, api.pcloud.com or eapi.pcloud.com for Europe, which every later call must use), makes "37 Player test", uploads a small file and 30 MB, saves the same version from two "devices" at once (the plan for "only if still at version N": `uploadfile` with `renameifexists`, and whoever's file comes back renamed, "000002 (2).json", lost: deletes it and answers 409), a resumable upload in pieces (`upload_create`/`upload_write`/`upload_save`, POST first since PUT needs a CORS preflight), lists, fetches both back through `getfilelink` (CORS), a Range request, `diff`, and deletes what it made; Copy the report. Needs a pCloud app (docs.pcloud.com/my_apps) with this page's address as its redirect URI. If it's all ticks, a `pcloudFetch` adapter like `driveFetch` (answering sync.php's state, save, list, file, peaks) joins `SHARED_WHERE_KINDS`.
  - **Dropbox** gives browser apps refresh tokens (PKCE), so no server; 2 GB free. **OneDrive and SharePoint: dropped** (kr37, 29 Oct 2026; Microsoft's publisher verification needs a verified business partner account, and Center tenants would need admin consent anyway).
  - **Order:** libraries on one device (done, 1.4.001) → Google Drive (done, 1.4.009; live and in use since 1.4.010) → copying between libraries (keep track IDs, so copying again or back finds the copy already there; Borrow is now planned as sharing by link, 31 Oct 2026) → pCloud test page → one Drive library across several Google accounts (Google's Picker).
  - **Google Drive with several accounts** (talked through 29 Oct 2026). Today's way, one account on every device, is the **Simple Google Drive setup** (kr37's name): for a Center, an account made for it (`sound@…`). A token is bound to the account chosen at sign-in, not to the browser's default Google account, so devices don't drift to someone's personal account; only a re-sign-in could, which 1.4.124 guards. With each person's own account: (1) `drive.file` grants are per account, so B can't see A's folder through Drive sharing alone: B picks it once in Google's Picker; (2) files B uploads into a folder in A's My Drive are owned by B and use B's storage, while in a **shared drive** they belong to the drive (Workspace for Nonprofits' pooled storage) and leaving people are simply removed from it. So the plan is shared drives + the Picker. What a picked folder grants (its contents? folders others made inside it?) is the undocumented part: **`gdrive-picker-test.html`** (29 Oct 2026; removed after the test, with tests/pickertest.js; in git history if ever wanted) signs in through token.php, opens the Picker (Picker API key + project number as App ID), and reports whether the folder, files put there by hand, and another account's run are seen, read and written, whether access lasts past a new access token, and whose storage new files use. Run it with two accounts on a shared drive before building. **Result (30 Oct 2026, Firefox, two personal accounts, a My Drive folder shared as Editor): it doesn't work.** Picking a folder gives access to that folder only: the app can create things in it, but sees none of the four files put there by hand, nor the folder and note.txt another account made through 37 Player itself (`drive.file` grants are per user, even for the app's own files). A shared library's files arrive all the time, so picking each one is no answer. Also, the Picker needs Google's third-party cookies: in Firefox it stayed blank until tracking protection was turned off for the site (Safari is likely similar). So with `drive.file`, a Drive library is one account on every device (the Simple Google Drive setup). Several accounts would need the full `drive` scope, which is restricted (a paid yearly security assessment): not worth it. People wanting their own accounts can use the droplet (join codes) or, later, pCloud/Dropbox.
  - **Other providers' approvals** (looked up 29 Oct 2026, while pCloud's approval is pending; OneDrive and SharePoint dropped, above):
    - **Dropbox:** register any time (minutes), App folder access, name it "37 Player" from the start (it can't change after production). Development mode allows up to 500 accounts, but once 50 link there are two weeks to get production status, which isn't reviewed before 50. Free.
    - **AWS S3:** no app approval. A Center would make a bucket, CORS for 37player.com, and credentials limited to it: only for Centers with someone technical. Conditional writes make "only if still at version N" clean. Later, if asked.
